# Handover: the Neper browser (conformance, the app, its targets) — updated 2026-10-31

The one browser handover. For another session or account continuing the work. Goal in force (user, repeated by a Stop hook every turn): **keep implementing the browser until it is fully implemented**, ending in **Neper-native applications**, tested on **Windows, Linux and the web (wasm32-wasi)**. Android and iOS do not compile yet. HTTP/3, WebSockets and WebGL are out of scope. Nothing here is "done"; this is a pause.

Sections 0-8 cover the CSS conformance stream, the app and its targets; section 9 covers the script-side WPT stream (DOM and web APIs, the JavaScript prelude in `lib/e/web/dom_js.e`).


**Session close (2026-11-05, 256 rounds).** All gates re-verified on the committed tree at `3249e328b`: vendored corpus **189 of 211**, `css/css-grid/alignment` **94 of 151**, `css/css-content` **6 of 14**, and `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/gpu_present` **green** (`gpu present ok`, exit 0). The browser app is verified on **Windows** (live window) and **wasm32-wasi** (exit 0); the Linux binary emits at 22 MB and still needs a host with WSL to run. The two landings of the closing stretch are the app itself - a worktree bisect to `74991611f` and the shared-queue fix `44dc9f93b`, which also turned `gpu_present` from exit 76 to green - and the grid-baseline answer: the row-baseline pass exists only in `layout_lanes_rows`, so normal grid placement has none, which is the 47-file block's root cause and its one-step fix. **The single next action is to add that per-row pass to `layout_grid_inner`** and compare against the reference's 16/20/24 px.

> **Round 256 (2026-11-06) supersedes the paragraph above.** The block-axis row pass *is* present in `layout_grid_inner` (offset `dy` at 8719, group `grp` at 8594) and is **correct** - it produces exactly the reference's 16/0/24/24 and puts all four baselines of `grid-self-baseline-horiz-002` at 56.4. Two candidate "fixes" to it were built and both left the suite at 94/151 with byte-identical failure sets, so both were reverted and the tree is unchanged. **The 47 baseline files split into two different defects:** the 32 `self-baseline` ones need the **inline axis** (`justify-items: baseline` has no implementation at all - `dx` comes from `grid_axis_offset` only, and `first_baseline` is block-axis only), which needs a new per-writing-mode inline-baseline primitive; `grid-baseline-align-001`'s test grid is already correct and fails because *the reference page's* `position: relative`/`absolute` pair renders wrong in our engine. Read round 256's entry before touching this again. No code landed for this item this session.

- **Round 257 (2026-11-06): LANDED - an orthogonal item no longer drags the row baseline. `css/css-grid/alignment` 94 -> 96 of 151.** The defect round 256 missed: `grp` (the per-row maximum) took `first_baseline` from **every** baseline-aligned item, including items whose writing mode is orthogonal to the grid container. For those, `first_baseline` measures in the item's *own* frame, so the value is not a distance down that box and it lifted the parallel items of the row (a `vertical-lr` item with a 40px font read `bup=24` where its height was 50, so it joined the group near the top and raised everything else). Fix, 7 lines at 8587-8597: an orthogonal item contributes `ib.h` (its bottom margin edge) instead of `bby` - CSS-align-3's baseline fallback.
  - **The second half of the fix matters as much:** the orthogonality test must compare the item's mode with **the grid container's** (`mode_code(is0) != mode_code(cs)`), not call `is_orthogonal(l, id)`. That helper reads `l.vmode`, which at this point in the layout is not the container's frame: it reported **every** item of a `vertical-lr` grid as orthogonal, which made the first version of this fix *swap* two files (gaining `horiz-002`/`-003`, losing `vertical-lr-001`/`vertical-rl-001`) for a net zero. With the container comparison the same change is +2 and no regressions.
  - **Gates, on the clean build `run-wpt398`:** `css/css-grid/alignment` **96/151** (the two gained are `grid-self-baseline-horiz-002` and `-003`; the failure diff against 94 is exactly those two lines and nothing else), corpus **190/212** (189 held + the new fixture), `css/css-content` **6/14**, and `css/css-flexbox` **635/908** (the flex run finished after this entry was first written; it is unchanged, so no suite dropped).
  - **Regression fixture:** `tests/browser/wpt/reftests/grid-baseline-orthogonal.html` + `-ref.html`, registered in the corpus manifest. It is a **verified** regression test: `run-wpt387` (pre-change) **fails** it and `run-wpt398` (post-change) **passes** it. Both pages are geometry-only (`color: transparent`, explicit `box-sizing: border-box` sizes) so the pair compares rectangles, not glyphs - hand-tuning a text reference cost six iterations before that, because a rotated glyph never matches a horizontal one.
  - **What is still open:** the 30 remaining `self-baseline` files and `grid-baseline-align-001`. **Round 258 measured the inline-axis plan and refuted it - do not start there.** See the round 258 entry below.

- **Round 258 (2026-11-06): the inline-axis pass was built, worked, and flipped NOTHING - it is stashed, and the 30-file block is a layout-shape problem, not a baseline-offset one.** Reading round 257's "the 30 files need the inline axis" as a missing `dx` shift, I measured first (`grid-self-baseline-horiz-005` is `grid-auto-flow: row` + `itemsBaseline`, i.e. a real `justify-items: baseline` column). Findings:
  - **`justify-items: baseline` really is a complete no-op**: two grids differing only in `justify-items: baseline` vs `justify-items: start` rendered **byte-identically** (item boxes at x=2-65 and x=2-25 in both). So the premise was right that nothing is implemented.
  - **The pass was then implemented and it works**: `inline_baseline` (next to `first_baseline`, horizontal text returns `border-left + padding-left`, vertical text returns not-found and falls back) plus a per-column group `cgp` mirroring `grp`, applied as `dx` at the same place `dy` is. A two-item probe now moves the second item from x=2 to x=42, aligned to the first item's text start. **`css/css-grid/alignment` stayed at 96/151 and the failure set was byte-identical to before.**
  - **Why it flipped nothing - measured, not guessed**: these tests compare a **grid** page against a **float/inline-block** reference, and the two arrange the items in different shapes. `grid-self-baseline-horiz-005`: our grey container is 438x350 with the 4 items stacked in 4 columns; the reference's is 595x447 with the items flowing horizontally. The same mismatch holds for `vertical-lr-002` (218465 vs 201665 grey pixels), `horiz-006` (99944 vs 324623) and `grid-self-baseline-001` (153765 vs 123762). No inline offset can close a different arrangement, so the 30 files are blocked on **how our grid places and sizes these blocks**, not on where a baseline sits.
  - **Reverted, not committed**: a change that removes a documented no-op but flips no file is an unvalidated behaviour change across every grid in the corpus. The patch is in **`git stash@{0}`** (a single `layout.e`, +56/-1) for whoever picks this up with a different theory. Gates with it applied were clean (corpus 190/212, css-content 6/14, flexbox 635/908), so it is safe to re-apply as a starting point - it is just not proven to gain anything.
  - **The next honest step on this block** is to explain the shape disagreement: read one reference page (they are plain `inline-block` in a `float: left` block, no grid at all) and decide whether our grid's shrink-to-fit width for a `grid-auto-flow: row` container with auto columns is what differs. Only after the two pages arrange items the same way does a baseline axis become the deciding factor.

- **Round 259 (2026-11-06): the shape question is answered - our grid is right and the reference page is a different layout. This block cannot be won by changing grid baseline code.** Reading the pair for `grid-self-baseline-horiz-005` side by side:
  - **The two `.block` rules differ in writing mode.** The **test's** `.block` is horizontal (`background: grey; float: left; margin: 5px; height: 350px`, no `writing-mode`) and its grid is `grid-auto-flow: row`, so its four items form **one auto column of four rows**. The **reference's** `.block` is the same rule **plus `verticalLR`** (or `verticalRL`), and its items are `display: inline-block`, so the same four items flow down the vertical inline axis and the block's *width* becomes its 350px height.
  - **Measured geometry, which makes this unambiguous:** our four blocks are **102px wide each**, all starting at x=5/117/229/341 in one row from y=53 to 402 (the `height: 350px` is honoured exactly). The reference's blocks are **296px and 345px wide**, and its first block's items occupy only y 63-144 - they sit at the top of the 350px-tall vertical block and spread 290px across. A different container shape, not a misplaced baseline.
  - **Our behaviour is correct for what the document asks for.** A `float: left` grid with `grid-auto-flow: row` and auto columns sizes its column to its widest item: a probe with and without a 30px left padding gives block widths **28px and 58px**, exactly the content, and `justify-items: baseline` still lines the item text up inside it. The test's own `<meta name=assert>` agrees with this reading: "no item shares any row-like Baseline Context and all items share 'first-column' Baseline Context" - one column of four rows, which is what we produce.
  - **What this means for the remaining 30 files.** The test/reference pair for this family is satisfied in a real browser because the reference is a *deliberately different but visually equivalent* rendering: a vertical block whose items happen to land in the same place as a baseline-aligned grid column. Reproducing that picture from our side needs the **reference's own vertical-block layout** to be right (the reference page is `verticalLR` + `inline-block` children), not more grid baseline code. So the block splits again: some of the 30 may be reachable by fixing **vertical writing-mode `inline-block` layout in a float**, which is a general layout area with a much wider blast radius, and none of them are reachable from `layout_grid_inner`.
  - **Nothing changed this round**: probes removed, tree clean, `git stash@{0}` (the inline `dx` pass from round 258) still holds. The recommendation stands that this family be dropped from the grid-alignment target and, if pursued, tracked as a vertical-writing-mode layout item instead.

- **Round 260 (2026-11-06): `grid-baseline-align-001` bisected halfway - the grid is right, the `.ref` block is not.** Measured rather than inferred this time, and the round-256 note "the reference's `position: relative`/`absolute` pair renders wrong" is now precise:
  - **The grid container is CORRECT.** The silver border boxes of block 2 are **identical in both renders**: bbox (10,68)-(201,169) on the test page and on the reference page. The baseline placement inside the first pair matches too (orange fills at x 13-82, y 81-156 in both).
  - **The difference is one block and one axis.** Black runs in block 2: **ours `13-92`, `116-135`, `139-198` (three runs); the reference `13-92`, `119-198` (two)**. Our run starts 3px left (116 vs 119) and is split in two, so the `.ref` box's right-hand absolutely-positioned child is drawn from the wrong origin. 3px is exactly `border: solid silver` with no width - `medium` = 3px - so the static position of an absolute child inside a bordered box is off by that border.
  - **Four probes did NOT reproduce it**, which is why this is a partial result: `position: absolute` with `left: 0`/`right: 0` in a plain `relative` box (correct: x 1-24 and x 57-80); the same in an `inline-block` container (correct); and the over-constrained case `right:0; top; bottom:0; width; height` (correct, x 67-80). So the defect needs the full `.ref` context - a `position: relative` box that is itself a grid item (`.grid self ref` are the same element) with a 3px border - and the next instrument belongs at the **static position** computation (`abs_origin` 9845 sums x/y to the root; the `.ref` placement path is at 9916-9947) rather than in more standalone probes.
  - **`abs_origin` is the leading suspect, not a proven cause:** it walks the parent chain to the root and `layout.e` then subtracts `pad_x`/`pad_y`, with a grid-area branch at 9918 and a root special case at 9910. Nothing here was changed: the fix needs one measurement of `static_x` for that child, and shipping a guess into the absolute-positioning path would touch every suite. **Half-diagnosed, no code change.**

- **Round 261 (2026-11-06): the static position is measured - `-3px` is the parent's border, and both attempted fixes are refuted.** One print at the static-position computation (`layout_abs`, 9948-9951) for `grid-baseline-align-001`, and the two halves of the document differ in a way that names the variable:
  - **Same parent geometry, different child offset.** The test's own `.ref` blocks (`ppos=41`, `81`) print `px=116 py=68 padx=119 pady=71 **bx=0 by=0** sx=-3 sy=-3`; the standalone reference page's blocks (`ppos=14`, `33`) print `px=116 py=68 padx=119 pady=71 **bx=3 by=3** sx=0 sy=0`. The parent's border is 3px (`pbord_l=3`, no padding) and the formula is `sx = px + bx - pad_x = 116 + bx - 119`, so **`-3` is precisely a missing `bx`**: the child's own flow offset is 0 where it should be the 3px the reference page supplies.
  - **Why `bx` is 0 in one and 3 in the other:** in the failing block the parent is `pinner=1 pinl=1` - a **grid container that is itself inline-level** (`display: inline-grid`, from `.grid { display: inline-grid }`). The correction that gives a child of a container its content-edge offset is guarded by **`!pb.inline_level`** at 9954 (now 9965 after the round's edits were reverted), so it never runs for an inline-level grid and `bx` stays 0.
  - **Attempt 1 refuted - relaxing that guard.** Allowing `pb.inner == .Grid` through regardless of `inline_level` changed nothing: still 96/151, `sx` still -3 (the branch adds `border+padding` on top of a `pad_x` that already contains the border, so it cannot help).
  - **Attempt 2 refuted, and loudly - `static_x = pad_x + bb.x`.** Reading `pad_x` as the parent's padding box made the suite **collapse from 96 to 41 of 151**, so `pad_x` is not that in general (it is adjusted by the grid-area branch at 9922 and the root case at 9915). Reverted; `run-wpt404` re-measured **96/151**, confirming the tree is back.
  - **Two attempts on this path, both refuted - stopped there**, as the round-258 rule requires. The remaining question is narrow and worth one more session: which of the two `bx` values a real browser produces for an absolute child of an **inline-level grid** whose own box is the containing block, and where in `layout_abs` (9888-9955) the inline-level grid's content edge should enter. Nothing changed; `layout.e` is byte-identical to the committed tree.

- **Round 262 (2026-11-06): `grid-baseline-align-001` is CLOSED as not-worth-it. The grid-alignment figure for this stream is 96 of 151.**
  - **The spec question was asked and could not be resolved in one pass.** CSS Position 3 §2.1 does answer the general case, and answers it usefully: the containing block of an absolute box is formed by the **padding edge** of the nearest ancestor that establishes one, *unless* that ancestor is an **inline box**, and the grid spec's §9.1 is named as the exception that can substitute a grid area. That rules our parent - an `inline-grid` generates an inline-*level* block container, not an inline box - into the **padding-edge** case. But the specific rule the fix needs, **Grid §9.2 "With a Grid Container as Parent" (the static position of an absolutely positioned child)**, could not be retrieved: `drafts.csswg.org/css-grid-1/#static-position` serves the document from the top and truncates before §9, and the two raw `Overview.bs` copies fetched (w3c and a mirror) both truncate at the same ~107 KB bound. Four fetches, no §9.2 text. The stop rule was "cannot be resolved from the spec in one pass", so it fires here.
  - **What the last two rounds did establish, and what a future attempt should start from:** the `-3px` is a missing `bx`, not a wrong `pad_x`; the parent is an `inline-grid`; `static_x = px + bx - pad_x` with `px` the border-box origin and `pad_x = px + border` subtracts the border twice only for the inline-level-grid case, but the obvious repair (`static_x = pad_x + bb.x`) collapses the suite to 41/151 because `pad_x` also absorbs the grid-area and root adjustments at 9915-9922. **Rule out first, next time: an inline-level grid must take the same path as a block-level one** - the guard at 9965 (`!pb.inline_level`) is the one asymmetry that survives all measurements, and relaxing it alone was inert only because the parent's `bx` was already 0 for a different reason.
  - **Banked: `css/css-grid/alignment` 96 of 151**, corpus **190 of 212**, `css/css-content` **6/14**, `css/css-flexbox` **635/908**, all last measured on `run-wpt404`/`run-wpt398` (rounds 257 and 261). Nothing in the tree changed across rounds 258-262: every attempt was reverted and each revert re-measured. `git stash@{0}` still holds the round-258 inline `dx` pass, which is real work (it moves `justify-items: baseline` items correctly) but flips no file, so it stays stashed rather than shipped.
  - **The recommendation for the next session is unchanged and now final for this block:** take `dom/events` single-activation-behavior (41 subtests, known-shape fix, no shared layout path). The grid baseline family has cost four sessions and gained two files; the remaining 45 baseline failures are blocked on vertical writing-mode `inline-block` layout (30 files) and this one positioning detail (1 file), neither of which is worth the blast radius.

## Where the objective stands (2026-11-06, end of a long session)

Measured, on the committed tree, with the numbers repeated where they matter:

- **CSS conformance: 9905 of 13377 (74%)** — `css-text` 776/1154, `css-writing-modes` 634/995, `css-flexbox` 635/908, `css-grid` 752/1545, `css-multicol` 186/402, `css-overflow` 318/501, `css-sizing` 330/457, `css-tables` 82/137, `css-content` 6/14, `css-values` 80/156, CSS2 5192/5829. The three biggest named gaps, each with a written plan and a baseline in the notes below: **`white-space: break-spaces`** (110 files, `white-space` 210/320), **`letter-spacing`** (26 files, 3/29) and **`content: url()` / element replacement** (9 files in `css-content`). `break-spaces` and `letter-spacing` share one root (a piece per affected character in `collect_inline`'s `cut_here`, next to `bspace` at layout.e:1916) and together are worth ~136 files; three branch-level attempts at them are recorded as refuted.
- **Script-side DOM/web APIs: whole `dom/` 11235 of 11664 subtests (96.3%)**, `dom/nodes` 9508/9642 (98.6%), `dom/events` **676 of 819 (82.5%)**, measured 2026-11-06 after the activation work. The remaining large item is the **cross-realm model** (8 files, 5 of 40 subtests): the cascade and constructors exist, and what is missing is a *creation-realm field on `html.Node`*, which is days of work rather than a round.
- **test262 REMEASURED 2026-11-07: 40,458 of 40,938 (98.83%), 480 failing, 107 skipped** — replacing the 2026-10-18 "96.2% (43619 of 45356 run)" figure, which was 20 days and several interpreter commits stale. Split, both halves run in full on the same day against `D:/wptdata/test262`: **`language/` 17,805 of 17,811 (99.97%)** and **`built-ins/` 22,653 of 23,127 (97.95%)**. Runner: `neper-self.exe emit-executable tests/browser/js/t262.e` then `t262-<n>.exe D:/wptdata/test262 <list>`. The lists are `D:/wptdata/t262_list.txt` (41,045 paths) filtered by `^test/language/` and `^test/built-ins/`. Note the old 45,356-test denominator is also wrong: the current list is 41,045, so a pass-rate quoted against it is not comparable with older runs.

- **DOM failure triage (2026-11-07, round 2 of the conformance goal).** The 353 non-range failures by folder: **`dom/nodes` 233, `dom/events` 85, `dom/traversal` 17**, `dom/xpath-evaluation-detached-nodes.html` 15, then three singletons. Inside `dom/nodes` the largest files are `ParentNode-querySelector-All` **48**, `processing-instruction-attributes` **30**, `namespaced-attribute-mutations` **12**.
  - **`processing-instruction-attributes` (30) is one unimplemented WICG proposal, not a standards bug.** The test links `github.com/WICG/declarative-partial-updates` (not the DOM or HTML standard) and asserts that `innerHTML = '<?t a="b"?>'` in an HTML document yields a **nodeType 7** node with attributes parsed out of its data - real HTML parsing makes that a bogus comment (nodeType 8), which is what we produce. Verified locally: HTML path gives `8`, `createProcessingInstruction` gives `7` with a working `setAttribute`, and `parseFromString('<?t ...?>','application/xml')` gives a `parsererror` whose root is element 1 because **a document containing only a PI is not well-formed XML**. So the 30 failures are "follow the proposal" or "lose them"; they are not a bug to fix blind.
  - **`find_attr` ignoring the attribute namespace was investigated and reverted.** `dom.e`'s `find_attr` matches on the local name alone, so a namespaced attribute could answer a plain `getAttribute`, which looks like the cause of `namespaced-attribute-mutations`' 12 failures. Adding a `namespace == .Html` guard (an attribute in no namespace is stored with the `.Html` marker, see `set_attribute`) **changed nothing**: the same 12 failures before and after, and `Element-classlist` (1415/1420) and `ParentNode-querySelector-All` (1927/1975) were byte-identical too. Reverted rather than ship an inert change. The real cause of those 12 is elsewhere - most involve `style` and SVG event-handler attributes, i.e. the CSS/event layer reading `attributes[]` directly rather than through `get_attribute`.
  - **`ParentNode-querySelector-All` (48)** fails on distinct selector features, each worth a look: `:link`/`:visited` inside `#head` (we match 2, expected 0), `:target`, `:root` not matching the document element, namespace selectors (`|*`, `|div`), `::slotted`, and unclosed-bracket handling (`[align="center"`). That file is the single best target in `dom/nodes`.
- **Round 35 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the subgrid width rule located in code, not yet fixed.**
  - **The declarations are correct**: `display: inline grid-lanes; grid-template-columns: 100px` on the parent and `display: grid; grid-template-columns: subgrid; width: 50px` on the child both reach the layout layer intact, so this is not a parsing problem.
  - **Where it works vs where it breaks**: `subgrid_prepare` (`layout.e:7763`) copies the parent's track sizes into `l.sub_c`, and `grid_build` turns them into tracks via `subgrid_tracks` (`layout.e:7969`) - so the subgrid's *tracks* are right. What is wrong is its **border box**: `width: 50px` is honoured, leaving 50px of the parent's 100px track exposed.
  - **The row axis already does this correctly and is the model to copy**: at `layout.e:8717` the parent's item placement detects `grid-template-rows: subgrid` and **zeroes the item's `rmin`/`rmax`** so the item has "no height of its own to give" and its rows stand in for it. There is **no equivalent for the column axis** - nothing zeroes `cmin`/`cmax` or otherwise prevents the subgrid's own `width` from setting its border box. That asymmetry is the bug.
  - Next step is small and specific: give the column axis the same treatment the row axis has, then measure `css/css-grid/grid-lanes/subgrid` (134 tests behind this shape).
  - **Note the shared tree**: `lib/e/fmt/css/layout.e` currently carries another session's uncommitted `inline_baseline` work (57 lines). Read the file fresh before editing and stage only your own hunks.
  - No source change shipped.
- **Round 34 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the grid-lanes note is STALE; the real cluster is subgrid.**
  - **`css/css-grid` measures 755 of 1545** (790 failures), essentially unchanged from the 752 recorded in round 5. **`css/css-grid/grid-lanes` is 294 of 858** - so the old claim that "793 failures are grid-lanes, the masonry proposal, so the score needs that feature" is **wrong now**: grid-lanes is substantially implemented (`layout_lanes`, `layout_lanes_dense`, `layout_lanes_rows`, `lanes_mirror`, `lanes_dense` packing all exist in `layout.e`), and what fails is mostly not the feature being absent.
  - **The largest single cluster is `grid-lanes/subgrid/grid-subgridded` at 134**, then `track-sizing/auto` 95, `intrinsic-sizing` 40, `item-placement/dense` 27, `subgrid/grid-lanes` 22, `baseline/column` 18. So the real gap is **subgrid inside grid-lanes**, not masonry.
  - **One clean rule identified**: `column-subgrid-ignores-width-001` has a `display: grid; grid-template-columns: subgrid; width: 50px` subgrid inside a `display: inline grid-lanes; grid-template-columns: 100px` parent. A subgrid must **ignore its own `width`** and take the parent's track size, so the green should fill the 100px square. Our render shows green at **50px with 50px of red** exposed - the subgrid's own width is winning. `subgrid_prepare` copies the parent's track sizes into `l.sub_c` and `grid_inner` turns them into tracks (`layout.e` around 7967), but nothing forces the subgrid's used width to those tracks.
  - No source change shipped. **Do not plan grid work from the "793 grid-lanes failures" note** - measure first; the shape has changed.
- **Round 33 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the `escapes` fix was built in two halves, measured, and reverted.**
  - **`sheet.decls_of` half: correct but scoreless.** Changing it to trim the TOKENS and decode afterwards, instead of trimming the decoded text, does what it should - the loader then reports `color=[ yellow]` and `background=[ red]` with the escape's space preserved. But `syntax` stays **233 of 275**, so it changes no test on its own. Reverted rather than shipped speculatively.
  - **`parse_color_value` half: refused and ineffective.** Guarding `parse_color_value` against a leading space (with `decls_of` fixed) still left `escapes` at 14/17 and the PNG byte-identical, and the binary was confirmed newer than both sources, so the guard really was compiled in. So the colours these three tests see are **not** resolved through `parse_color_value` - the yellow comes from somewhere else despite the declaration being `color=[ yellow]` in the rule. That is the next thing to trace, and it is why both halves together changed nothing.
  - Both changes reverted; tree clean. `syntax` stays **233 of 275**.
  - **This family is now at four rounds without a win.** The remaining lead is real (the declaration survives with its leading space and something still paints it yellow), but the cost is out of proportion to 3 tests.
- **Round 32 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the `escapes` failures are a redundant trim in the COLOUR parser.**
  - `escapes-014/-015/-016` use `.test { color:\0020yellow }`. CSS Syntax consumes one whitespace as the escape terminator, so the value is **`" yellow"`** - invalid as a colour - and the earlier `color: white; background: green` must win. **Our tokenizer is correct**: it produces `ident [ yellow]` with the leading space.
  - **The space is lost in `style.parse_color_value`**, whose first line is `let t = cv.trim_ws(v)`; that turns `" yellow"` into a valid `yellow`. The declaration value is already normalised upstream, so this trim is redundant and is what accepts the invalid value. The render (yellow on red) confirms it: the declaration is being honoured.
  - **Fixing it in `sheet.decls_of` is INERT** - trimming the tokens and decoding afterwards, instead of trimming the decoded text, changed nothing (byte-identical PNG, `escapes` stayed 14/17) because `parse_color_value` trims again. Reverted.
  - So the fix belongs in `parse_color_value` (or, more safely, by having the value reach it pre-normalised and rejecting a leading space there). **Watch the blast radius**: that trim serves every colour declaration, so gate on `syntax`, `css/css-color` and the vendored corpus.
  - No source change shipped; `syntax` stays **233 of 275**.
- **Round 31 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the `uri` family is DROPPED; the whole parse-and-load chain is proven correct.**
  - `sheet.new_loader` + `sheet.load` on `uri-012`'s exact CSS returns `load err=ok rules=2` with **rule 0 carrying all four declarations** (`color=[red]`, `border=[solid red]`, `background=[red url( { test )]`, `border=[solid green]`) and rule 1 carrying `color=[green]`. So tokenizer, `parse_declarations`, the loader and the sheet all keep the valid declarations exactly as CSS Syntax requires.
  - `expand.parse_layer` sets `good = false` for an unparseable layer and `expand` returns early emitting **no longhands** - which is the correct behaviour for the invalid `background` and must not be "fixed".
  - **Therefore the loss is downstream of the sheet, in `rank_expanded`/the cascade application.** That is the only place left, and it is a different subsystem from everything checked so far.
  - **Family dropped** at 5 tests by the round-30 stop condition: three rounds have gone into it and the remaining work is a cascade investigation rather than a parser fix. `syntax` stays **233 of 275**.
  - **Cleared layers worth not re-checking**: CSS tokenizer (escapes, `badUrl`), `url.parse` (percent-encodes the space, keeps the apostrophe), `e.net.fetch` (`file:` paths are percent-decoded), `parse_declarations`, `sheet.load`, `expand.parse_layer`. Each was verified this round or the two before.
- **Round 30 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): `uri-012` narrowed to the cascade, with the parser cleared.**
  - **Everything up to the cascade is correct, proven by probe.** Tokenizing `p { color: red; border: solid red; background: red url( { test ); border: solid green; }` yields a single `badUrl` token followed by `semicolon`, `ident[border]`, `colon`, `ident[solid]`, `ident[green]` - the trailing declaration survives tokenizing. And `parse_declarations` on the **block's inner tokens** returns `err=ok count=4` with all four intact, including `border = [solid green]`.
  - **Probe trap worth remembering**: `parse_declarations` takes a *block's inner tokens*, not a whole stylesheet. Passing the full stream makes the first `{` look like a nested rule, `consume_simple_block` swallows everything, and the call returns `count=0` - which looks exactly like the bug being hunted. Half this round went on that false signal.
  - **So the loss happens after declaration parsing**, in expand/cascade (`e.fmt.css.expand` / `style.rank_expanded` / `cascade`), where the invalid `background: red url( { test )` value is presumably invalidating more than its own declaration.
  - `sheet.declaration_supported` is **dead code** (no callers) - do not read it as part of the path.
  - No source change shipped; `syntax` stays **233 of 275**.
- **Round 29 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): the `uri-*` failures are INVALID-DECLARATION RECOVERY, not loading.**
  - The whole loading chain was proven correct this round, so none of it needs touching: the tokenizer decodes escapes (round 28), `url.parse` gives `path=[/.../support/'green%20block.png]` - space percent-encoded, apostrophe kept - and `e.net.fetch` already percent-decodes a `file:` path before reading it.
  - **The real defect is error recovery.** `uri-012` has `p { color: red; border: solid red; background: red url( { test ); border: solid green; }` - a deliberately invalid `URL` token (`flags: invalid`). CSS Syntax says a `bad-url` makes **only that declaration** invalid; the rule's other declarations still apply. Our render shows **red text on a red background** where the reference is green with a green border, so the invalid `url()` is poisoning the whole rule (the trailing `border: solid green` and the later `p { color: green }` both lose).
  - Next step: find where a `bad-url` propagates past its declaration - most likely the declaration parser in `e.fmt.css.syntax`/`sheet.e` rejecting the rule rather than the declaration.
  - No source change shipped; `syntax` stays **233 of 275**.

  - **Found the concrete defect**: `svg_entry` fills an absent `width` with the **300px default** (`attr_number(p, node, "width", 300.0)`), so for `inline-replaced-width-003`'s SVG (`height="50"`, no width) the reported intrinsic became 300x50 and `sratio` **6.0** instead of the drawing's real 2:1. The viewBox fallback that would correct it ran only when **both** width and height were absent, so one present attribute defeated it.

So the ratio reported here is not what reaches `used_replaced_size` for an inline SVG - the next attempt must trace the value from `intrinsic_of` **through the measure hook** to the layout, not assume it.

Reverted. Note that reporting `has_height: has_sh` at the same time **regressed 18 -> 17**, so the height must stay definite (`has_height: true`); that is a real constraint for whoever tries again.

- **Round 23 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): FIXED - replaced sizing kept the ratio after a clamp (+4).**
  - **Cause.** `used_replaced_size` made the ratio correction and then **undid it**: the trailing `if wc != w { w = wc }` compared against a `wc` computed *before* the correction. A 200x100 image with `height: 12.5em` and `max-height: 6.25em` therefore clamped its height to 100 and then went back to 200 wide, instead of following the clamped height through its 1:1 ratio - so the red swatch stayed 200x100 and poked out from under the 100x100 green box meant to cover it. The instrumentation printed `fires=true` and then `w=200`, which is what named it.
  - **Fix**: apply both clamps first, then re-derive the **unconstrained** axis from the ratio - and only when exactly one axis was constrained (if both were, the author's sizes win).
  - **css/CSS2/normal-flow/inline-replaced 14 -> 18 of 20**, `normal-flow` **623 -> 627 of 675**, vendored corpus unchanged at **190/212**.
  - This is the sixth fix that came out of printing state at the decision point rather than reasoning about it; the pattern is consistent enough to be the default first move now.

Our rendering draws it only 120px wide. So the test wants the text to **overflow** its 120px box, and a probe shows the span coming out `w=120 h=120 lines=1` at `font_size=30` with eight characters - i.e. the advance is being squeezed to fit rather than overflowing. **Stop condition applied**: two rounds on this family with no win, so it is left alone; the note above stands.

Test 021 is `<div><span>12345678</span></div>` with the div absolutely positioned (`left/right/width: auto`, RTL body) and the span `display: inline-block; max-width: 4em` at `font: 30px Ahem`, so the preferred width should be **120px**. In our rendering the green box is **wider than the reference's and is clipped at the viewport edge**; the reference shows the whole ~130px square inside the page. The assert states the rule exactly: *"if its own child uses a constrained length resulting from a max-width, that constrained length defines the preferred width in the shrink-to-fit calculation"*, i.e. a child's `max-width` must cap the parent's max-content contribution. Look at `content_widths` -> `inline_widths`, which takes an atomic piece's width from `p.trail` and never applies the child's clamp.

  - **Cause.** `collect_inline` set `ab.aux = 2u32` on EVERY absolutely positioned piece. A **block-level** absolute box therefore took the inline branch of CSS 2.1 10.3.7, where a right-to-left container places it at `static_x - width` - it landed on the LEFT edge of an RTL parent instead of the right. The flag now goes on only inside the branch that already tests `inline_level || was_inline`.
  - **How it was found**, after two rounds of wrong theories: instrumenting `paint_atomic` printed `box=16 bx=3 pos=Absolute` where the layout had computed **103**, which proved painting was fine and the *layout* value was wrong; instrumenting the absolute-positioning path then printed `aux=2 has_l=false has_r=false static_x=0 w=100 parentdir=Rtl`, which named the flag.

  - Worth keeping: printing the box's own state (`aux`, `has_l`/`has_r`, `static_x`, `w`, the parent's direction) at the decision point is what finally settled it. Reading the PNG told me *where* it was wrong; only the state dump told me *why*.

The candidate paths left are `paint_inline_layer` (which paints atomic fragments from `fr.x`) and the `paint_floats`/`paint_atomic` fallbacks. Instrument `paint_atomic` to print the box and the `x` it is given, and compare with `abs_origin`; that single run should name the caller.

So the bug is inside `collect`/`paint_entry` or the deferred-entry clip, not the layer list.

- **Round 14 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): XHTML parsing FIXED - `inline-table-zorder` 2/5 -> 5/5, `normal-flow` +6.**
  - **The fix is two halves and neither works alone.** (1) `e.fmt.mime.extension_type` now maps `xhtml`/`xht` to `application/xhtml+xml`, so `page.load` turns `html.set_xml_syntax` on for those files; (2) the tree builder's `style` case (`html.e`, `nm_in(",noframes,style,", name)`) uses **TS_RCDATA instead of TS_RAWTEXT when `xml_syntax` is set**. RCDATA is the right state: it decodes entity references and still stops at the closing tag, so the body is not parsed as markup. Verified with a parser probe - HTML mode keeps `p &gt; span { ... }` literal, XHTML mode yields `p > span { ... }`, a real child combinator.
  - **Round 13's half-fix is what made this look deep**: skipping `tb_text_element` entirely lost the body (the style text came back empty) because the content then went through normal tree building. Keeping the element and only changing the tokenizer *state* is the whole answer.
  - **css/CSS2/normal-flow/inline-table-zorder 2 -> 5 of 5**, `normal-flow` **617 -> 623 of 675**, css/CSS2/lists unchanged at 143/155, vendored corpus unchanged at **190/212**.
  - **Parser gate**: html5lib **1791/1791** still, which matters because this round changes the tokenizer path for a text element. Always re-run it when touching `html.e`.
- **Round 13 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): the `inline-table-zorder` failures are an XHTML parsing gap, not paint order.**
  - **All three (`-003/-004/-005`) hinge on entity decoding inside `<style>`.** Their stylesheets are written `div#test &gt; span { ... }` and their references use plain selectors, so if `&gt;` survives literally the selector is invalid, the declarations vanish, and the test renders the un-styled shape. Round 12's guess ("paint order") was wrong.
  - **`&gt;` inside `<style>` is an HTML tokenizer question, and we always take RAWTEXT.** Confirmed with a parser probe: the style element's text is `p &gt; span { ... }` verbatim in HTML mode. `.xht`/XHTML documents would decode it, because there the body is character data rather than raw text.
  - **The correct fix is real but deeper than a flag check.** Two things are needed: (1) `e.fmt.mime.extension_type` has **no `xht`/`xhtml` entry**, so `.xht` never becomes `application/xhtml+xml` and `page.load` never turns `html.set_xml_syntax` on; (2) the tree builder decides RAWTEXT for `style` at `html.e:2180` (`tb_text_element(p, tk, TS_RAWTEXT)`) - skipping that in XML mode alone is **not** enough, because the style body then goes through normal tree building and its text is lost (the probe returned an empty style body). XML mode needs the builder to collect character data for `style` and decode references in it.
  - Attempted both changes this round, measured the result (empty style text), and **reverted** rather than ship a half-built path. `css/CSS2/normal-flow/inline-table-zorder` stays 2 of 5.
  - Note `html.e:496` is a *different* RAWTEXT site (it serves the `tokenize` helper used for CSS and other content); the tree builder has its own at 2180. Editing only 496 changes nothing for a parsed document.
- **Round 12 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): inline-table baseline fixed (+1), the rest of `normal-flow` classified.**
  - **`set_baseline` let a table fall through to `last_baseline`** (the last in-flow child), but CSS 2.1 10.8.1 makes an inline-table's baseline that of its **first row** - which `first_baseline` already computes. Adding a `.Table` case takes `inline-table-valign-001` (its assertion is exactly this rule); **css/CSS2/normal-flow/inline-table 9 -> 10 of 15**, css/CSS2/tables 207 -> 208 of 250, vendored corpus unchanged at 190/212, `normal-flow` 617 of 675.
  - `inline-table-002a/002b` **still fail** and are a different gap: their table has no row boxes at all (a bare `display: inline-table` holding a block child), so `first_baseline` finds nothing. Recorded rather than folded into the fix.
  - **All six `inline-replaced-*` failures are image/SVG intrinsic-ratio cases** (flags `image` or `nonHTML svg`), not rule bugs: they need the replaced-element intrinsic pipeline (`html_view`'s default host is `none_intrinsic`, returning `zero`). Do not plan them as one-line fixes.
  - **The three `inline-table-zorder-*` failures are paint order** - an inline-table's background must be painted under a later inline that overlaps it via a negative margin; ours is reversed. A paint-walk fix, separate from layout.
  - **Build note**: with another session's 9 GB process running, `neper-self.exe emit-executable` died with `fatal: out of memory` (exit 137) on the reftest runner. **`--arena 2g` builds it**; retrying without it just burns time.
- **Round 11 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): the list-style `applies-to` failures are FIXED (+7) - an empty list item was wrongly self-collapsing.**
  - **Cause, traced rather than guessed.** `self_collapsing` skips a box's marker child and then looks for other children; an **empty** `display: list-item` therefore looked empty, so the whole item was judged self-collapsing. The self-collapsing branch of `layout_block_child` returns *without laying out children*, so the item's subtree was never traversed: the item stayed `w=0 h=0`, `position_markers` was never called for it, and its marker box kept `w=0 text=""`. A marker occupies a line, so the box is not self-collapsing - `ret false` on the marker branch fixes it.
  - **How it was pinned**: `position_markers` was instrumented (it never received the list item's box) and so was `layout_block_child` (the traversal stopped at the styled parent's anonymous child). After the fix an empty list item measures `w=704` with a marker at `x=-32 w=32 h=16`.
  - **css/CSS2/lists 136 -> 143 of 155** (the round-8 counter fix is the other 9 of the gains since the 127 baseline). Gates unchanged: vendored corpus **190/212**, css-text **785/1154**, css-overflow **318/501**, css-content **6/14**.
  - **Round 9's "empty-block width bug" stays retracted** - blocks were never zero-width; this was list-item-specific. The real lesson from rounds 9-11 is the probe rule below, which is what finally made the answer visible.
  - **Probe rule that cost three rounds**: `rect_of(Page, id)` returns the first non-anonymous box whose `node` matches, which is **not necessarily the element you named** when the box list holds several boxes or extra wrapper nodes. **Dump every box (`inner`, `anon`, `inline`, `node`, `parent`, `first`, `next`, `x`, `y`, `w`, `h`, `text.len`) and match by `node` explicitly.** Every wrong turn in rounds 9, 10 and the first half of 11 came from skipping that.
- **Round 10 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): round 9's "empty-block width bug" is RETRACTED - it was a probe artifact.**
  - **Blocks are NOT zero-width when empty.** Instrumenting `used_width_block` directly showed `cbw=800 avail=800 ml=0 mr=0 extras=0 cw=800 -> w=800` for both the empty block and its parent. A box dump then explained the earlier `w=0` readings: with no sibling, `rect_of("<id>")` matched a **different box** than the element's own (the box list held `node=3 w=800`, `node=4 w=0` and one extra `node=5 w=0` box), so the round-9 probe was reading a wrapper, not the element. Adding a sibling moved `rect_of` onto the right box and it read `w=800` immediately. The engine is fine here.
  - **The lesson is about the probe, not the engine**: `rect_of(Page, id)` matches the first non-anonymous box whose `node` equals `get_element_by_id`. When a box list contains several boxes and extra wrapper nodes, that is not necessarily the element you named. **Dump every box (`inner`, `anon`, `inline`, `node`, `x`, `y`, `w`, `h`, `text.len`) and match by `node` explicitly before trusting any single-element reading** - two rounds were spent on a phantom because I did not.
  - What is still true: the 19 `list-style*-applies-to` tests fail, all with an **empty** `display: list-item` target, `style.list-item` boxes coming out `w=0 h=0`, and the marker box never appearing in the dump even though `position_markers` sets `mb.w`/`mb.h`/`mb.text` unconditionally and falls back to `mb.y = 0` when there is no baseline. So the open question is narrower than round 9 claimed: **is `position_markers` reached at all for an empty list-item** (it is called from `layout_children` and from the self-collapsing branch of `layout_block_child`), and does a zero-height list-item get a line box. Start there, with a box dump.
  - css/CSS2/lists unchanged at **136 of 155**; no source change shipped.
- **Round 9 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): the `list-style applies-to` cause is a general EMPTY-BLOCK WIDTH BUG, not a marker problem.** — **RETRACTED in round 10; see the entry above. The width measurement it rests on was a probe artifact.**
  - **A block with no children and no height comes out `w = 0` instead of the containing block's width.** Reproduced in isolation: `<div style="margin-left:96px"></div>` (an empty block, `width` auto) yields `w=0, h=0`, while the same element with `height:10px` yields `w=704` correctly. Adding `height: 16px` to the failing WPT structure makes the marker appear perfectly (`x=-32, w=32, h=16, textlen=4`), so the marker code is **not** at fault — the marker is only positioned once the box has a width and a line.
  - **This is why every `list-style*-applies-to` test uses an empty target**: they are incidental victims. The 19 failures all reduce to the empty box collapsing.
  - **Round 8's `margin-left` lead was WRONG** and is now dead: the same failure happens with no margin at all. Recorded so it is not chased again.
  - Where I got to: the path is `layout_block_child` -> `self_collapsing(l, c, cw)` true -> `used_width_block(l, c, cw, cw)` -> `cb2.h = 0` -> `position_markers(l, c)`. `used_width_block` computes `cw = avail - ml - mr - extras`, so a zero width means `avail` was already 0 when it was called. The chain breaks **one level up**: in the probe, the `div` that *contains* the span is itself `w=0` even though it is not empty and its parent is `w=800`. That is the next thing to instrument (the `avail`/`cw` handed to `layout_block_child`), not the marker code.
  - css/CSS2/lists unchanged at **136 of 155** this round (the round-8 counter fix stands); no source change was shipped because every candidate fix measured inert.
- **Round 8 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): CSS2 counters fixed (+9), the list-style `applies-to` family mapped.**
  - **`counter-reset`/`counter-increment` rejected an explicit `+` sign.** Both parse the value with `str.parse_i64`, which accepts only `-`, so `counter-reset: ident +10` failed to parse and the counter silently took its default. Fixed with `counter_integer` in layout.e (strip a leading `+` before delegating). The fix is deliberately local to the counter properties, not in `str.parse_i64`, which HTML attributes and reflection also use. **css/CSS2/lists 127 -> 136 of 155:** counter-reset-009/-022/-035/-046/-048 and counter-increment-009/-022/-035/-048. Vendored corpus 190/212 and css-content 6/14 unchanged.
  - **The 19 remaining `list-style*` failures are all `applies-to-008/009/012/014/017`** — the same five numbers repeated across `list-style`, `list-style-type` and `list-style-image`, so it is ONE cause, not nineteen. They all use an **empty** `display: list-item` target, and every one matches the same reference (`css/reference/single_square_list_marker.xht`, a `list-item` holding `&nbsp;` with `margin-left: 96px`). `applies-to-001` (table-row-group) **passes** against that same reference, so "empty list-item loses its marker" is NOT the explanation — I checked and the marker is created unconditionally (`gen_child`, layout.e:962) and `position_markers` does run for an empty item. The open lead is the marker's interaction with **`margin-left` on the list-item or its parent**: in 001 the margin is on the enclosing table and the test passes, in 009 it is on the list-item's parent `span` and the test fails. Not yet confirmed.
  - `normal-flow` re-measured at **498-58 = 440 of 498** and is a long tail, not a cluster: block-in-inline 11, inline-replaced 6, inline-table 6, max-height 5, then 4s. The 11 `block-in-inline` failures are the same structural gap round 7 identified (CSS 2.1 §9.2.1.1 split), so they will not move until that split exists.
- **Round 7 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): the `abspos-007` cause is NOT the absolute box's own placement.** Round 6 blamed `scan_line` setting the box's `y` to the current line; that was wrong, and two attempted fixes (in `scan_line`, then in `emit_line` keyed on the container) both measured **inert** - CSS2 positioning stayed 438 of 498 and the vendored corpus 190 of 212 - so both were reverted.
  - **The real cause is that the inline container is not an inline formatting context at all.** Instrumenting `emit_line` showed it running with `cont=7` (an *anonymous* block) and never with `cont=3` (the `display: inline` div), whose `inline_level` is false: the inline box is **blockified** because it holds a block child (`style.blockify`, style.e:1688), and its text is then split into an anonymous block. So an absolute box inside it is laid out in a block context and gets a block static position — the WPT test depends on the true inline split (CSS 2.1 §9.2.1.1: an inline box containing a block box is broken up), which this engine simplifies away.
  - **Do not re-approach this as a placement tweak.** Any fix has to give an inline box with block children the real split (or at least keep enough of the inline frame for the static position of absolute children). Both cheap variants are measured dead ends; `abspos-007` should stay red until that split exists.
  - A box dump (`inner`, `inline_level`, `node`, `x`, `y`, `w`, `h` for every non-text box) is the fastest way to see this; the numbers for the WPT structure were: root 800x600, body 800x48, the inline div **blockified** at 784x32, the abs box at 0, and the control block at y=16 inside it.
- **Round 6 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): CSS2 positioning mapped, one bug located but not fixed.**
  - **CSS2's 637 failures group as positioning 60, normal-flow 58, tables 43, syntax 42, floats-clear 35, text 32, generated-content 29, margin-padding-clear 29, floats 28, lists 28.** The height/max/min absolute families are nearly green already (23 of 26); the work is in **`abspos` (32 of 54, 22 failures)** and **`absolute-non-replaced-width`** (24 of 36) - the latter is mixed, only 9 of its 36 files use RTL, so it is not one rule.
  - **`abspos-007` located and reproduced outside the runner.** The test puts an absolutely positioned `div` and a control `div` as siblings inside a `display: inline` parent; the absolute box must cover the control ("no red"). Our geometry puts it **one line too high**: with a stand-in fixture the absolute box lands at `y=8` and the control at `y=24`. Cause: a block-level absolute box inside an **inline** parent is queued as an `.Abs` piece at its collection point (`collect_inline`, layout.e:2024) and takes that line's `y`; CSS 2.1 wants the static position of the hypothetical box that would follow it, which is the next block sibling - not known at collection time, so it needs a post-pass, unlike the `aux == 2` inline case that `3669` already corrects (x only).
  - Measuring this needs a layout probe with a real `Measure`: `dom.parse` with `zero` options rejects everything (`max_nodes: 0`), and `layout_document` with `measure: zero` computes nothing. Copy `chars_of`/`ahem_width`/`ahem_metrics`/`ahem_intrinsic` and `html.Options { max_bytes: 65536, max_nodes: 1024, max_attributes: 32, max_depth: 64 }` from `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/css_layout` - that fixture's `build` + `rect_of` is the fastest way to see real box geometry.
- **Round 4 of the conformance goal (2026-11-07): two clusters ruled out, one fix reverted.**
  - **`dom/events/non-cancelable-when-passive/` is NOT a browser failure and cannot be measured here.** All 42 files / 53 subtests were run: **40 of the 53 fail with `action_sequence() is not implemented by testdriver-vendor.js`** - they drive real input through the WPT testdriver, which this harness does not provide. They are unrunnable until a testdriver exists; do not treat the ~40 red results there as conformance debt. The same applies to any `dom/events/scrolling/` file.
  - **The `Document-contentType` cluster is smaller and differently caused than it looks.** 15 files, 14 subtests, **6 pass**. The image types (`bmp`/`gif`/`jpeg`/`png`) **time out** loading an image as a document - a separate hang, not a content-type bug. The genuine `contentType` failures (css/plain/xml/javascripturi) load the file in an **iframe**, so the document is created by the *frame* path; patching `page.start_scripts` (the top-level path) changed nothing - 6/14 before and after - and was reverted.
  - **`dom/nodes/moveBefore` and `insertion-removing-steps` are not quick wins**: their failures are style-recalculation invalidation for `:has()`/`:empty` after a move, and HTML's synchronous script-observes-style insertion steps.

- **Conformance round of 2026-11-07 (the "cover all conformity tests" goal, round 1).**
 Re-measured all four domains on the committed tree rather than quoting the entries above, and found two defects on the way:
  - **HTML is `1791/1791` (fragment 192, errored 0)**, the recorded floor - and the run used to **abort** before this round. `decode_reference` guarded `at < text.len` for the numeric path but handed the offset straight to `lookup_entity`, which read `text[at]` with no check: a document ending in a bare `&` trapped with a bounds error, which the entity data file does. Fixed in `691667a8d` (two lines); that is why the floor is reached at all now.
  - **CSS re-measured per suite, all matching the recorded figures**: flexbox **635/908**, writing-modes **635/995**, css-content **6/14**, grid alignment **96/151**, vendored corpus **190/212**. So the HTML fix regressed nothing.
  - **DOM, first whole-tree measurement: `39,021 / 39,590 subtests (98.56%)` over 614 files** (all of `dom/` except `.https.` files and the `scrolling/` testdriver tests). The handover's "11,664 subtests" figure is on a different, smaller list - the denominators are **not comparable**, so use this one for this tree and say which list a figure came from.
    - **How to measure it** (this took several failed attempts): split the list into files-per-process and run the processes **in parallel**, because one `web-run` process cannot do it. `dom/ranges/` must run **one file per process** (`Range-selectNode.html` alone passes 296/296 and needs **2.7 GB**), and everything else runs fine 8 per process. Give every process a **timeout** (`Start-Process` + `WaitForExit(180000)`): one hung test otherwise holds a parallel worker and the whole batch stalls with no output, which looks identical to "still running".
    - The two halves: `dom/ranges/` **27,361 / 27,593**, everything else **11,660 / 11,997**. Four non-range chunks (32 files) timed out and are excluded from the figure - they are named in `tmp/nr_res.txt` when re-running.
    - A chunked run that reports only ~25.7k subtests for the whole tree is **wrong**: it means the range chunks died and took their files' subtests with them. Add the range total separately or the figure silently loses 27k subtests.
  - **`web-run`'s harness diagnostics were unreadable and are fixed (`7437336ee`).** A failing page printed `HARNESS <path>: [50, 9]` - the raw byte array of the status-and-tab slice, not its text - so those pages could not be diagnosed. Binding the slice to a `str` first (as the `FAIL` branch already does) prints `HARNESS <path>: 2`. **This is the first thing to check when a `dom/` page reports 0 subtests.**
  - **Running `dom/` as one process is not viable**: 614 files in one `web-run` process consumed ~3,500 CPU-seconds and then died of an **access violation** partway through, losing the whole measurement (the same failure mode `tests/browser/web/run.sh` chunks against - *"so a crash or a hang loses one chunk and not the run"*). Chunk the list (8 files per process) and aggregate; the per-file failure that looks like a crash in isolation is often only a harness status 2.
  - **The recorded biggest-gap cluster was ALREADY FIXED.** The 2026-11-06 summary listed `Iterator.prototype.includes` 0/44, `windows` 0/40, `chunks` 0/38 and `join` 0/18 - 140 tests at zero. Re-run today they are **44/44, 40/40, 38/38, 18/18**. Those features are implemented in `lib/e/js/prelude.e` (the `chore` section at 262-345); the summary predated them. **Do not plan work off that summary file** (`D:/wptdata/t262_summary4.txt`); re-run the affected directory first.

- **The app on three targets: all emit** (x64-windows 22 MB, x64-linux 22 MB, wasm32-wasi 16 MB). **wasm32-wasi is run-verified** — `scripts/run-browser-wasm.mjs` runs it under `lib/wasm/neper-host.js` to its windowless exit 0. **Windows is run-verified again (2026-11-05)**: it had begun exiting 14 with "the scene was invalid"; a worktree bisect pinned that to `74991611f`, whose present-time check compared the addresses of two `QueueState` values and so refused every present from a caller holding its own `Queue` for the same queue. The proper fix is `44dc9f93b`: `window.open` takes the renderer's queue so one queue per device is used, `app.init` creates it before the window, and the check is back to device-and-index so `#641` holds. `app-browser-win.exe about:blank` keeps a live window after 25 seconds, the wasm build exits 0, the corpus stays at 189 of 211, and `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/gpu_present` (red with exit 76 before) now prints `gpu present ok` and exits 0. **Linux is emitted but not run here**, because WSL is denied in this environment.
- **Vendored reftest corpus: 189 of 211** (every landed fix has a fixture; the tree's own regression suite).

**Re-verified on the committed tree 2026-11-05** (the four suites this stretch touched, plus the corpus): vendored corpus **189 of 211**, `css/css-grid/alignment` **94 of 151**, `css/css-content` **6 of 14**, `css/css-flexbox/balance` **8 of 32** - all as recorded, no regressions from the twenty-plus measurement rounds. Nothing in the last three bullets is a one-round item; the three CSS families in the first bullet were the recommended block, and this stretch closed them as: `break-spaces` (four refuted shapes, needs the piece loop), `flex-wrap: balance` (two neutral implementations, the intrinsic width ignores the wrap), `content: url()` (the pseudo path works up to a created box that no container lists, and every layer around it measured).

## 0. Resume in five commands

```bash
cd D:/repos/neper-wt-browser            # a worktree of D:/repos/neper on branch browser-conformance
git status --short | grep -v '^??'      # expect nothing (section 2)
git log --oneline -3                    # head was 5e6923d83 (the second merge of origin/master) before this handover edit
gh auth status | grep -B1 Active        # must be nebosa-company; oboobortot gets a 403 on this repository
D:/repos/neper/build/windows/neper-self.exe emit-executable tests/browser/wpt/run.e . x64 windows build/windows/run-wpt<N>.exe
```

Compilers live in the **main checkout** (`D:/repos/neper/build/windows/`); the worktree's `build/windows` is cleaned. Use `neper-self.exe` (`neper-try.exe` fails type-checking on any edit to `run.e`). Use a new `run-wpt<N>.exe` name per build.

**Do not pass `--arena` when emitting a large program** (2026-11-04). On `emit-executable` that flag sizes the **compiler's own arena**, not the program's: `--arena 1g/2g` makes `neper-self.exe` fail with "the compiler's arena is exhausted" on `tests/browser/wpt/run.e` and the browser app. A compiler *built* with `--arena 3g`/`6g` (`build/windows/neper-big.exe`) emits the browser app for all three targets but died with `fatal: access violation` on `run.e` for both windows and linux, so it is not reliable for every large program — use it for the app, `neper-self.exe` for the reftest runner. With no flag, `neper-self.exe` emits `run.e`. Verified 2026-11-04 from the current tree: `examples/browser/main.e` emits x64-windows (22.3 MB), x64-linux (22.4 MB) and wasm32-wasi (16.5 MB); the Linux and wasm outputs are **not run** in this environment (no WSL; the wasm app needs a canvas host).
