## 3. The app and its three targets

`examples/browser/main.e` is the windowed browser (tabs, omnibox, back/forward/reload, star, history, profile file `neper-browser.profile` in the working directory), over `lib/e/web/shell.e` and `lib/e/web/page.e`. Build **with a large arena**:

```bash
C=D:/repos/neper/build/windows/neper-self.exe
$C emit-executable examples/browser/main.e . x64 windows OUT.exe --arena 4g
$C emit-executable examples/browser/main.e . x64 linux   OUT      --arena 4g
# wasm needs a compiler built with a bigger arena (the stock one runs out of arena on this program):
$C emit-executable src/main.e . x64 windows D:/Temp/neper-big.exe --arena 6g
D:/Temp/neper-big.exe emit-executable examples/browser/main.e . wasm32 wasi build/wasm-sweep/neper_browser.wasm
```

- **Windows:** verified (rendering, scripts, timers, real clicks, `rules.html`). The default arena makes every https page fail as "not private" (the tab arena and the TLS chain check share it; an RSA verify that cannot allocate now reports a memory error rather than `UnknownAuthority`, so the page says the certificate could not be checked — the app still needs a large arena to load https pages).
- **Linux:** verified on a virtual display (rendering, wheel scroll, a real click on an `onclick` div, `rules.html` combo toggle). Recipe: WSL has `Xvfb` and `xdotool`; WSLg mounts `/tmp/.X11-unix` read-only, so run `wsl -u root -e unshare -m bash script.sh` where the script does `mount -t tmpfs none /tmp/.X11-unix`, `export HOME=/home/gaddl`, starts `Xvfb :99 -ac -screen 0 1280x900x24`, serves the page with `python3 -m http.server`, runs the binary with `DISPLAY=:99`, drives it with `xdotool` (`click 5` is wheel down), and `xwd -root -display :99 -out f.xwd`. Convert XWD (25 big-endian header words, 32-bit BGRA) with PIL. Fonts: DejaVu files are in the face list. The Linux binary was not re-run after the last font edit (4a19b7a85) or after the merge.
- **Web (wasm32-wasi):** `tests/wasm/browser/neper_browser.html` runs the app on a canvas; serve with `node scripts/serve-wasm.mjs --isolated --port 8765` (the window needs a cross-origin isolated page) and open `/tests/wasm/browser/neper_browser.html?url=<encoded data: URL>`. Verified once in the Browser pane with a `data:` page. **Not verified on the web:** scripts, clicks, scrolling. The host (`lib/wasm/neper-host.js`) has no sockets, so only `data:` pages and seeded files load; web networking needs a host bridge. Fonts are the two Jost files the page seeds (`jost-regular.ttf`, `jost-bold.ttf`); there is no italic.

Things that silently broke pages before, all fixed and recorded in D5941: a 128 MB tab arena starved the page's JS runtime (now 256 MB, about 15 tabs in a 4 GB arena; 512 MB made the web_shell fixture's default arena run out); a pending timer never ran in an idle window; a tap reached links only, never `onclick`; the renderer held 16 fonts and dropped the rest (now 32).

## 4. The loop that works

1. Pick a failing cluster: `build/windows/run-wpt<N>.exe --show --only css/<suite>/ D:/wptdata/wpt/MANIFEST.txt > f.txt`, then count by prefix (`grep FAIL f.txt | sed -E 's/-?[0-9]+[a-z]?\.(x?html?|xht)( \(match\))?$//' | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn`).
2. See one test: `--only css/<suite>/<test> --out D:/Temp/rv` writes `.test.png` and `.ref.png` for failures only. The reference is rendered by our engine too; read the reference markup before trusting it. Compare run-lengths or bounding boxes with PIL.
3. **`--text` prints every laid-out fragment as `x,y:text`** (`lib/e/ui/html_view.e` `fragment_text`). It found the counter, multicol and font bugs; use it before reading pixels. Empty boxes have no fragment, so colour runs in the PNGs are the only view of them.
4. Fix in `lib/e/fmt/css/layout.e` (or `style.e`, `paint.e`). Apply multi-site edits with `D:/repos/neper/build/windows/patch.exe spec` (`@@@ path` / `<<<` / `===` / `>>>`), single edits with the Edit tool; never a Python patch script.
5. Gate before every commit, no suite may drop: css-lists, css-multicol, css-overflow, css-sizing, css-flexbox, css-grid, css-writing-modes (10-15 min each, quicker if run one at a time) and CSS2 (**about two hours** on this machine; a monitor on a done-file, not chained sleeps). A sweep that "stops growing" is usually one slow test; check CPU before killing.
6. Commit only your paths with the Co-Authored-By trailer, and **push after each commit** (the user asked for commits and pushes from time to time). Pushes need the `nebosa-company` GitHub account.

## 5. Traps

- Compiler "arena exhausted": the compiler itself needs a bigger arena (build one with `--arena 6g`); a program's `--arena` is separate. `neper-try.exe` mis-types edits to `run.e`.
- A PowerShell window capture of the app must call `user32 SetProcessDPIAware()` first, or `GetWindowRect`/`PrintWindow` crop the top-left of the real window and a correct render looks clipped. Capture only the app window (`PrintWindow` on `MainWindowHandle`), never the whole screen: the user's other windows (a browser with their tabs) are on it.
- Real mouse input: use `docs/grid-parity/drive.ps1` (it shows the warning-emoji overlay the user requires). Posted `WM_*` messages scroll but do not click.
- The app refuses `file://` by design; serve a folder with `python -m http.server` (use `D:/tools/neper-pyenv/Scripts/python.exe -I`). Git Bash has no python.
- Multicol: one tall column is laid out and then sliced. D5940 builds a table of column starts (`mc_prepare`, `column_of`, `col_start`, `col_end`, `col_dy`) for the plain balanced case only; spanner runs (`colify_run`) and `column-wrap` rows keep the old formula.
- `self_collapsing` (empty blocks) runs before the block layout path: anything that gives an empty block a height (size containment, now) must be checked there too.
- A passing test can pass by coincidence (test and reference wrong the same way); judge a flip by what newly fails.
- Windows is Git Bash: `D:/...` to the `.exe` tools, `/d/...` to bash tools.

## 6. Open work, largest first

- **The hanging-punctuation images, actually compared (2026-11-05).** The earlier "blank reference" reading was wrong: a manifest written outside the corpus makes the runner resolve `css/...` under the manifest's own directory, find nothing and paint an empty page (so "blank pairs" counts *your* mistake, not the corpus's). Copying `hanging-punctuation-first.html` and its reference into `tests/browser/wpt/reftests/` and rendering with `--out` shows both non-blank, and the difference is confined to the last two blocks: the plain hang and the second-line case already match, while the `text-align:justify` + `text-indent:1em` interaction differs by one character (the test line takes one more glyph than the reference). So `hanging-punctuation: first` works and what is left in that file is the indent/justify interaction; `border-left` and the no-strong-second-line cases already agree. Next: make the hang interact with `text-indent` the way the reference spells it (`text-indent:-1em` in the ref versus `hanging-punctuation:first` in the test, with justify spreading over the excluded glyph), and keep using `--out` + images rather than guessing from PASS/FAIL.

- **U+3000 is not a piece at the line start, which is why `hanging-punctuation: first` never fires for it (2026-11-05).** Bisected with `--text` on four one-line pages: `(X` with the property inline hangs (`-16,0:(` + `0,0:X`), a leading *ASCII* space before `(` also hangs (the space is trimmed, `-16,0:(` + `0,0:X`), but `<span>　</span>X` and `　X` both render `0,0:　` + `16,0:X` with no hang, whether the property is on the block, inherited, or set directly on the span. Three fixes tried and reverted: `n == p.clen` (a cluster count, not bytes), `n == p.text.len` with `p.w`, and with `p.w + p.trail` — all still produced no hang, so `proc[p.start]` is not `0xE3` for that piece: the ideographic space is carried as whitespace/trail rather than as the start of a text piece. Hanging it is therefore a **piece-builder** change (make U+3000 begin a text piece like the openers do), not a change in `hanging_punct_w`.
  - **Instrumented 2026-11-05** (temporary `io.printf` in the fragment loop, reverted): for `<div style="hanging-punctuation:first">　X</div>` the first text piece reports `start=0 len=3 b0=227 w=0 trail=16 flag=1`, and `hanging_punct_w` still returns `hw=0 hn=0`. So the byte is right (`227`, E3), the flag is right (`1`), the piece is right (3 bytes) — but its advance sits **entirely in `trail` (16) with `w=0`**, and the helper's measurement of the single scalar returns 0. The `p.w + p.trail - text_advance(rest)` form did not change the outcome, which means the `p.text` slice the helper sees is longer than the piece's `clen` (it includes the following `X`), so `rest` is 16 and the difference is 0 — instrument `p.text.len` *inside the helper* (not in the loop) before changing the formula again. **Answered 2026-11-05**: printing `p.start`, `proc.len`, `p.clen`, `p.text.len`, `p.w` and `p.trail` at the top of `hanging_punct_w` shows it is called with `start=3 plen=4 clen=1 textlen=1 w=16 trail=0` - i.e. on the piece that starts at `X`, not on the U+3000 piece at byte 0. So the ideographic space piece never reaches the fragment loop: it is dropped (or skipped as leading whitespace) between `emit_text_pieces` and the placement, and the hang code is fine. Fix the *scan*: `out.lead` must not skip a leading space piece whose box has `hanging-punctuation: first` and whose first scalar is U+3000. **Also ruled out 2026-11-05**: exempting such a piece from the `out.lead` skip (`p.w == 0 && !p.ink` for a text piece before content) changed nothing, so that branch is not the one dropping it. The piece the helper sees is the one at `start=3` with `textlen=1`, and `--text` still paints `0,0:　X` as one fragment - so the space is inside the *painted* fragment while the piece the loop processes starts at X. Next: print the piece stream itself (`k`, `start`, `clen`, `text.len` for every piece) in `emit_line` before the fragment loop, not inside a helper. **Border guard ruled out too (2026-11-05)**: `hanging-punctuation-first-and-last-together.html` puts `border: 1px` on the `.hang` block and still hangs, so the guard was made to apply only to inline-level boxes with a border on that side - the directory stayed at 7 of 21 and block 2 still renders `0,0:(This` against the reference `-16,0:(This`. Removing the guard entirely changes nothing either, so the hang is not attempted for that piece at all. Next: print `p.box`, `inline_level`, the style flag and `border_width[3]` for block 2 s first text piece, and check whether the class rule (`.hang { hanging-punctuation: first last }`) reaches that box. **Measured 2026-11-05**: the class rule does reach the box (`flag=3`), and for both cases the piece sits in an **anonymous, inline-level** box whose own `Box.border` is 0 while its *style* carries the border of the nearest element - `sbor=16` for `first.html`s bordered span, `sbor=1` for `first-and-last-together`s bordered block. So neither the Box border, nor `inline_level`, nor `anonymous`, nor `p.box != x.cont` separates them (using `anonymous` as the guard dropped the directory to 5 of 21). The predicate has to be "does the nearest *element* ancestor with a left border differ from the line container": walk up from `p.box` to the first non-anonymous box and compare it with `x.cont`. **Tried and no gain (2026-11-05)**: walking up to the first non-anonymous box and stopping at the first block-level box left the directory at 7 of 21 and the file rendering exactly as before (`0,0:(This` against the reference `-16,0:(This`), so the guard is not what stops this hang either. The reference achieves the hang with a **negative inline margin** (`<span style="margin:0 -1em">`), which shifts the whole run left by the hang width, while css-text hangs only the punctuation and leaves the following text at the content edge; that reference technique cannot be reproduced by hanging one glyph, so this file needs the reference read against our own `--text` line by line before any more code. **Two more measured 2026-11-05**: `hanging-punctuation-inline-001` and `-last-001.xht` both want a closing punctuation that would overflow the line to hang instead of wrapping (`字字字字」` on one line; `これは、「満たす文字」` with the `」` hung), and neither the "closer overflows the line" rule added to the `last` branch, nor dropping its `pos > 0` guard, nor allowing a `.Hard` after the closer, changed the rendering - the directory stayed at 8 of 21. Both files break inside a CJK character run, so the next step is the same piece-stream print as the shy case: the closer is presumably not a piece end at the moment the break is decided. **Closed 2026-11-05 at 12 of 21**: at the shy page's comma piece `pos=64`, `needed=32`, `lim_eff=65` and `last_soft=1`, so the allow-end block leaves `needed` unchanged and the `last_soft` branch takes the break; relaxing `hanging_period_w` to read `p.text.len` (trimming trailing spaces) instead of `clen` changed nothing, so the helper still does not see the comma as that piece's last scalar. A tenth rule attempt is not worth it: the rest of the family is paint (variable-font-size), fonts (force-end-001), RTL reference geometry, a reference that encodes the hang as a negative margin, and this shy path. Move the stream to the tracker's next named feature (column spanners, 65 subtests) and reopen this only with a debugger rather than another print. **Pieces printed 2026-11-05**: `hanging-punctuation-inline-001` gives `字`(32) `字`(32) `字`(32) `字`(32, the div) `」`(32, the span) as separate pieces at a 128 px width, so the closure *is* a piece of its own and the rule "a closer that would overflow hangs" (`needed = w - closer_w`, `pos + w - closer_w <= lim` and `pos + w > lim`) still did not fire - neither with the block style nor with the piece style reading the flag, and the directory stayed at 8 of 21. Instrument the overflow point itself (print `pos`, `lim`, `needed`, the piece style flag and `cn2` for that piece) rather than adding another rule. **Run lookahead tried and reverted (2026-11-05)**: generalising the one-piece lookahead to "the text up to the next hanging punctuation still fits once it is left out" dropped the directory from 12 of 21 to 11, because it lets a closer two or three characters away pull text past the edge. The committed one-piece form is the safe one; `last-001`s Final quotes block needs a narrower rule (only across a single intervening character, and only when that character itself would otherwise start the next line). **The shy break is taken even when the visible text fits (2026-11-05)**: two pages with the same visible text and `hanging-punctuation: allow-end` at 65 px - `a b&shy;c、` and `a bc、` - render `0,0:a b-` + `0,16:c、` and `0,0:a bc、` respectively, so the soft hyphen widens the piece (or its break is taken unconditionally) while the same text without it fits on one line. Making `text_advance` skip soft hyphens (measuring the runs around them) changed neither, so the extra width or the break comes from the piece builder or the segment break table, not from the advance function. That pair is the reproduction to start from; five rule attempts on this file have now been ruled out. **Piece stream printed 2026-11-05**: for the shy page the pieces are `a `(16 px, flag 8) and `bc、`(48 px, flag 8) - the processed text drops the soft hyphen and keeps the comma in the same piece, so the allow-end block should hang it (`pos` 32 + 48 - 16 = 64 <= 65) and yet the line still breaks with a hyphen. The break is therefore taken outside that block - the `p.soft`/`last_soft` path or the paint-time hyphen - so instrument `p.soft` and `last_soft` at that piece next, not the hang. **`variable-font-size` is a paint difference, not a rule (2026-11-05)**: its `--text` fragments are byte-identical to the reference (`-16,12:(`, `0,0:1234`, `128,12:)`) and the rendered images differ by about 8 px on the opener's merged run, so the closer's advance is right and the glyph run paints differently at the span's smaller font size. That is a paint or metrics question, not layout; do not spend a rule attempt on it.

- **`force-end`/`allow-end` were implemented and measured, then reverted with the cause pinned (2026-11-05).** The rule is: a trailing comma or full stop hangs; `force-end` always, `allow-end` only when the punctuation is the character that would overflow (the text before it fits, the punctuation does not, so `needed = w - punctuation_w` and the cursor still advances over it). With that in `scan_line`, `hanging-punctuation-allow-end-basic.html` matched on five of its six cases and the directory stayed at 6 of 21: the blocker is the **soft-hyphen case** (`a b&shy;c、` in a 65 px frame), where the engine breaks at the soft hyphen and shows a hyphen instead of hanging the `、`. So the hang logic is right and the piece stream around a soft hyphen is what to trace next — `--text` on a one-line fixture (`a b&shy;c、`, `hanging-punctuation: allow-end`, `width: 65px`) will show whether the shy splits the piece or the `last_soft` branch fires first. `force-end-001.xht` needs CJK fonts (IPAMincho/IPAGothic) and is not reachable from this engine's font set.
  - **Traced 2026-11-05**: the same page at `width: 200px` renders `a b­c、` on **one line** (the shy is invisible and only breaks when needed), and at `65px` it renders `a b-` + `c、`. So the pieces are `a`, the space, and `b&shy;c` (the shy inside the piece) plus `、`: at `pos` 32 the `b&shy;c` piece (48 wide) overflows, the scan takes the **shy break inside that piece**, and only afterwards does it see the `、` that would have hung. The fix needs a **lookahead across the shy break**: before breaking at a soft hyphen, check whether the rest of the line, with its trailing punctuation hung, fits. That is a real change to the break decision, not to the hang, and it is why the implementation was reverted a second time.

- **Two hanging-punctuation attempts measured and reverted (2026-11-05, after `first` and `last` landed at 6 of 21).**
  - **RTL**: giving `hanging_punct_w` an `rtl` flag (border on the other side, `cx + hw` instead of `cx - hw`) left the directory at 6 of 21 and `hanging-punctuation-first-rtl.html` still failing; its reference spells the hang as `text-indent: -1em` inside an RTL block, and `--text` shows the reference's opener at `0,0` with `Hang` at `16,0` while ours is at `32,0` — the reference is not hanging on the right edge the way the mirror of our LTR model predicts, so read the reference's own geometry from `--text` first.
  - **Border guard scope**: `hanging-punctuation-first-and-last-together.html` puts `border: 1px` on the `.hang` block itself and still hangs, so the guard must only look at an *inline* box's border. Restricting it to `p.box != x.cont` dropped the directory from 6 to 4, which means the piece's box for a block's own text is not always the line's container (`x.cont`) — instrument which box a piece reports before scoping the guard, and keep both `first` and `last` green.

- **`run-wpt --text` is the instrument for text-layout work, and it now pins the hanging-punctuation remainder.** `--text` prints every fragment as `x,y:text`, so two pages can be compared line by line instead of by guessing from PASS/FAIL. Measured on `hanging-punctuation-first.html` against its reference (2026-11-05):
  - the plain hang, the second line and the `border-left` case already agree;
  - `<span>(</span>This should hang.` puts the opener at `0,0:` in the test against `-16,0:` in the reference, because the hang only fired when the line's first *piece* was the text piece — an inline box emits an `.Open` piece first, so `k == lead` was false. Restricting the trigger to "the line's first text piece" fixes the opener's position but moves the *following* text as well (`-16,0:This should hang.` instead of `0,0:`), because `this_w -= hw` shrinks the following fragment by the hang while the cursor should advance by the full width;
  - therefore the correct shape is a **fragment split**: flush the opener alone at `cx - hw` with width `hw`, then start the remaining text of that piece at `cx` with width `this_w - hw` and let the cursor advance by the whole `this_w`. `hanging_punct_w` has to return the opener's byte length as well as its advance;
  - the `justify` block still differs after that (test spreads `(This should` over 268 px, the reference over its 284), which is the `avail += hang_width` question from the previous note — settle it only once the split is in, with `--text` comparing the two lines.

- **Two hanging-punctuation measurements to start from (2026-11-05).** The `avail += hang_width` idea in `scan_line` (giving the line back the room the hanging glyph does not use, which is how the reference's `text-indent: -1em` spelling should agree) moved `hanging-punctuation-first.html`'s last block from seven glyphs to six against the reference's five — the wrong direction and unexplained — so it was reverted rather than kept as an unexplained change; instrument `used_w`/`lim` on that block before trying it again. And `hanging-punctuation-first-002.html` fails on both the pre-U+3000 and the current binary: it is `<div>　↓</div>` with `hanging-punctuation: first` against a reference `<div>↓</div>`, and its images render the arrows as the fallback font's box glyphs, so the first thing to check is whether the U+3000 is a separate leading piece at all (it has width, so `scan_line`'s `out.lead` does not skip it) — instrument the piece stream, not the paint.

- **`hanging-punctuation: first` landed 2026-11-05, and the reason no WPT file flips is the webfont.** The property is parsed (a keyword flags byte) and the first line's opening punctuation hangs one advance left of the content edge, taking no room in the line; the piece's box must have no border or padding on that side, and the hanging set is `( [ {`, U+2018, U+201C and U+3000. The vendored fixture `hanging-punct-first-001.html` fails on the previous binary and passes now, while `css/css-text` stays 766 of 1154 and `hanging-punctuation/` stays 2 of 21 — those pages take their font from `/fonts/ahem.css` (`@font-face` to a WOFF), the runner paints the reference blank (the pair shows up as a **blank pair**, i.e. skipped), and a blank reference cannot distinguish anything. `@font-face` is already collected by `sheet.e` and the runner's own Ahem-like TTF is the default, so the missing piece is **loading the WOFF** (`src: url(...)`, WOFF decompression, family registration). That single gap is also behind `text-transform` 17 (GentiumPlus WOFF via `@font-face`) and the `letter-spacing` 26 (Ahem), so it is worth more than any one suite: the reference images exist and compare wrongly only because nothing renders them. Until then, the reliable way to prove a text-layout change is a self-authored absolute-reference fixture with the runner's default font, as `hanging-punct-first-001.html` does.

- **Where the cheap work stopped (2026-11-05), and the next three items in order.** The browser stream is tracked in `docs/browser-progress.html` rows 9.2/9.3, not in `docs/work-queue.json` (that queue is the compiler/library stream). The last two rule-level items that a one-file experiment could settle are done (`dir=auto`, inline `overflow-wrap`); everything left in `css/css-text` is a small feature: `hanging-punctuation` 19 (the property is not parsed at all), `hyphens` 20 (dictionaries), `line-break` 19 (strict/loose tailoring of UAX#14), `word-space-transform` 18, `text-transform` 17 (Greek Extended case tables plus a WOFF webfont). `css/css-sizing`'s 127 are container interactions (aspect-ratio 51 split across flex/grid/floats/abspos), and `grid`'s misses are 564 grid-lanes. Next, each needing the image-diff loop for a whole round: (1) `hanging-punctuation: first` — parse the property and hang a leading opening punctuation one advance to the left of the content edge while the rest of the line starts at it, with `hanging-punctuation-first.html` pinning the `text-indent` and `border-left` interactions; (2) column spanners (`column-span: all`, 65 subtests, the largest named multicol family — a spanner splits the flow into segments that each balance); (3) the `dom/nodes` scrolling family, which needs a viewport model (`scrollend` on a virtual clock) and is the same ceiling as `html/interaction`. Every fixture for these must compare against an **absolute** reference: a relative one cannot tell "both ignored" from "both honoured" (`dir-auto-first-strong-rtl.html` and `overflow-wrap-inline-anywhere-001.html` both fail on the previous binary; the earlier relative direction fixtures were deleted for this reason).

- **Pull request** nebosa-company/neper#1626 (`browser-conformance` into `master`) is open. A review of it found 10 issues, all fixed in the commit after 5b1f39f74: `contain: inline-size` no longer reads as full size containment (whole keywords), `column-wrap` counts and applies columns with the same breaks (`colify_t` takes a `table` flag), the replaced min/max table needs a natural ratio, the app asks for a frame only when a timer is due (`timer_due`, `app.request_frame`) instead of every frame, filter-list arrays are sized by lines and every rule of a host is kept with its options (`typed` holds all rules; a request is blocked when any covers it), filtering applies to any request with a referrer or a destination, `--text` uses a string builder, the `viewBox` lookup is one helper, and the stale 128 MB comment is gone. Fixtures `net_fetch_filter` (new multi-rule case, typed count 2 became 3) and `css_layout` (case 136) cover them. Gate: CSS2 5167, grid 719, flexbox 634, writing-modes 625, sizing 328, multicol 182, overflow 274, lists 99.
- **Silent-exit bug, fixed:** the Windows app used to exit with code 0 about a second after load on a page without a `<title>` loaded from a long address (about 250 bytes or more; a data: URL does it). The page had nothing to do with it: the tab label is the address, and measuring that text in the tab strip took more than the app's 16 MB frame arena, so `lay_text` failed with `mem.Exhausted`, which `widget.measure` turned into `InvalidTree`, and the main loop treated any step error like a closed window. Now: `widget.measure` passes `mem.Exhausted` through (fixture `ui_widget` checks it), the app clips tab labels to 48 bytes (`clip_label`), shows an address over 400 bytes clipped in the field and navigates to the whole address on Enter while it is unedited (`omni_real`, `omni_clipped`; a typed or pasted address longer than 400 bytes is cut, a known ceiling), and the main loop says why on standard error and exits with status 14 (`report_stop`, `error_text`). The text layout's memory use for long texts (it looks quadratic in the length) is not fixed; the quadratic loop was not located. The field also draws a long address across the toolbar buttons.

- **Column spanners exist and are wrong in detail (corrected 2026-11-05).** The previous entry in this list claimed `column-span` appears nowhere: that was wrong, because the property is read as an *extra* string property (`is_spanner` calls `extra_text(st(style), "column-span")`), and `layout_multicol_spans` already segments the container's children into runs around spanners, lays each spanner out with `layout_block_child(..., cw, ...)` at the container's content width and stacks the runs by `colursor`. The first visible bug: in `multicol-span-all-005.html` the spanner content wraps to three lines at the column width while the reference has it on one line at the container width, so the re-layout at `cw` is not taking effect (or the narrow pass that runs first leaves the child's boxes at `cols.width`). Look there before touching the run segmentation, and read `--text` for the spanner's x/width rather than the whole page. **Measured 2026-11-05**: the spanner re-layout does get `cw=400` and the grid, flex, fieldset and details spanners come out `w=400`; the table spanner comes out `w=294` (its content wraps to three lines, so its intrinsic width is short). More importantly `--text` shows each spanner's text **twice** - once narrow from the measuring pass and once wide from the placement - so the measuring pass's fragments are not discarded. Truncating `l.frags` and `l.lines` to their lengths before the measuring pass drops the multicol suite from 182 to 176, because the first run's placement relies on that pass, so the reset has to happen at each run's and spanner's own re-layout instead of once at entry. Fix the duplicate first: it corrupts every spanner image. **Where the duplicate has to be fixed (2026-11-05)**: `colify_run`'s apply path only moves boxes and records column fragments, so a run's painted text comes from the measuring pass and nothing repaints it; that is why truncating at entry loses the runs. The spanner, by contrast, *is* re-laid out (`layout_block_child(..., cw, ...)`) and its new wide fragments are appended while the narrow ones stay in the middle of the array. The fix is therefore to lay a spanner out at the container width **inside the measuring pass** - give `layout_children` a per-child width override for spanner children - so no duplicate is ever created and the placement keeps moving boxes only. Removing fragments afterwards is not possible without an array edit, so do not try that again. **The duplicate was a misreading (2026-11-05)**: `multicol-span-all-005-ref.html` carries the same two `grid as a spanner` lines as the test, so the repeated text is two articles in the reference, not a double paint. The file's remaining difference is paint (outlines and the column rule), not text. A per-child span-width override in `layout_children` (`flow.span_width`, set by `layout_multicol_spans`, with the spanner placed by its own margins instead of a second `layout_block_child`) makes the test's `--text` match the reference exactly and leaves the suite at 182 of 402 - neutral, so it was not committed. Two conclusions: check the reference before calling a repeat a bug, and spanner work now has to be judged on images, not fragments. Grouped by name: 14 `*-children-height`, 3 nested, 3 balance-with-span, 3 fieldset, 2 abspos, 1 block-sibling, 1 column-rule, and about 38 of the plain `multicol-span-all-*` shape. Plan, in order: (1) parse `column-span: none | all` into a `Style` flag and expose it through the computed value (the `extras` list is the mechanism `hanging-punctuation` uses); (2) in the multicol layout, partition the container's children into segments at each `column-span: all` child, run the existing column layout per segment and stack the segments vertically, laying the spanner out at the container's full content width between them; (3) the 14 `children-height` cases then need the spanner's own height participating in the next segment's start position, and the balance/abspos cases need the segment boundary to act as their containing block. Verify with `--text` on `multicol-span-all-005.html` (three articles: table, grid and flex spanners) before touching balance. **Status 2026-11-05**: spanners are *implemented* (`is_spanner` reads `column-span` as an extra property, `layout_multicol_spans` runs and places them) and this file's `--text` matches its reference exactly once the spanner is laid out at the container width inside the measuring pass (`flow.span_width`) - but that change measured neutral at 182 of 402 and was not committed. `scripts/png-diff.ps1` on the pair shows **258 differing rows** with the first at (8,12) where we paint the spanner's blue outline and the reference paints the column's black one: the outlines are in different places, so this is a layout difference (the table spanner measures 294 px where the reference's single-column stack gives it 400) rather than paint. The next step is the spanner's own width, then re-running this diff - the row count makes it an easy pass/fail signal. **Looked at both images (2026-11-05) and the spanner is fine**: the spanners are placed at `x=0` with `cursor` 21-27 and widths 294 (table) and 400 (grid, flex, fieldset, details), which is what the reference's single-column stack gives them. The difference is **vertical**: the reference has a one-line gap between the five articles, from the stray `<br>` between them, and ours packs them together - a stray inline `<br>` between block siblings is not producing an anonymous line box. That is an inline-layout detail outside multicol and it will affect any test that separates blocks with a `<br>`; measure it with a three-block page before touching the spanner code again. **That conclusion is wrong (2026-11-05)**: two pages, `<div>a</div><br><div>b</div>` and `<div>a</div><div>b</div>`, render **differently** in this engine, so the stray `<br>` between block siblings *does* produce a line box. The vertical difference in `multicol-span-all-005` therefore comes from something else - most likely the articles' own heights or the `<br>` that follows a multicol container specifically - and must be measured with a `png-diff` of one article rather than assumed. **Sliced 2026-11-05 and the cause is the run's balanced height**: one article alone diffs at 46 rows and the spanner's blue outline sits at `y=13` where the reference has `block1` - because the spanner is placed at `cursor`, and `cursor` advanced only **5.33 px** for a one-line run balanced across three columns (`total / cols.count = 16 / 3`). A line box cannot be split across columns, so the balanced height has to be at least one line; clamping it to the tallest *`unbreakable`* child did nothing, because a one-line `div` counts as breakable. The clamp needs the tallest unsplittable unit (a line box or atomic content, not a splittable block), and the multicol suite stays 182 of 402 either way, so it has to be judged on the slice diff (46 rows) until it moves. **Tried the line-aware clamp 2026-11-05**: `colh = max(total / cols.count, tallest line of the run)` makes the one-article slice **pass** and the real `multicol-span-all-005` pair **pass**, and flips four files (`-005`, `-006`, `-008`, `no-balancing-after-column-span`) - but it breaks four others (`always-balancing-before-column-span`, `baseline-006`, `multicol-span-all-020`, `orthogonal-writing-mode-spanner`), so the suite stays at 182 of 402 and the change was reverted. The clamp is right in principle and too coarse in practice: it must apply only where a run really holds a single unsplittable line (or a single unbreakable child), not to every run - that is the condition to add before the next attempt, judged on the four files it breaks as well as the four it fixes. **Landed with that condition 2026-11-05**: only a run that is exactly one line tall gets the clamp, which gives **four fixed and zero broken** (multicol-span-all-005/-006/-008 and baseline-007) and moves css/css-multicol to **186 of 402**. The next family, `multicol-span-all-children-height-*` (14 files), was sliced: `-001` diffs at 66 rows with the spanner outline at `y=32` where the reference has it at ~58, i.e. its `height: 50%` children resolve too short. Resolving child percentages against the column height (`ch / column count`) moved the spanner from 23 to 32 and still failed, so the percentage base is only part of it; instrument `ch`, the computed column height and `block1`'s resolved height next, before changing anything else. The change was reverted to keep the suite at 186. **Instrumented 2026-11-05**: `ch=200` reaches the function, and with the base set to `ch / column count` the children resolve to `h=50` (from 16), which is right - but `multicol-span-all-children-height-001` still fails with the spanner outline at `y=32` while the reference has it at ~58, so the remaining gap is the **spanner's placement**, not the percentages: the segment before it is balanced to `column height / 2` while the reference stacks the spanner right after the first child. A two-column fixture with a `height: 50%` child and a fixed-height reference did not discriminate either way, so the change is neutral (multicol 186, corpus 186) and was reverted. Next: the segment's advance (`cursor`) for a definite-height child, instrumented with `cursor` and the run's `colh` on this file. **Both halves tried together and both are negative (2026-11-05)**: with the column-height base the children resolve to 50 and `RUN total=16 colh=16` becomes `total=50 colh=25`, and advancing a run that fits one column by its content height (`used_r == 0` -> `cursor += total`) costs one file on its own (186 to 185); applying both still leaves `children-height-001` failing and still costs one (185). So the reference's stack (block1 50, spanner 100, block2 50 in a *single-column* rendering with `height: 200`) is not what a two-column multicol with a spanner should produce, and this family is most likely another reference encoding like `first-and-last-together` and the ellipsis-alone block-ellipsis files. Stop here unless a browser comparison says otherwise; the multicol suite stands at 186 of 402 with the single-line clamp landed. Record of a wrong turn: four rounds of spanner work and one `<br>` theory have now been refuted on this file, and none of it was the spanner.

- **Suite sizes and current scores measured 2026-11-05** (smaller suites that are not in the tracker's list): `css/css-content` **5 of 14** - eight of its nine failures are `element-replacement-*`, the `content: url()`/element replacement feature, plus `pseudo-element-inline-box` and `quotes-first-line`; `css/css-values` **80 of 156**; `css/css-tables` **82 of 137** (unchanged from the tracker's number). `css-content` is small enough to finish in one round if element replacement is wanted, and it is a self-contained feature rather than a rule. **Scoped 2026-11-05**: `content` today is pseudo-element text only - `eval_content` (`layout.e:559`) tokenises the value and keeps the **strings**, dropping `url()` tokens, and `gen_pseudo` (`layout.e:694`) turns that text into text boxes for `::before`/`::after`; a regular element's `content` is not read at all, and `paint_replaced` draws a `.Replaced` box from `env.image(ctx, node)`, which resolves by node, not by a CSS value. Element replacement therefore needs three pieces: keep the `url()` token in `eval_content`, give the element a replaced box that skips its children in layout, and let the image lookup take the url (a per-box image source rather than the node's own). That is a feature round, not a rule fix; `pseudo-element-inline-box` is the same `content: url()` gap on a pseudo-element (a blue border must wrap the green image), and `quotes-first-line` needs `::first-line` colouring plus `q` quotes, so it belongs with the `::first-line` gap rather than here. **The `::first-line`/`::first-letter` cascade already works (2026-11-05)**: `PseudoElement` is `{ Before, After, Marker, FirstLetter, FirstLine, Placeholder, Selection, Backdrop }`, the selector parser accepts both (`selector.e:526`), and the style engine already runs `compute_for(el, want)` for every pseudo bit a sheet sets, storing the result in `Style.pseudo[8]` - so `pseudo[4]` is the computed first-line style and `pseudo[3]` the first-letter one. Only **consumption** is missing: the line builder gives every fragment its box's style. Do `::first-letter` **colour only** first (no metric change, and `block-ellipsis-019`/`-021` want exactly that, painted on the first fragment of line 0); **Measured and corrected 2026-11-05**: `::first-letter` **already worked** - a `<p>ab</p>` with `p::first-letter { color: #ff0000 }` against an all-black reference *fails* the comparison, so the pseudo style reaches the paint; the earlier line here claiming its consumption was missing was wrong. `::first-line` did not: a `p::first-line { color: #ff0000 }` page rendered identical to an all-black one. **Colour-only `::first-line` now landed** (`paint_text_run_color` takes the pseudo style and uses only its `color`, so metrics and line breaking are untouched; `paint_lines` passes `pseudo[4]` for `li == 1`), which takes `css/css-content` **5 to 6 of 14** (`quotes-first-line` flips), with `css/css-text` 776 of 1154 unchanged, `css/css-overflow` 318 of 501 and the corpus **187 to 188 of 209**. What is still missing is the *metrics* half - `font-size`/`font-weight` from `::first-line`, which needs a style variant for the pieces of line 0 in `layout_inline_content` because it changes wrapping. **css-text's biggest cluster measured 2026-11-05**: of its 378 failures the subdirectories are white-space **110**, word-break 38, letter-spacing 26, line-breaking 25, hyphens 20, text-autospace 20, line-break 19, word-space-transform 18, text-align 17, text-transform 17, overflow-wrap 15 and text-fit 10 - and almost all of `white-space` is **`white-space: break-spaces`**. The property is parsed (`WhiteSpace.BreakSpaces`) and used in the break-opportunity code, so the gap is narrower: `break-spaces-001` (`div { white-space: break-spaces; width: 4ch }` with `" XX  XX"`) paints `" XX  "` and `" XX"` where the reference paints `XX` and `XX`, i.e. we push a preserved space onto the next line while the reference keeps it on the previous one (for break-spaces a space is a break opportunity *after* it, so it hangs there and the next line starts at a non-space). `scan_line`'s leading-space skip only handles zero-width collapsed spaces (`p.w == 0 && !p.ink`), so preserved spaces are not covered; that is the shape to fix, and it is worth 110 files. **First attempt measured and reverted 2026-11-05**: widening the `hangs` rule at `layout.e:2429` to also cover `BreakSpaces` (so a preserved trailing space costs nothing) does move the space to the previous line - `break-spaces-001` goes from `" XX  "`/`" XX"` to `" XX   "`/`"XX"`, closer to the reference - but `css/css-text/white-space` drops from **210 to 176 of 320**, because `hangs` then applies to *every* space piece after content rather than only at a break, so lines pack more text than they should. The rule has to be in the break decision instead: when a break is taken and the piece before it is a preserved space, keep that space on the line (hanging) for `break-spaces`, and only there. Baseline for the next attempt: `css/css-text/white-space` **210 of 320**. **Instrumented 2026-11-05**: for `break-spaces-001`'s div (avail 80 = 4ch at 20px) the line scans start at pieces **0** and **2**, so the second line begins with the preserved-space piece and the wrapped text is `" XX  "` / `" XX"`. `collect_inline` already makes every preserved space end a piece (`bspace` in `cut_here`, layout.e:1916), and the piece push there passes `soft: false`, so the space never becomes the `last_soft` the break path uses (`out.end = last_soft + 1`); the break therefore falls at the piece boundary after the space and the space stays at the start of the next line. The next attempt should make a break-spaces space piece soft (or handle `bspace` in the break branch) rather than widen the hang rule - and it must be judged against the 210 of 320 baseline, since the wide version cost 34. **Second attempt measured and reverted 2026-11-05**: ending the line *at* the overflowing break-spaces space (`if pws.white_space == .BreakSpaces && p.soft && p.kind == .Text { out.end = j + 1; ret }` in the break branch) is the rule that makes `break-spaces-001` render `" XX   "` / `"XX"` - the second line correct - but it drops `css/css-text/white-space` to **133 of 320** (from 210): it fires for every overflowing break-spaces space, including the ones that legitimately start a new line, and an extra line box also survives (the test's frame is 40 rows tall where the reference is 0). The committed tree was re-gated today: corpus **189 of 211**, `css/css-content` **6 of 14**, `css/css-text` **781 of 1154** - so the handover's numbers and the tree agree. Also learned: `build/windows/patch.exe` applied only the **last** edit per file when a spec carried several `@@@` blocks for the same file - one edit per file per run works, so a multi-site edit is several runs or one block per file. **Landed 2026-11-05**: the fixture renders with `monospace` (the inline `Ahem` face was the blank-pair cause) and now discriminates - `abc<span>defghij</span>` in a 64 px box puts `defghij` at (48, 0) before the rule and at (0, 16) after, against a `<br>` reference. Gates: `css/css-text` **776 to 781 of 1154** (+5, the only mover), `white-space` 210 of 320 unchanged, `letter-spacing` 3 of 29 unchanged and the vendored corpus 189 of 211 with the new fixture. Note the trap that cost two rounds: a manifest written **outside** the reftest directory resolves its paths under that directory, so both pages paint nothing and the pair passes vacuously - always write the manifest next to the fixtures and check that `--text` shows fragments. **The url half is cheaper than it looked**: `env.url_image(ctx, url)` already exists (`paint.e:104`, used by background layers at `paint.e:2402`), so a pseudo-element with `content: url()` can paint through the same call, and the url needs no box field - it is in the box's style (`extra_text(s, "content")`). What layout must add is the pseudo box itself: an atomic/replaced box with the pseudo's border and background, its intrinsic size from the image, and no text children. Do that first (it may also close `element-replacement-image-*`), then the element-level `content` which additionally has to skip the children. **`<q>` quoted 2026-11-05**: the user-agent sheet had no `q::before`/`q::after` rules, so the quotes machinery (`open-quote`/`close-quote` in `eval_content`, with its default pairs) never ran for the element - every page with `<q>` rendered bare text. Adding `q::before { content: open-quote }` and `q::after { content: close-quote }` to **both** copies of the sheet in `ua.e` fixes it; `quotes-first-line` still needs `::first-line`, but the quotes themselves now render. css/css-text stays 776 of 1154, css/css-content 5 of 14 (its five blanks are the image cases), and the vendored corpus goes **186 to 187 of 208** with a `<q>t</q>` against `“t”` fixture that fails on the previous binary.
- **css-multicol (220 failing).** The clusters: `column-height` 24 (css-multicol-2: `column-wrap`, `column-height`, row gaps), nested multicol 21 (an inner container with `column-fill:auto` and a fixed height fragmenting across the outer's columns), fill-balance 17 (nested and flex-item multicols, a flex-stretched multicol needs a re-layout with the definite height), span-all 14+11+11, `multicol-columns`/`multicol-breaking` ~20. Fixing the nested and spanner cases needs **fragmentation-aware inner columns**, not another slice tweak.
- **`stretch` parses but is not the blocker (2026-11-05).** Adding a `Stretch` `LenKind` (`stretch`, `-webkit-fill-available`, `-moz-available`, `fill-available` -> the containing block, with the `len_text` case in `cssom.e` for the computed value) builds and leaves css/css-sizing at **330 of 457**, unchanged: the 13 failing `stretch` files are `abspos-*`, `replaced-aspect-ratio-*` and `bfc-next-to-float-*`, where the basis is not the normal containing block, and a float fixture with `width: stretch` did not discriminate, so the change was reverted. The keyword itself is small; the work is deciding the basis per formatting context. **Second attempt 2026-11-05**: rebuilding the kind and resolving it through `resolve_len` leaves css/css-sizing at 330 and `stretch/abspos-1.html` still failing, so an absolutely positioned box's width is not sized through `resolve_len` with the containing block's basis at all. Next: find the abspos sizing path (the callers of `is_abs_positioned` around line 746 and the block at 1985 only *collect* absolutes as pieces) and pass the containing block's padding-box width as the `stretch` basis there. Reverted again; the keyword is proven to compile and is not the hard part. **Corrected once more 2026-11-05**: `layout_abs` *does* resolve the width with the containing block's padding-box width (`resolve_len(s.width, cb_w)` at layout.e:9985), so a `Stretch` kind would resolve correctly there - `stretch/abspos-1.html` fails for a different reason, most likely that its `#reference-overlapped-red` needs `z-index: -1` stacking (the green box must paint over the red one) rather than the width. So the `stretch` group is not a basis problem after all: check the stacking context before touching `resolve_len` again, and land the `Stretch` kind only together with a file that actually flips.
- **css-sizing (129 failing):** aspect-ratio 53, contain-intrinsic-size (9, section 2), intrinsic-percent 14, stretch 13, replaced-aspect 5, float-clearance 4, fit-content 4, box-sizing-replaced 3 (one image is 9 px too wide; per-image sizes not traced), svg-intrinsic 3, range-percent 3. `block-aspect-ratio-with-margin-collapsing-002` is unresolved.
- **css-flexbox 274, css-grid 826, css-writing-modes 373, CSS2 677** still failing; the 2026-10-20 cluster notes (grid-lanes, subgrid unimplemented, baseline alignment, bidi) still describe them. css-overflow: line-clamp 113, scroll-markers 44, text-overflow-string 21.
- **The browser as a product:** page scripts, timers and clicks work on small pages and on `rules.html`; **real-site behaviour is unmeasured** (jQuery 3.5.1 init, AJAX re-render, iframes, `getBoundingClientRect` feedback), as is performance and memory. Form controls, text input, selection, favicons and downloads are not checked. Broken images draw their alt text but not Chrome's broken-image icon.
- **Web target:** networking, and scripts/clicks/scroll unverified; fonts are Jost only.
- **Fixed 2026-11-04:** the `x509.verify` out-of-memory mislabel. `sign.rsa_pkcs1v15_verify_hash_e` reports `InvalidSignature` for a signature that does not verify and keeps a memory error as itself; `x509.verify` returns that error instead of `UnknownAuthority`, `tls` passes it on, fetch and the app's error text do too. `crypto_sign_wide` checks all three layers under a 64-byte arena. **Not done:** queue evidence for L166-L171.
