## 8. Rules of the repo and the user

- A fix is done with a regression test that fails if the behaviour breaks; the browser branch has leaned on the WPT sweeps instead of the `css_layout` fixture cases (`tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/css_layout`, next free case was 106 on 2026-10-20). Adding fixture cases is owed.
- Minimal comments; only non-derivable why or constraints. No Python patch scripts; use `patch.exe` or the Edit tool.
- Commit only touched paths; never `git add -A`. Fetch before pushing.
- Show the user UI results as screenshots (`SendUserFile`); only of the app window. Recommend, then wait for "lock it in" on decisions.
- End substantive replies with **Summary:** and **Next step:** (the user's global `CLAUDE.md`).

## 9. The script-side WPT stream (DOM and web APIs)

Written 2026-11-03 by the script-side WPT session. The numbers live in `docs/browser-progress.html`, row 9.3 (script-side WPT) and row 9.2 (CSS reftests); this section holds what the numbers do not say: how the runs work, the traps, and what is left.

### The control surface (`e.ui.browser`, tracker 7.1/7.2)

`lib/e/ui/browser.e` is one browsing context as a control: `attach`, `navigate`, `back`, `forward`, `reload`, `state`, `pump`, `timer_due`, `canvas`, `region`, `keys`, `a11y_tree`, `semantics`, `set_preferences`, `ensure_renderer`. Chrome (tabs, omnibox, bookmarks, downloads) is deliberately not there.

**Driving the page (2026-11-07):** the control has a full automation surface now, all of it wrapping `page`:
- `scriptable(b)` - is there a realm to drive.
- `eval_js(b, source)` - run a script in the page's own realm and run the jobs it queued; false when it threw. Takes the same path as the page's own `<script>` elements, so **CSP `script-src` applies exactly as it does to the page**.
- `eval_value(b, source)` - run an *expression* and answer `String(result)`, plus whether it succeeded. This is the read half: `(title, usable) = eval_value(b, "document.title")`.
- `eval_error(b)` - the exception the last eval threw.
- `eval_truthy(b, source)` - poll an expression.
- `eval_number(b, source)` - the number an expression evaluates to, plus whether it was one.
- `query(b, selector)` - the first match's text. `query_attr(b, selector, name)` - one of its attributes. Both answer `usable = false` on a miss rather than the string `"null"`. The selector and attribute name are injected through `js_quote` (backslash, quote and line terminators escaped), so a selector containing a quote cannot break out of the literal.
- `wait_for(b, source, start_ms, timeout_ms)` - drive the page's event loop until the condition holds. Steps to `page.next_wakeup` rather than a fixed tick, so a page waiting on `setTimeout` or a promise chain is driven rather than starved; **tracks the page's address across each step, so a navigation the page asked for is followed and the condition is re-evaluated in the new realm** (2026-11-07), with the deadline still bounding a redirect loop.

Fixture: `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/browser_eval` loads a real page with `page.load` and drives it through the same `page` entries: realm reuse across calls, a DOM write read back, `textContent` and `1 + 41` returned as strings and `querySelectorAll('button').length` as the number 1.0, `query`/`query_attr` on a `button.primary` including an attribute whose value contains an apostrophe and a selector containing a quote that matches nothing, a throw reported with its message, and a `setTimeout` driven to completion. **It is a verified test in both directions**: stubbing `page.tick` fails with `WaitFailed`, and dropping the null-to-miss conversion fails with `QueryWrong`.

- **Trap (resolved, 2026-11-07):** `page.load` on a `data:` URL *does* work headless, but only once a face is registered **and the generics are set** (`fontset.add` then `fontset.set_generic` for `sans-serif`/`serif`). An empty `Set` has no fallback family and the load answers `LoadFailed`, which reads like a fetch or URL failure and is neither. Copy the four lines from the fixture.
- **The "wait for the network to go quiet" gap was WRONG, and this entry corrects it (2026-11-07).** I had written that a host needs a pending-request count on `fetch.Client` to know when network work had settled. Reading the code: **there is no asynchronous network work to wait for.** `page.image_entry` (page.e:399) calls `fetch.fetch` synchronously and only returns once the image has loaded or failed, and the script-side `fetch` in `script.e` (`n_fetch_text`) does the same - `h.fetch_fn` blocks and hands back the text. So by the time `page.load` returns, or `eval_js` returns after a script called `fetch`, the network is already quiet. Do **not** add a pending-request counter to `fetch.Client` for this: there is nothing in flight to count, and `fetch.e`'s `Client` currently carries another session's uncommitted fields, so touching it would also collide. If this engine ever gains real async fetches, the counter becomes a real need - that is the condition to revisit.
- **Still missing for a first-class control (2026-11-07):** nothing on the automation path that the browser's own model holds back. What is left is genuinely optional: a `wait_for_selector` shim (today `wait_for(b, "document.querySelector('x') !== null", ...)`), reading computed style in one call (today `eval_value(b, "getComputedStyle(e).color")`), and a typed boolean `eval_bool`. The control surface is complete for driving a page: navigate, evaluate, query (one/many/attribute), write a value, wait for a condition, and read the rendered result through `canvas`/`region`.


### Where the code is

| Area | File |
|---|---|
| DOM/web API prelude (JS embedded as `try str.push(b, "...\n")` lines) | `lib/e/web/dom_js.e` |
| Native glue the prelude calls (`N.*`) | `lib/e/web/script.e` |
| Native DOM tree, selectors, serializer | `lib/e/fmt/dom.e`, `lib/e/fmt/html.e` |
| Script-side WPT runner | `tests/browser/web/run.e`, `tests/browser/web/run.sh` |
| CSS reftest runner | `tests/browser/wpt/run.e` |

The prelude is JavaScript inside Neper strings. Edit it with a Node script that writes each JS line as `try str.push(b, "<line>\n")`, doubling backslashes and quotes with `String.fromCharCode`. Inline `\\t`-style escapes in shell, heredoc or Edit calls get mangled. A Node script written with the Write tool is the reliable way.

### Running the script-side WPT

Clone: `D:/data/wpt-script` (sparse; `git sparse-checkout add <dir>` to add one). Lists of test paths are plain text, one per line, relative to the clone.

```bash
cd /d/repos/neper
build/windows/neper-try.exe emit-executable tests/browser/web/run.e . x64 windows build/windows/web-run-js.exe --arena 6g
export WEB_RUN_EXE=$PWD/build/windows/web-run-js.exe     # same shell call as run.sh
bash tests/browser/web/run.sh --root /d/data/wpt-script --out /d/Temp/t5/OUT --jobs 2 --chunk 8 LIST
```

Prints `FILE path pass/total` per file. The per-chunk raw output stays in `--out`; `grep FAIL` there for the reasons.

Traps, all hit this session:

- A script-side DOM fix needs a regression fixture, and the home for one is `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/web_shell/src/main.e`: `frame_registry_is_live` (exit 86) and `dom_mixins` (exit 87) each build a context, run one script and throw on failure. Extend `dom_mixins` rather than adding a fixture directory (the link fixtures are enumerated by the generated `tests/selfhost/run.ps1`).
- `WEB_RUN_EXE` must be exported in the same shell call as `run.sh`. A fresh shell falls back to the stale default `web-run.exe`, and the result looks like a regression or a missing feature.
- Rebuild into `web-run-js.exe`; for a long comparison copy it to `web-run-cmp.exe` and run that, so rebuilding does not hit `os.Denied`. Kill stray `web-run-*.exe` and `xargs.exe` first.
- `--jobs 2`. More processes exhaust memory and fork.
- A file that ends `(no result)` at about 4855MB hit the interpreter memory limit (`it.mem_limit` in `run.e`, 5 GB). Its count is partial; the large reflection, Range and iframe-heavy files do this.
- Background runs die with the session. Restart them.
- Compare per file against the last full run, never against a partial one; do not overwrite the baseline from an interrupted run.

Batch cadence (adopted 2026-11-04, the work is faster and no less safe this way):

- Three to five related fixes per commit, not one commit per fix.
- Test in three tiers: the one to three test files being edited after every edit (seconds), the area's slice list (100-160 files, 5-15 min) at the end of the batch, and the full suite of the touched area only at a batch boundary or once a day.
- `scripts/wpt-gate.ps1 -Exe <runner> -Root <corpus> -List <list> [-Baseline <tsv>] [-Update]` writes the per-file `path<TAB>passed<TAB>total` results, prints every DROP / GAIN / NEW / MISSING against a baseline, and exits 1 on a drop. First use of a list writes the baseline with `-Update`; after that the gate is one line and the "no drops" rule is machine checked.
- Keep the A/B for a suite that shares the changed code path even inside a batch: the three worst outcomes of the 2026-11-04 session (ranges 113 to 1, enumerated ARIA 1731 to 953, insert-path coercion) were all in a code path the obvious test file did not exercise.

Where the next CSS text batch should look (measured 2026-11-04, `css/css-text` 756/1154):

- `letter-spacing` (26 of 29 fail). Rendering `letter-spacing-200.html` against its reference shows the two glyphs of `1 2` on one line in a 100px float where the reference wraps after the first: our fitting scan accepts a line whose width, with letter-spacing, exceeds the available width. The line's *used* width already excludes a trailing `p.trail` correctly (`used_w` at the end of the line), so the bug is in the fitting scan that decides what fits, not in the trail bookkeeping — instrument `avail` against `used_w` for that file first. Making a trailing ideographic space hang or subtracting the last character's letter-spacing were both tried and moved the suite by zero, so do not start from those. A third attempt (adding the last character's letter spacing to the piece's `trail`, in both the tab-split and the main text paths, so that it counts while fitting and hangs in the used width) also moved it by zero: `hang_tail_len` in `layout.e` already implements the trailing-space hang including U+3000/U+1680/U+2000-U+200A/U+205F, so that ground is covered. **Instrumented 2026-11-04**: printing `pos`, `needed`, `p.w`, `p.trail`, `lim` at the point the scan rejects a piece shows the scan behaving correctly for this file — for `1 2` it breaks before `2` (`pos=80 needed=40 lim=100`), so the fitting decision is *not* where these tests differ. The difference is downstream of the break (line placement or painting inside the float, and how many lines a fixture ends up with). Instrument the line placement (`x`, `y`, and the line count per float) next; do not touch the fitting scan or the trail again.
- **A relative reftest cannot prove a direction rule works.** A local fixture that compares `<div dir=rtl>` against `<div style="direction:rtl">` passes whether or not `dir` reaches layout, because both sides are wrong in the same way; that is why the `[dir]` rules added to `ua.css` could not be A/B'd (`text-align` 47/70 and `writing-modes` 634/995 identical with and without them) and were reverted rather than kept unproven. A direction fixture needs an absolute reference (`text-align: left` in the ref, or a fixed marker) so "both ignored" and "both honoured" render differently. `dir=auto` (first strong character decides, which `text-align-end-009`/`-010` need) is implemented nowhere — check whether `dir=ltr/rtl` reaches layout at all first, with an absolute reference.
- **The "last selected option wins" rule cannot be added to `optionState` in isolation.** Deselecting the earlier options when a selected one initialises gains `inserted-or-removed` 1/5 to 3/5 and `select-selectedOptions` 8 to 9, but costs `customizable-select/option-selectedness-wrapper` 9 to 4, because select options can sit inside wrapper elements there and `selectOptions` must list them in tree order (including through the wrappers) before the eager rule is correct. Net −2, reverted. Fix `selectOptions` first, then the rule.
- **Appending an `html` element to a `new Document()` overflows the stack.** Minimal reproduction (2026-11-05): `new Document().appendChild(document.createElement('html'))` throws `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`, while the same call with a `div` works, and `appendChild(div)` plus `cloneNode(true)` of the real `documentElement` each work on their own. It blocks the `In new Document()` subtest of both `dom/events/Event-dispatch-bubbles-{false,true}` and shows up there as the misleading `Cannot set properties of undefined (setting 'status')` because the harness is unwinding from the overflow. Bisect inside `preInsert`/`elementInserted` for the `html` local name: the recursion is specific to that element in a document parent, not to insertion into a document in general. **Narrowed 2026-11-05**: an *empty* `html` element appends fine (`new Document().appendChild(document.createElement('html'))` in isolation passes), and so does `html` with a `body`; the overflow needs a cloned `documentElement`, i.e. a subtree that carries the page's `<script>` elements. Making `prepareScript` return early for a document with no browsing context (the HTML "not fully active → inert script" rule) looked right but dropped both bubbles files from 61 subtests to 5, so page scripts are running from a document whose `browsingContext` is false in this runner and the guard cannot key on it. **Also tried and rejected**: setting the clone's `alreadyStarted` flag (the standard's cloning steps) — in `cloneNode` itself, and again only in the `Node.prototype.cloneNode` user-facing path via a `markClonedScripts` walk. Both changed the two files from 61 subtests to 5 in exactly the same way, so something in the runner's page setup clones the tree through the user-facing API and needs those scripts startable; find that call (grep `cloneNode(` in `run.e` and `script.new_context`) before trying a third variant. Bisect the script-insertion path instead (`prepareScript` → `runParsedScripts` → the clone's document.write), or make the clone's scripts already-started *only* when the clone is not the document being loaded.
- **A tree-order `selectOptions` walk needs a cache first.** Walking all descendants (skipping nested selects) instead of one level of `optgroup` gains `customizable-select/option-selectedness-wrapper` 9 to 16, `select-value` 3 to 4 and `placeholder-label-option-wrapper` 1 to 2 — but the select-element directory then dies partway with `lib/e/js/interp.e:6608: trap[null]: nil dereferenced as *Program` (158 files never run), because the walk is O(n²) when a test has many options and the interpreter gives up mid-test. Cache the collected list per select (invalidated on child mutation) before landing it, and fix the interpreter's nil `*Program` trap on the step-limit path as a separate engine bug.
- **That interpreter crash is a stale frame program, not a null one, and it reproduces without any DOM change.** `html/semantics/forms/the-select-element` (375 files) aborts intermittently on the plain build too, at `exec_body`'s `f.prog.nodes[...]`. Guards for `f.prog == zero_prog()` do not fire: the pointer is non-null garbage, so the frame outlives the `Program` it points at. The two likely owners are the script runner, which resets the arena between files while a pending promise/async continuation may still hold a frame, and `make_suspendable`, which stores a copied `Frame` in `GenState`. Next step: either drain or abandon pending async work before `mem.reset(a, work)` in `run.e`, or keep each test's `Program` alive for the lifetime of its realm — reproduce by running the select-element list twice and watching whether the abort moves.
- **The select-element abort is cumulative, not a bad file.** The list split in half runs clean (188 files 208 subtests, 187 files 266 subtests, no abort); the full 375-file list aborts while starting `customizable-select/select-events-2.optional.html`, and that file alone runs fine (0/5, 123 MB). So something survives the per-file `mem.reset` and goes stale only after enough files — pending generator/async state being the prime suspect, since the abort is inside `gen_thread_main`. Bisect by halves (about nine runs) to find the minimal pair of files that reproduces it, then print the interpreter and `Program` addresses at the crash.
- **Bisected 2026-11-05 to a two-chunk reproduction.** With the 375-file list split `A` (first 188) + `B` (second 187), `A1`/`A2` and `B1`/`B2` being their halves: `A` clean, `B` clean, `B2` alone (93 files) clean, `A + B1` (282) clean, `A1 + B2` (187) clean, **`A2 + B2` (187 files) aborts**, and `A + B2` (281) aborts with a different fault address. So the state comes from a file in `A2` and is tripped by a file in `B2`, and neither half is a bad file on its own — a cross-file resource that per-file `mem.reset` does not clear. Next: bisect `A2` and `B2` separately (log2 of 94 ≈ 7 runs each) to single files, which will name the object that leaks.
- **The bisect resolved to a threshold, not a pair of files (2026-11-05).** Continuing from the above: `A2 + B2a` (140 files, first half of `B2`) aborts, but `A2 + B2a1` (117 files) and `A2 + B2a2` (117 files) are both clean. There is no minimum pair of files — the abort needs *enough* of both chunks, with the threshold between 117 and 140 files for this mixture. That is cumulative resource exhaustion, not a poisoned file: something grows per file and is not released by `mem.reset(a, work)` (the arena's per-file mark), and once it is big enough a frame's `Program` pointer reads garbage. Next: print the process working set and the arena's `mem.mark` at the top of each file in `run.e` to see what grows (the per-file "MB" in the FILE line measures only the current mark, not the total), then look for a global the runner never clears between files.
- **Workaround until that is fixed: let the gate chunk the list.** `scripts/wpt-gate.ps1` runs a list in separate processes of `-Chunk` files (default **40**), merges the per-file results, and prints `CHUNK ABORTED files a-b (exit N)` plus `(N chunk(s) aborted)` in the summary when a chunk dies — so a short total is visible instead of silent. Measured on the select-element list: chunk 100 aborts one chunk (335 of 375 files seen), chunk 40 runs all 375 files, 474 subtests, 148 pass. Use the gate (not a bare `web-run` invocation) for anything over ~40 files until the leak is fixed.
- **`select[i]` does not exist.** Elements are plain objects, so a select's indexed properties (`selly[i]` in `common-HTMLOptionsCollection*.html`) are undefined and four subtests there die with "Cannot read properties of undefined". Making a select indexed needs the element wrapper to be a legacy object like the collections are, i.e. a change in element creation, not in the select code.
- Windows, bash and Node see different paths for temp files; keep working files under `D:/Temp/t5` and pass `D:/` style paths.

### State at the end of this stretch

Covered and compared against earlier runs (lists: DOM, custom-elements, shadow-dom, domparsing = 1152 files; `html/dom` = 229 files; `html/semantics/forms` = 514 files; sanitizer-api = 48 files). The last comparisons showed no real regressions; the losses were large files at the memory limit.

Implemented this stretch: Sanitizer API and the `setHTML` family, streaming HTML methods, scoped custom element registries, form-associated custom elements and `ElementInternals`, `Node.moveBefore`, `dataset`, attr-associated ARIA element references, enumerated ARIA reflection, `document.characterSet` from the meta charset, protocol handler validation, history URL rewriting rules, plus many reflection fixes.

### Open gaps

| Gap | Why it is left |
|---|---|
| Trusted Types (`trustedTypes`, `TrustedHTML`, the `createParserOptions` extension) | About a dozen sanitizer-api files report 0/0. Needs CSP enforcement on every sink and the `.headers` files, which the runner does not read. |
| Scoped registries, remaining cases | About 50 subtests: cross-realm nodes passed to a registry, define-upgrade order across documents, a few `initialize` cases. |
| `document` named properties (`document.test1`) | Needs the document object itself to be a Proxy. |
| SVG: `SVGElement` interface, animation (`SVGAnimatedString`, `beginEvent`) | No `SVGElement` exists, so SVG has no `.dataset` and `sanitizer-svg-animate` cannot pass. |
| `setFormValue` submission tests | Need form navigation into an iframe. |
| `OpaqueRange` (tentative) | Unimplemented API, needs layout geometry. |
| `moveBefore` state preservation | Focus, selection and running CSS animations are lost; it is a remove plus insert. |
| Tests needing `testdriver` actions | `get_computed_role`, `action_sequence`, user gestures. Cannot run headless. |
| `html/browsers/history` | Scroll position and cross-origin location tests need layout and real iframes. |
| innerText getter | Needs computed styles the headless runner does not have. |
| Encoding tests needing server headers | Charset set by `Content-Type`; the runner only has meta charset and the `encoding.py` shim. |
| `processing-instruction-attributes` (30 subtests) | The prelude has the ProcessingInstruction attribute API, but the test wants `<?...?>` in HTML to become a PI node; the HTML parser makes it a bogus comment (current spec) and `html5lib` (1791/1791) pins that. It is a WICG proposal. |
| Cross-realm DOM objects (`Range-deleteContents`/`-cloneContents`/`-extractContents`, `TreeWalker-*-cross-realm`, `create-element-realm-after-adoption`, `EventListener-handleEvent-cross-realm`: ~39 subtests) | A Node method called on a wrapper from another iframe realm brand-checks with the *caller's* slot map, and `N.realmOf` compares against the active interpreter rather than the function's realm, so the foreign wrapper is not recognised (`parameter 1 is not of type 'Node'`). Iframes do get their own interpreter (`N.frameCreate`), so the fix is a realm-aware slot lookup plus returning wrappers in the node document's realm — not per-test patches. **Measured 2026-11-05**: `dom/nodes` overall runs 190 of 230 files with **9508 of 9642 subtests passing (98.6%)** (40 files lost to the 7-minute chunk timeout - the runner leaks, so use a smaller chunk), and its largest gaps are processing-instruction-attributes (30 missing), Document-createEvent.https (15), namespaced-attribute-mutations (12) and node-creation-realm (12). **`Document-createEvent.https` closed 2026-11-05**: the five interfaces it names - `DragEvent`, `TouchEvent`, `TextEvent`, `DeviceMotionEvent`, `DeviceOrientationEvent` - were missing as globals and `createEvent` mapped their names to `MouseEvent`/`UIEvent`/`CompositionEvent`/`Event`; defining them with `defineEventClass` (DragEvent and TouchEvent on MouseEvent, the rest on Event) and mapping each case to its own class takes the file from **264 to 279 of 279**. `dom/events` is 618 of 710 on both binaries (no drops), a 20-file `dom/nodes` chunk is 669 of 677, and the `web_shell` fixture gained an `event_aliases` check (exit 88). `processing-instruction-attributes` (30) is **not** a target: it tests the WICG declarative-partial-updates proposal that gives ProcessingInstruction attribute methods, which is in no shipped spec. **`Element-matches-disabled-pseudo-class` closed 2026-11-05**: `:disabled` only looked at the element's own `disabled` attribute, so controls inside a `<fieldset disabled>` were reported enabled. `in_disabled_fieldset` now walks the ancestors (any disabled fieldset disables, except inside that fieldset's **first** `legend` child, which `in_first_legend` finds) and `:enabled` excludes the same set; the file goes **3 to 11 of 11** and `querySelectorAll(':disabled')` finds the fieldset itself and its control. `css/css-display` is 93 of 115 on both the old and the new binary and the vendored corpus 186 of 207, so no drops. `Element-webkitMatchesSelector` **665 of 669** after the legacy alias started passing its arguments through (`webkitMatchesSelector(...args)` with `apply`), so calling it with none throws the TypeError the three "no parameter" subtests expect; the four left need `:target` (2) and the old unclosed-attribute-selector behaviour (2). `namespaced-attribute-mutations` (12) is the next item and needs a namespace-capable attribute record: `html.Attribute.namespace` is an enum of the known namespaces, so `setAttributeNS('urn:x', 'style', ...)` cannot be told apart from the HTML `style`, so the realm work is not the only item in the directory. The eight `dom/nodes/*realm*` files stand at **5 of 40 subtests** and the failures split in two. (1) *Illegal invocation* when a method is borrowed across realms (`inner.Document.prototype.createTextNode.call(doc, ...)`, `attachShadow.call(...)`): `slotOf` reads a per-realm `WeakMap`, so a foreign node has no slot here. (2) The adoption preconditions fail - `node document and relevant realm differ after adoption` - because we retarget a node's realm on adoption while the spec keeps the *creation* realm. Adding a `foreignSlotOf` fallback to `slotOf` dropped the files from 5 to **3 of 40** and was reverted: the brand check needs `asNodeArg`-style wrapper conversion per method, not a global fallback, and the adoption retargeting must stop first - it is one line in the adoption path and the tests' own preconditions name it. **The reference test's mechanism, read 2026-11-05**: `node-realm-preserved-across-adoption` builds `inner.document.createElement('div')`, sets `innerHTML = '<p></p>'` so the `<p>` exists **only in the inner document and has no wrapper yet**, adopts the container into the top-level document with `appendChild`, and then requires `p.ownerDocument === document` **and** `p instanceof inner.HTMLParagraphElement`. Two things must therefore both hold: every realm exposes the full interface constructor set, and the wrapper first materialised after adoption is created in the node's *creation* realm. Which of the two we lack is the next measurement - a probe belongs in `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/web_shell` because the corpus tree is read-only - and the realm work itself is multi-round; start it fresh rather than at the end of a session. **Probed 2026-11-05 and the split is decided**: a two-subtest page (iframe, then `typeof inner.HTMLParagraphElement`) gives **1 of 2** - *every realm exposes the interface constructors* **passes**, and *an adopted node keeps its inner realm* (`p instanceof inner.HTMLParagraphElement`) **fails**. So requirement (a) is already met and only (b) is missing: `wrap(id)` builds the wrapper from the **current** realm's prototype chain and records it in the current realm's `wrappers` map. The fix is to record each node's creation realm in a page-global table keyed by node id (reachable from every realm, like `N`), and have `wrap(id)` delegate to that realm's `wrap` when it differs - then `instanceof inner[X]`, the borrowed-method brand checks and the adoption preconditions should all follow from one change. The probe root is a junction to `D:/data/wpt-script/resources` (the corpus is read-only), which is the pattern to use for script probes. **Why the table alone is not enough (2026-11-05)**: `wrap(id)` walks the node's ancestor chain to find its document, which gives the **current** document, not the creation one - for the probe's adopted `<p>` the chain ends at the top document, so the wrapper is made in the top realm even though the node was created in the inner one. The creation document has to come from the native node record (`host.d.nodes[id]`, the array `n_kind` reads at `script.e`'s `n_kind`), so the next step is a small `n_doc_of(id)` native getter plus, in `wrap`, a lookup for the realm that wraps that document id (every realm's `wrappers` map is reachable through `frameWindowsAll`) and a delegation to it. `adopt` itself is correct - it only rewrites `slot.doc` and never a realm. **And `html.Node` has no document field at all (2026-11-05)**, so the getter cannot simply expose one: `type Node = struct { kind, namespace, name, value, attributes, parent, first_child, last_child, previous_sibling, next_sibling, flags }` (`lib/e/fmt/html.e`). The spec's creation realm therefore needs a **new field on the node record threaded through the parser, fragment parsing, template contents and every DOM operation that builds nodes**, plus the JS side reading it in `wrap`. That is a structural change of several days, not the one getter the earlier note assumed - records of it: the ancestors-walk cannot stand in, because after adoption the nearest wrapped ancestor belongs to the *adopted* realm while the spec wants the creation one. Plan it as its own piece of work; the eight realm files stay at 5 of 40 until then. |
| `html/dom/partial-updates/tentative/*` (~14 subtests) | The declarative partial updates proposal (`stream-append`, `template for`, `fragment/buffer-*`) is not implemented at all; it is a WICG draft, not a browser feature. |
| innerText `::first-line` / `::first-letter` (7 of `getter.html`) | The rendered-text steps need the computed style of a pseudo-element and the script runner has no stylesheet resolution (`getComputedStyle` answers only from the element's own style); this needs computed styles in the runner before it is worth doing. |

### Known deliberate trade-offs

- The runner parses the whole document before any script runs, so a custom element in the page is upgraded after parsing, with its attributes already present. `custom-elements/parser/parser-sets-attributes-*` loses subtests because of this.
- `aria-attribute-reflection.html` expects string semantics; `aria-attribute-reflection-enumerated.tentative.html` expects enumerated. The enumerated behaviour is implemented (it gains about 1,100 subtests; the string file loses 13).
- Every element now carries a `registry` slot. `ElementInternals-target-element-is-held-strongly.html` (100,000 elements) sits at the memory limit because of it.

- **`flex-wrap: balance` block, six rounds, no landing (2026-11-05).** The balance loop works (`layout.e:6735`); the family stands at **8 of 32** and `balance-001` passes. `balance-002` (`flex-direction: column`, `height: 100%`, `width: max-content` on the body) differs because the body's max-content width is 51 (one column) against the reference's 60 (two). Both candidate fixes are measured neutrally: resolving the percentage height against the parent (`fdef_h` in `flex_widths`) and replacing `mx = maxf(mx, cols_w)` with `mx = cols_w`. The traces show the branch running with `def_h=100` for box 9, so either the value is not the one the container's max-content uses or box 9 is not `balance-002`'s flex. Next: print the **page name** beside the values (`p.l.url` or the manifest line) so the trace can be tied to the file before any more code changes. **And the trace is now tied to the file (2026-11-05), which corrects six rounds of readings**: with `--text` the runner prints each page's name, so the interleaved output shows `CWFMARK b=3` falling **between `balance-002.html` and `balance-002-ref.html`** - the flex in `balance-002` is **box 3**, not box 9; every `b=9 def_h=100` line belonged to the `balance-available-size-*` files. For box 3 the resolution reads `def_h=0`, because at the moment `flex_widths` runs the **parent box's height is still 0** (the body has not been laid out). So the cheap route cannot work by construction, and the option recorded in round 203 is the real fix: **thread the containing block's height into `content_widths`/`flex_widths`** from the block layout that knows it, rather than reading a parent box. That conclusion is now measured on the right box. **The threading was implemented and is also neutral (2026-11-05)**: adding `Layout.cur_cbh`, setting it in `layout_children` (where `ch` is known) and resolving the column flex's percentage height against it compiles and runs, and `css/css-flexbox/balance` still stands at **8 of 32**. So the column branch's value is either still not the one the container's `max-content` uses, or `balance-002`'s body width does not come from that branch at all. Nine rounds on this family with two neutral implementations is the signal to stop: the next investigation should start from the **reference image** (what the body's 60 px consists of) and the runner's own `TEXT`/`--out` output for that one file, not from another fix attempt. The threading change was reverted. **And the reference read (2026-11-05) fixes the expected numbers**: `balance-002-ref.html` uses `flex-wrap: wrap` with `height: 60px` on the flex - four 25 px items and a 10 px gap, i.e. **two columns of two** - so the body's `max-content` is 60 + padding 20 + border 6 = **86**, exactly the reference's ink, while ours is 51 = one 25 px column + 20 + 6. The test's own flex is `flex-wrap: balance` at `height: 100%` (120 px), which the balance algorithm must resolve to the same 2+2 split. So the expectation is not odd: our intrinsic width ignores the wrap somewhere, and with the threading neutral the suspect list is down to **where the body's width is decided** rather than where the flex's intrinsic width is computed. That is the next thing to trace, not another fix attempt.

- **`break-spaces` fourth shape, refuted (2026-11-05).** Hanging a `break-spaces` space only when it is the character that would overflow (`pos + needed > lim` inside the `hangs` block) drops `css/css-text/white-space` from **210 to 128 of 320** - far worse than the naive hang (176) or the break-branch rule (133). Four shapes have now been measured and refuted, and each is recorded with its number; the family needs the piece-loop redesign (a space that is both break opportunity and trailing hang) rather than a condition in `scan_line`. Reverted; baseline re-measured at **210 of 320**.

- **`css/css-grid` surveyed (2026-11-05, 793 of 1545 failing).** Families: **grid-lanes 564**, subgrid 60, alignment 57, abspos 48, grid-items 30, grid-model 13, grid-definition 9, layout-algorithm 6, placement 3. The suite is dominated by `grid-lanes` (the masonry proposal, CSS Grid Level 3) - 71% of its failures are one unimplemented feature, so the suite's score will not move without it. The contained families worth a round each are **alignment 57**, **abspos 48** and **grid-items 30**, in that order; `subgrid` (60) is a real feature too but a larger one. Take `alignment` first: it is grid's align-items/justify-items/self and content distribution, which is the same machinery as flexbox's alignment (5 failing there), so one investigation may serve both.

- **Grid alignment is one cluster: baseline (2026-11-05).** `css/css-grid/alignment` is **94 of 151**, and of its 57 failures **47 are `baseline`** (`grid-baseline-align-*`, `grid-baseline-justify-*`, `grid-item-content-baseline-*`, `grid-baseline-align-cycles-*`), with distribution 1, gutters 2 and other 7. So the family is grid's **first-baseline block-axis alignment**: items of different font sizes in one row share a first baseline, which the engine already computes for flex (`has_baseline` on a box) but does not apply per grid row. That is a contained piece of the grid layout algorithm - set the row's baseline from the items' first baselines and shift each item to it - and it is the best single target left in the CSS stream: 47 files behind one mechanism, with the existing flex baseline code as the reference implementation. **Hook located 2026-11-05**: in `layout_grid_inner`'s item loop (layout.e:8687) `align` is resolved from `align_self`/`align_items` and the code handles `Normal`/`Stretch` for the item's height - **`Baseline` is never branched on**, so it falls through to the default (start) and every item keeps its own top. The fix is a per-row post-pass after the items are placed: collect each item's `has_baseline`/`baseline` (set by `set_baseline`, which already handles grid containers), take the row's maximum, and shift each item by that difference. `flex_place_item` and the flex baseline code show the shape. **Wrong - grid baseline alignment is already implemented (2026-11-05)**: the auto-placement path ends with a per-lane pass (`lane_up[64]`, `first_baseline`) that raises each lane to its lowest first baseline and shifts the items there (`bpb.y = oy0 + g.rows[rstart[bi]].pos + ... + (lane_up[...] - ... - bup)`). So `Baseline` **is** branched on, and the 47 failures lie elsewhere: either `first_baseline` returns the wrong value for these items (they have `border: black 10px` above and below and different font sizes), or the **explicit-placement** path (which uses `g.items[i].rs` rather than `rstart[]`) does not run the pass at all. Next: check which path `grid-baseline-align-001` takes - its items are auto-placed or explicitly placed - and read `first_baseline`'s treatment of a border above the first child line. **First corpus diff read (2026-11-05)**: `grid-baseline-align-001` differs from **(139, 68)** over 188 rows, but the **ink counts are equal** (172 against 172 on that row) and the pixel is **black in ours against silver (192,192,192)** in the reference - silver is the grid's `border: solid silver`, so at that pixel we draw an item's black border where the reference has the grid's own border. Equal ink with a colour swap points to a **one-pixel offset** of the item or the grid's border box, not to wrong baseline arithmetic. Next: compare the item border boxes (x and y of each `div`'s border box) between the two pages rather than the baselines - `--text` cannot show it, so print the boxes for that file. **The row index was the suspect and it is neutral (2026-11-05)**: the pass reads `rstart[bi]`, which only the auto-placement loop fills, so an explicitly placed item would be treated as row 0 - but changing both passes to `usize(g.items[bi].rs)` (the grid row every item carries) leaves `css/css-grid/alignment` at **94 of 151**. Also learned: `--only` takes a **prefix**, so `--only css/css-grid/alignment/grid-baseline-align-001` runs a single file from the corpus - the fastest way to instrument one case. The change was reverted; the 47 baseline failures are still unexplained and now have three eliminated candidates (arithmetic, `first_baseline`, lane indexing).

- **`dom/events` surveyed through the gate (2026-11-05).** The directory is 618 of 710 and its largest single file is **`Event-dispatch-single-activation-behavior.html`, 91 of 132 (41 missing)**; then `event-listener-not-fully-active-target` (1 of 16), `EventListener-handleEvent-cross-realm` (0 of 5), `event-listener-not-fully-active-adopted-node` (1 of 5), and a long tail. The 41 are all nested-`<form>` shapes ("child `<FORM><INPUT type=submit>` of parent `<FORM><INPUT type=image>`") built through `<template>` in script, so the test is about DOM's *single* activation target. **That machinery already exists**: `dom_js.e` keeps `activationTarget` across dispatch and only runs its behavior (lines ~447-509, following the spec). So the failures are in *which elements define an activation behavior* (forms, `input type=submit/image/reset`, buttons), not in the target selection. Next: print `hasActivationBehavior` for each element of the first failing case to see which side lacks one - it is a five-line fixture, and 41 subtests hang on it.

- **Round 263 (2026-11-06): `dom/events` single-activation-behavior measured, root cause NOT found.** Baseline reproduced exactly: `web-run-js77.exe D:/data/wpt-script` on `dom/events/Event-dispatch-single-activation-behavior.html` gives **91/132 (41 fail)**, matching the 2026-11-05 survey. What the 41 failure messages actually say, which corrects round 306's "print `hasActivationBehavior`" plan:
  - **The activation-target machinery is not the problem.** `dispatch` (dom_js.e 447-511) already implements the spec's single `activationTarget`, including the `activationTarget === null` guards on both the window-like branch (468) and the target-override branch (474), and `hasActivationBehavior` (589) is `!!o.activationBehavior`. Nothing there needs changing.
  - **The failures are of two distinct shapes, and only one is a form problem.** (a) `assert_array_equals: lengths differ, expected 1, got 2` where **both entries print as the same element** - i.e. one element's `activated()` ran twice, not two elements activated. (b) `expected property 0 to be ".../#testNN_link" but got <input ...>` / `got []` - the `a`/`area` cases, where the window's `hashchange` activation never arrived (empty array) or the wrong element activated first.
  - **Four standalone probes all PASS**, so the failure needs the real test's shape and cannot be reproduced cheaply: nested `createElement` forms with a submit input, `submit` reaching a nested outer form's listener, and `onsubmit` firing once per form all behave correctly. The test's actual nesting is deeper (`.container` elements between the forms) and mixes `input type=image` parents, which no probe covered.
  - **Harness notes for the next session** (each cost time): the corpus is **`D:/data/wpt-script`** (sparse clone), not `D:/wptdata/wpt` (CSS-only); `web-run` takes `ROOT LIST`, and ROOT must be a directory the runner can read **relative to itself** - the sparse clone is outside the sandbox for writes, so probes go in a workspace root that also carries `resources/testharness*.js`; `testharness`'s `t.step_timeout` does not exist in this reporter (use `setTimeout`); and `promise_test` must take `(t)` explicitly or `step_timeout` is undefined.
  - **No code change.** Nothing was attempted that could be gated, so the tree is untouched and the directory stays at 618/710 with this file at 91/132. The file is worth a fresh session with a real per-subtest print inside `activated()`; it is not a five-line fixture as round 306 predicted.

- **Round 264 (2026-11-06): FIXED - a submit or reset event no longer crosses into an enclosing form. `dom/events` 635 -> 643 of 830; the file 91 -> 99 of 132.** The print inside `activated()` answered it in one build and refuted round 263's guess: the duplicate was **not** one element twice. `probe: 25:FORM:false | 26:FORM:false` - two *different* form elements, so `activated()` ran once per form.
  - **Root cause, and it is unspecified behaviour that is nevertheless universal.** Nested `<form>` elements cannot be written in HTML but **are constructible from script**, which is how the test builds them. When the inner form dispatched `submit`, our `nodeGetTheParent` returned the parent unconditionally, so the event bubbled out into the enclosing form and fired its `onsubmit` too. The case is reported at [whatwg/dom#756](https://github.com/whatwg/dom/issues/756) (2019), which states it plainly: "a `submit` event dispatched from the inner form **does not bubble up to the outer form** ... This seems to be the case for all modern browsers", and Anne van Kesteren's reply confirms the spec does not say so yet. So the fix follows interoperable behaviour, not spec text.
  - **The fix is three lines** at `dom_js.e` `nodeGetTheParent` (838-842): if the event is `submit` or `reset` **and** the box being propagated out of is a form, return null. Other events still bubble through a form boundary - the regression case asserts that too, so the rule cannot be over-applied.
  - **Gates:** `Event-dispatch-single-activation-behavior` **99 of 132** (was 91); `dom/events` **643 of 830 subtests over 201 files** (was 635 over the same 201) - the only delta is this file's 8. No regressions. The CSS gates were re-confirmed on the rebuilt compiler `run-wpt406` after round 265: corpus **190 of 212**, grid alignment **96 of 151**, css-content **6 of 14**.
  - **Regression fixture, verified both ways:** `tests/browser/web/cases/nested-form-submit-does-not-bubble.html` (3 subtests: nested submit, nested reset, and click-still-bubbles). It scores **1 of 3 on the pre-fix build and 3 of 3 on the fixed one**. `tests/browser/web/cases/README.md` records how to run it - `web-run ROOT LIST` serves paths relative to ROOT, so the case must be staged next to a checkout's `resources/`, and `list.txt` is the file to extend.
  - **CORRECTION (round 265): `hash.e` is NOT broken. The compiler was stale.** The `E-TYPE-0002` above is real but its cause is not the source tree. `os.sha256_blocks` gained a leading `features: usize` parameter in `76dcba220` (2026-11-06 14:56), and `build/windows/neper-self.exe` was built **2026-10-29**, eight days earlier, so it still carries the old 2-parameter signature and rejects the new 3-argument call. Proof: `neper-try.exe check-file lib/e/crypto/hash.e . x64 windows` prints `module check ok`, and after rebuilding with `neper-try.exe emit-executable src/main.e . x64 windows build/windows/neper-self2.exe` the new `neper-self2.exe` checks the file clean and emits `tests/browser/wpt/run.e` without complaint. **No source change was made** - do not "fix" `hash.e:178` or the signature in `src/check.e`; they already agree. **When a signature-bearing commit lands, rebuild the compiler before believing a build failure** (`neper-try.exe` was also stale, built 14:48, eight minutes before the commit).

- **Round 266 (2026-11-06): the stale compiler is replaced; `neper-self.exe` now matches the tree.** `build/windows/neper-self.exe` was rebuilt from the current tree with `neper-try.exe emit-executable src/main.e . x64 windows ...` (no `--arena`, as the note below requires) and installed over the October copy, which is kept as `build/windows/neper-self.bak-20261029.exe` (both are gitignored build artifacts, untracked). Verified after the swap: `check-file lib/e/crypto/hash.e` -> `module check ok`; `emit-executable tests/browser/wpt/run.e` -> written; `emit-executable tests/browser/web/run.e` -> written (a second large-program case, so the no-flag arena is enough for both runners); corpus on the resulting `run-wpt407` -> **190 of 212**, unchanged. **The browser app was NOT re-emitted** at the time of writing: `check-file examples/browser/main.e` passes, but `emit-executable` on it kills the shell's Windows job runner (`exit code 134`, "before proving its managed range empty") in this environment, which is a harness limit rather than a compiler verdict. **It was re-emitted successfully in round 267 (below).**

- **Round 267 (2026-11-06): all three targets emit from the reinstalled stock compiler, and the app RUNS on Windows - the "exits 14 at startup" ALERT is retired.** The round-266 failure was entirely the shell's output-capture path: run the same emit through `cmd /c "... > tmp\app-emit.log 2>&1"` as a background job and it returns **exit 0**.
  - **The one flag that matters is quiet stdout.** Piping the compiler's output into PowerShell (either `|` or `2>&1` captured by the tool) kills the job runner at exit 134 on this program; redirecting to a file from `cmd /c` is fine. Use `cmd /c "... > file 2>&1"` for any emit of `examples/browser/main.e`, and read the file afterwards.
  - **Emitted, all three, with the reinstalled `neper-self.exe` and NO `--arena`:** x64-windows `build/windows/app-check.exe` **22.9 MB**, x64-linux `build/windows/app-check-linux` **22.95 MB**, wasm32-wasi `build/windows/app-check.wasm` **16.96 MB**. So the "wasm needs a compiler built with `--arena 6g`" recipe is **no longer needed** - the stock compiler does all three, which simplifies section 3's build instructions; verify on a future tree before deleting them.
  - **Windows is RUN-VERIFIED:** `Start-Process ... -RedirectStandardError` + `Start-Sleep 25` leaves the process **alive after 25 seconds** with a window up, on `about:blank`. That is the probe round 244 used, and it passes now.
  - **wasm32-wasi is RUN-VERIFIED:** `node scripts/run-browser-wasm.mjs build/windows/app-check.wasm` -> exit 0, "this host has no windows", "wasm32-wasi browser ran: exit 0, 14 files left in its file system".
  - **Linux emits but is still not run here** (no WSL in this environment) - unchanged from before.
  - **The Windows ALERT below is therefore historical.** It described the tree before `44dc9f93b`; the current tree emits and runs. Left in place because its bisect record is still the reason `gpu_present` has the queue rule it has.

- **Round 268 (2026-11-06): release builds of the app work for the first time. `--release` costs 22,906,246 -> 20,803,460 bytes (9%, debug vs release).** **The fix is `8bc553cd7`, NOT round 268's own commit - see the correction at the end of this entry.**
  - **Correct root cause (`8bc553cd7`, "Size the inline oracle's defer table from the candidate's span", 17:12):** the inlining oracle lowered a candidate body with a **zero-length `defer` table**, so every candidate whose body contains a `defer` answered `Capacity`, and that surfaced as a hard `cannot lower` error. The fix sizes that table from the candidate's span (`oracle_defer_cap = source_end - source_start + 8`). **`e.gpu` was the module that broke the build because it is full of `defer`-using functions** - not because of any loop, size, nesting or kernel flag (all four of those were tested and falsified in round 269).
  - **Why it looked like a cap bug:** the failing function moved with `--inline-cap` (`subgroup_exchange64` at 8, `subgroup_begin` at 16, `subgroup_add_u64` at 32) and `--inline-cap 0` never failed. The cap was a red herring: at a low cap the walk never reaches a `defer`-bearing candidate, so the `Capacity` failure never triggers.
  - **Round 268's own `past_cap` change was REVERTED (`d6b363614`).** It was diagnosed against `neper-self.bak-round266.exe`, built **17:10:29 - two minutes before `8bc553cd7` landed at 17:12:12**, so the backup predated the real fix and appeared to reproduce a failure that was already gone. Proven by control afterwards: with the `past_cap` branch fully removed the release build still succeeds. The stderr probe that settled it printed **zero** `INLINECAP` lines on both the app and `minigpu.e` - the branch never ran.
  - **Verified equivalent, not just smaller:** the **release-mode** reftest runner scores corpus **190/212**, grid alignment **96/151**, css-content **6/14** - identical to debug - and the release app stays **alive after 25s** on `about:blank`.
  - **Fixture (kept):** `tests/selfhost/fixtures/link/gpu_release` builds and runs under `--release` and passes in debug. But the **Windows build exits 14 immediately** with `browser: the window stopped: the scene was invalid. Exit status 14`, even for `about:blank`, before any page loads. That is a renderer/scene validation failure, not a missing display: it must be bisected. Candidates: this session's paint changes (`paint_text_run` now delegates to `paint_text_run_color`, and `::first-line` passes a pseudo style) and the concurrent edits other sessions made to `lib/e/gfx/scene.e` (seen modified twice mid-session). That is several 20-30 minute builds (a worktree per point), which is a fresh-session job. Until then the Windows target must be treated as broken in the tracker, and the wasm target as still working. **And it is `scene.Invalid`, not `TooLarge` (2026-11-05)**: `error_text` maps `scene.Invalid` to "the scene was invalid", and raising `MAX_FONTS` from 32 to 96 - the browser loads 36 faces, so the font table was the obvious suspect - still exits 14 unchanged, so the limit is not the cause and the change was reverted. `scene.Invalid` is a **malformed command**, which the CPU renderer accepts without validating: that is why every reftest suite is clean while the app dies. The bisect therefore has to compare the **scene command stream**, not counts: build the app with scene validation instrumented to say which command and which field was invalid, at this commit - that turns the bisect into one build instead of a dozen. **Instrumented and named (2026-11-05)**: tagging every `scene.push` case showed the last accepted commands as `3, 4, 1, 3, 5, 2, 2` (Transform, Clip, Save, Transform, FillRect, Restore, Restore) and then the failure - but **none of the validation branches printed**, so the invalid command is rejected at `push`'s **entry**: `if mem.address_of(s) == 0usize || s.finished { ret Invalid }`. The builder is already **`finished`** when that push happens, i.e. something paints **after** `scene.finish` - called from `lib/e/ui/widget.e:3419` (and the runners). So the app bug is a **push after finish**, not a malformed field: find who paints after the UI finishes its scene (most likely the page or chrome drawing a second pass into the same builder). The instrumentation was reverted; `scene.e` is unchanged. **And the builder is per frame, so the extra push is inside one frame (2026-11-05)**: `scene.builder` is called in exactly one place, `lib/e/ui/widget.e:3386`, and that builder is finished at 3419 in the same call - so the builder is not reused across frames, and whatever pushes after the finish does it **in the same frame**: the app paints something after the UI's widget tree, which means the composition order in the browser shell rather than the scene layer. `lib/e/web/shell.e`'s last commits are from another session (`12936a42c` "ui: the control carries prefers-color-scheme..."), so that ordering may have changed there; diff that commit's shell changes first. The instrumentation was reverted and `scene.e` is unchanged. **And the shape of it is now visible in the app (2026-11-05)**: `examples/browser/main.e`'s `build` calls `browser.attach`/`browser.pump` for the active tab **inside** the widget tree, so a page painted during `pump` is inside the frame - the only way a push lands after the finish is that the page's paint runs **later**, on a timer, into the same builder. That matches the earlier note that "a pending timer keeps frames coming" (`8411d013c`). So the fix is to make the tab's paint part of the widget paint callback (inside the frame) instead of a deferred paint. That is in `e.ui.browser`/`e.web.shell`, which another session has been editing, so it was not attempted here; the diagnosis is recorded instead. **Correction: `pump` does not paint (2026-11-05)**: `lib/e/ui/browser.e`'s `pump` only ticks the page, follows pending navigations and dispatches a click - no scene commands. So the deferred-paint theory above is unproven, and the remaining possibility at `push`'s entry is the other half of its condition: `mem.address_of(s) == 0usize` (a null builder state) rather than `s.finished`. One print distinguishes them - `whether state is null` and `whether finished`, at the entry check - and that is the next step, not another theory. **The entry check is not the place at all (2026-11-05)**: printing `state_null`/`finished` there produced **no output** before the failure, so the failing command never reaches `push`'s entry check and the `Invalid` comes from elsewhere. What is known is what it is *not*. The systematic way out is one build with a **print at every `ret Invalid` in `scene.e`** (there are about twenty, all listed by `Select-String 'ret Invalid'`), so the trace names the site instead of testing a theory. That is the next step, and it should be done before anything else on this app: the CPU reftest streams are clean, so only the app is affected. **The strongest remaining site, from the code (2026-11-05)**: `render_scaled` returns `Invalid` for a null target state, an out-of-range size or scale, and - most plausibly - `scene_slot` returning an error, which happens when the `SceneId` is **not live**. `ui/widget.e` releases the previous scene id immediately before committing the new one ("The previous frame's scene goes with the new one committed"), so a render of a **released** scene id gives exactly `Invalid` with no push and no font involved - consistent with every print so far being silent.
