So its remaining gap is absolute/relative positioning inside an `inline-block`, **not** baseline alignment - do not spend another round on baseline arithmetic for it.
  - **Method note, for the next session:** anchor a print on a value that must change (`dy`, `bup`, `grp`), print the *composition* (`y` and `y + bup`), and check the invariant ("all baselines equal") rather than the absolute offset. Four instruments in this round were silent or irrelevant because they printed a variable the next statement overwrote. `io.printf` needs `use e.io` in `layout.e`; `use e.os` is impossible there because `os` is already a local at 3215 and 9022 - use a module-scope `bool` set by the caller instead. 

- **Round 257 of the conformance goal (2026-11-08): lanes `repeat(auto-fill, auto)` on rows now counts item heights - `track-sizing/auto-repeat` 37 -> 39 of 132, `css/css-grid` 759 -> 761 of 1545 (the 756 recorded earlier was stale), vendored corpus 188 -> 189 of 212 (r49 vs r50, no new failure).**
  - `grid_build` fed `item_min` from item widths for columns only; rows got 0, so one row was built. Added the row pass (explicit `px` height, non-spanning items only).
  - Not done: auto-height items still contribute 0 (no content-height helper); ~55 `intrinsic-auto-repeat` files remain; `inline_baseline` is still missing from `layout.e`.
  - A vendored-corpus run takes seconds; a run that exceeds a minute is a hang, not slowness (an r49 run sat 58 min and was killed).

- **Round 258 (2026-11-08): the lanes row `auto-fill` pass measures each item the way grid row sizing does (`grid_item_width` + `flex_place_item`, percent heights indefinite) instead of reading only fixed `px` heights.** `row-auto-repeat-auto-002` passes: `auto-repeat` 39 -> 40 of 132, `css/css-grid` 761 -> 762 of 1545, vendored corpus unchanged at 189/212, no newly failing test. Remaining `row-auto-repeat-auto` files (003, 005, 006, 011-014, 017-019, 022-025, 027, 029, 030) are untouched: 005 is a *deliberately invalid* track list, 006 is an inline lanes box with `aspect-ratio`, 011 uses `width: min-content` plus a 100px row gap.

- **Round 259 (2026-11-08): `width: min-content|max-content|fit-content` honoured in `grid_item_width` and in `layout_lanes_rows` - measured NET -2 and reverted.** `css/css-grid` 762 -> 760: it fixed four (`column-fill-reverse-justify-content-001`, `-indefinite-size-001`, `row-fill-reverse-align-items-001`, `subgrid/standalone-axis-size-001`) and broke six (`column-fill-reverse-justify-items-001`, `-indefinite-size-001`, `row-fill-reverse-align-items-indefinite-size-001`, `row-explicit-placement-002/003/006`). The intended target, `row-auto-repeat-auto-003`, was never reached by it: that file uses `grid-row: span N` items, which the round-257/258 `auto-fill` row pass skips (non-spanning items only), so the repeat count stays 1. Next: spanning items in the auto-fill count, not the width rule.

- **Round 260 (2026-11-08): the lanes row `auto-fill` count takes `grid-row: span N` items (height / N) and honours `min-content`/`max-content` width, in the lanes path only.** `row-auto-repeat-auto-003` passes (reference: 11 tracks of 15px = 30px two-line item / span 2); `css/css-grid` 762 -> 763 of 1545, vendored corpus 189/212. One swap: `row-fill-reverse-align-items-001` now passes while `row-fill-reverse-align-items-indefinite-size-001` fails (same cause as round 259's regressions: a `min-content` width in a reversed lane) - net +1, not a clean win. The shared-path width change of round 259 stays out.

- **Round 261 (2026-11-08): lanes row sizing with `min-content`/`max-content` tracks - `track-sizing/auto-repeat` 41 -> 49 of 132, `css/css-grid` 763 -> 772 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212.** Two defects, and **neither alone moved a test** (each measured inert when applied singly, 41 -> 41): (1) `layout_lanes_rows` passed zero item contributions to `size_tracks` whenever the container height was definite, so an intrinsic (`min-content`/`max-content`) row collapsed to 0 and every item stacked at the top; (2) the natural-height pass measured items at the container width and ignored `width: 100px`/`min-content`, so a wrapped two-line item contributed 15px instead of 30px. Fix: take the item-contribution branch when any row track is intrinsic (stretch only when the height is definite), and give the natural pass the item's own width. Lesson: when a fix is inert, look for a second defect on the same path before discarding it - the PNG showed the first one fixed and the second still there.

- **Round 262 (2026-11-08): the column twin of round 261 - lanes columns with an intrinsic MAX (`min-content`/`max-content`) take item contributions even when the container width is definite.** `css/css-grid` 772 -> 775 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; `column-auto-repeat-min-content-001/002` and `-minmax-003` pass. `grid_build`'s lanes branch zeroed `exn` whenever `cs.width` was `px`. **First version checked min OR max and broke `column-auto-repeat-030`** (`minmax(max-content, 100px)`: a length max, so the column must not size from content) - testing the **max** kind only fixed it. Still failing in the family: `column-auto-repeat-min-content-003` (a template the reference reads as two 150px columns - not yet understood), plus `fit-content`, `max-content` and `mixed-intrinsic` files.

- **Round 263 (2026-11-08): a lanes `repeat(auto-fill, max-content)` counts columns from the items' MAX-content width.** `css/css-grid` 775 -> 778 of 1545, `track-sizing/auto-repeat` 51 -> 55 of 132, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212 (run on this build). The column count used the item's min-content width for every intrinsic track, so `max-content` over-counted (3 columns of 120 where the reference has 2). `GridParse` gained `item_max`; the fallback picks it when the repeated track's max is `max-content`. Not done: `fit-content(..)` and `mixed-intrinsic` tracks (they use neither measure alone), and the row axis, which measures natural height at one width and so has no max-content variant.

- **Round 264 (2026-11-08): lanes `repeat(auto-fill, fit-content(L))` columns: count = avail / min(item max-content, L), and the columns take item contributions at a definite width.** `css/css-grid` 778 -> 780 of 1545, `auto-repeat` 55 -> 57 of 132, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `column-auto-repeat-fit-content-001/002` pass; `-003..005` and the row twins (`row-auto-repeat-fit-content-*`) still fail - the row axis has no max-content measure (see round 263).

- **Round 265 (2026-11-08): lanes `fit-content(L)` ROW tracks count as intrinsic and take item contributions - the row twin of round 264.** `css/css-grid` 780 -> 783 of 1545, `auto-repeat` 57 -> 60 of 132, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `row-auto-repeat-fit-content-001/002/004` pass. My round-264 note that "the row axis has no max-content measure" was WRONG for these files: a row's max-content is the item's natural height, which the row pass already measured; the only gap was the `fit-content` kind (6) missing from the `intrinsic_rows` test. Remaining in the family: `row-auto-repeat-fit-content-003/005`, `-max-content-003/005`, `-min-content-003/005`, `-minmax-004/005`, `-mixed-intrinsic-*`, and the `auto-005..030` set.

- **Round 266 (2026-11-09): an `auto-fill`/`auto-fit` repeat beside a non-fixed track makes the whole track list invalid (computes to `none`).** `css/css-grid` 783 -> 787 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `gp_list` now returns an empty list when the text holds an auto repeat and any other top-level track has a min or max that is not a length or percentage (`auto_repeat_list_invalid`); the repeat's own body is unaffected. Passes `column-auto-repeat-auto-005`, `-fit-content-003`, `-max-content-003`, `-min-content-003` - the files whose own comment says "not currently a valid track definition and will fall back to none". This is the **rule that explained four tests with one change**, found by reading the markup comment, not the pixels. The row twins (`row-auto-repeat-auto-005`, `-fit-content-003`, `-max-content-003`, `-min-content-003`) still fail; they use `grid-lanes-direction: row`, which the column twins do not.

- **Round 267 (2026-11-09): a percentage-width item sizes an `auto-fill` column by its MAX-content width (cyclic percentage), not by the resolved percentage.** `css/css-grid` 787 -> 788 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `column-auto-repeat-auto-002` passes (3 columns of 120px, where the item's `25%` of 360 would have given 90px and 4 columns). **Ruled out, with evidence:** the row twins `row-auto-repeat-auto-005` / `-fit-content-003` / `-max-content-003` / `-min-content-003` - their reference is a hand-built plain `display: grid` with six items placed on `grid-row: 1|2` and a `span 2` item, which is not a rendering of any rule the invalid-list fallback gives, so there is no cluster-wide fix to chase. Left: `column-auto-repeat-auto-003` (`75%` width with a `50px` height, same cyclic rule plus the spanning arithmetic), `-006/-007` (inline `grid-lanes` with `aspect-ratio`), `-013` (`auto-fit`), and the `mixed-intrinsic` set.

- **Round 268 (2026-11-09): the column `auto-fill` count takes `grid-column: span N` items (width / N) - the column twin of round 258.** `css/css-grid` 788 -> 789 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `column-auto-repeat-auto-003` passes (reference: 6 columns of 60px = a 120px item spanning 2). One file only, but it closes the row/column asymmetry: both axes now divide a spanning item's contribution by its span.

- **Round 269 (2026-11-09): a lanes `auto-fill` repeat of SEVERAL tracks, some intrinsic, is as wide as its tracks' own sizes added up.** `css/css-grid` 789 -> 793 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Per track: length/percentage as written, `min-content` = item min width, `max-content` and `auto` = item max width, `fit-content(L)` = `min(max, L)`; the count is `avail / sum`. Passes `column-auto-repeat-fit-content-004`, `-max-content-004`, `-min-content-004` (each a two-track repeat) and `-mixed-intrinsic-003`. The row axis shares the code (`item_max` is set equal to the height there, since block-axis min and max content coincide). Not done: `mixed-intrinsic-001/002/004` (column) and `-001/002` (row) - they combine more track kinds and a `minmax()`; `auto` as max-content is a guess the passing files neither confirm nor refute. `column-auto-repeat-auto-013` was looked at and dropped: `repeat(auto-fit, auto)` with explicit `grid-column: 3` placement whose reference leaves a collapsed middle column.

- **Round 270 (2026-11-09): lanes columns with intrinsic tracks keep the `auto`-track stretch.** `css/css-grid` 793 -> 795 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Round 261's column edit passed `stretch_cols && !intrinsic_cols`, which switched off the stretch for *every* column whenever any was intrinsic; `size_tracks` only stretches tracks whose max is `auto`, so the guard was never needed and left the `auto` track of `repeat(auto-fill, min-content max-content fit-content(100px) auto)` at 120px instead of the 190px the reference shows. Passes `column-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-001` and `-minmax-004`. **A guard added defensively in one round was the bug of a later round**: the count was right and the PNG showed only the last track too narrow. Left in the family: `mixed-intrinsic-002/004` (column), `-001/002` (row).

- **Round 271 (2026-11-09): a masonry item with `grid-column: span N` and an automatic start picks its lane run like any auto-placed item.** `css/css-grid` 795 -> 797 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `layout_lanes` ran the "run that ends highest" search only for `sc.kind == 0` (no placement at all); a `span N` item (`kind 2`, auto end) kept the start `grid_build`'s static placement gave it, so the second brown span-2 item of `column-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-002` sat on lanes 1-2 while the reference has it on 0-1 (a tie, which the search resolves to the lowest start). The count and the track widths were already right; **a debug print of `best`/`ends` showed the span item never reached the search at all**. Passes `-mixed-intrinsic-002` and `-004` (both measured separately: 0 blank pairs). Note: the sweep's summary counted 10 blank pairs against 9 before, with no test changing status - unattributed, probably the harness, not this change. The `row-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-*` pair is untouched.

- **Round 272 (2026-11-09): the row twin of round 271 - a `grid-row: span N` item with an automatic start takes the lane run that ends highest in `layout_lanes_rows`.** `css/css-grid` 797 -> 799 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; passes `row-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-002` and `row-auto-repeat-002` (the latter outside the intrinsic family). The blank-pair count returned to 9, so round 271's 10 was a transient of that build's sweep, not a test. Left in the family: `row-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-001`.

- **Round 273 (2026-11-09): survey, no source change - where the 746 remaining `css/css-grid` failures are, and two families examined and dropped.**
  - By directory (r71 sweep): `grid-lanes/subgrid/grid-subgridded-to-grid-lanes` **130**, `grid-lanes/track-sizing/auto-repeat` 56, `alignment/self-baseline` 30, `grid-lanes/tentative/item-placement` 28, `item-placement/dense-packing` 27, `flow-tolerance` 16, `grid-lanes-subgridded-to-grid-lanes` 13, `grid-lanes-subgridded-to-grid` 9, then ~40 single `subgrid/*` files.
  - **`grid-subgridded-to-grid-lanes` (130) is not one rule.** By name it splits into `line-names` 21, `gap` 21+2, `track-sizing/*-extra-margin` 18, `*-standalone-axis-size` 8, `baseline` 9, `orthogonal-writing-mode` 8, auto-placed inherited tracks 12 and others. `column-subgrid-grid-gap-001` renders the outer tracks 54/74/54 against the reference's 64/64/64 - the subgrid's padding and gap contribution to the tracks it spans, not an `inline-grid-lanes` parsing gap (that value is supported; 53 of the 130 use it). Dropped after one render: it needs the subgrid-contribution arithmetic, one sub-cluster at a time.
  - **`row-auto-repeat-mixed-intrinsic-001` dropped:** the reference shows 100/100/100/200 tracks for `min-content max-content fit-content(100px) auto` in a 500px container with 30px-tall items, i.e. the free space is shared by more than the `auto` track; our render has a single lane. No rule extracted.
  - **`css/syntax` (233/275) and `normal-flow` (627/675) were not touched** - both were mined for many rounds earlier with nothing shipped (see rounds 22-38).
  - Method note: the `grid-lanes/track-sizing/auto-repeat` directory went 37 -> 57+ of 132 over rounds 257-272 because each round fixed **one rule shared by several files**, found by rendering one PNG and reading the reference's own markup. Subgrid is the next place that method has not been tried.

- **Round 274 (2026-11-09): subgrids inherit the line names of the parent lines they span; `span <name>` and an out-of-range `<name> N` resolve.** `css/css-grid` 799 -> 805 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `subgrid/line-names-002/003` (plain grid parent) and `grid-subgridded-to-grid-lanes/line-names/{column,row}-line-names-002/003` pass. Three pieces, **none of which moved a test alone** (the resolver half measured 799 -> 799 first): (1) `Layout.sub_cn`/`sub_rn` carry the spanned parent names, renumbered from the subgrid's first line (`subgrid_names`), set at all four hand-off sites (`subgrid_prepare` columns and rows, `subgrid_prepare_lane`, `subgrid_prepare_lane_rows`); (2) `grid_build` pushes them into the subgrid's own name list after `subgrid_tracks`; (3) `resolve_axis` honours `span <name>` (back to the Nth earlier line of that name) and `resolve_line` clamps an `<name> N` that exceeds the count to the last such line (the reference puts the item on the last explicit track). Before this the names did not reach a subgrid at all - the subgrid kept no `[a]` of its parent. 17 `line-names` files still fail (`-006..-017`): they write `subgrid [x] [y]` names of their own and use named areas, which this does not read.

- **Round 275 (2026-11-09): a named line that is missing falls to the implicit lines; a subgrid's own `subgrid [x] [y]` names are read.** `css/css-grid` 805 -> 812 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Passes `grid-placement-using-named-grid-lines-009`, `subgrid/line-names-009/015` and the lanes twins `line-names/{column,row}-line-names-009/011/015/017`. `resolve_line`: with NO line of that name, `<name> N` is line `explicit + 1 + N` (the N-th implicit line after the explicit grid, per the spec); with SOME but fewer than N, it clamps to the last one. **The two halves had to be split**: the pure spec rule alone gained 9 and lost 3 (`line-names-002` x3, whose reference puts the item on the last explicit track), the pure clamp alone gained none of the 009 family. `subgrid_own_names` adds the k-th `[..]` group after `subgrid` as line k+1; measured inert on its own (805 -> 805) and kept because the 009/011/017 files need it together with the implicit-line rule. Left: `line-names-006/007/008/012/016` (writing-mode / `rtl` subgrids - a different feature).

- **Round 276 (2026-11-10): `flow-tolerance` accepts `normal`, `infinite`, `em` and `%`; a masonry item goes to the first tied lane AFTER the one the previous item took.** `css/css-grid` 812 -> 818 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Passes `column-flow-tolerance-infinite`, `flow-tolerance-column-001/003/004` and `flow-tolerance-percentage-intrinsic-{column,row}-001`. The tolerance parsed only whole `px` before (`lanes_tolerance`, `axis_total`; a `%` is of the tracks-plus-gaps size, which the intrinsic reference states as "400px"). `pick_run` finds the lowest run end, then takes the first run within `tolerance` of it starting after the previous item's lane - infinite tolerance therefore deals items round-robin, which the reference shows; with tolerance 0 it is the old lowest-index choice. **Measured trap:** making an ABSENT `flow-tolerance` mean `1em` (the spec's initial value) gained `column-initial-flow-tolerance` but lost ten tests that never mention the property (810 vs 812), so absent stays 0 and only the keywords `normal`/`initial` mean `1em`; `column-initial-flow-tolerance` and `row-initial-flow-tolerance` therefore still fail. Dense packing (`layout_lanes_dense`) keeps its own chain rule - its flow-tolerance files (8) still fail.

- **Round 277 (2026-11-10): dense packing searches every lane run for a `span N` item with an automatic start, and takes the first run within `flow-tolerance` of the lowest.** `css/css-grid` 818 -> 825 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Passes `column-dense-packing-001`, `-flow-tolerance-002`, `-multi-span-004/007/009/010` and `column-fill-reverse-dense-packing-flow-tolerance-001`. `layout_lanes_dense` pinned any item whose start was not `auto` (`sc.kind != 0`) to its static start - which included `grid-column: span 2` (kind 2, auto start), the **third** place this round-271/272 defect has appeared (`layout_lanes`, `layout_lanes_rows`, now this). The tolerance half (collect each lane's earliest free top, then the first lane in ORDER within `tolerance` of the lowest) was inert until the span fix reached it: two defects on one path again. In dense the tie goes to the lowest index, not the lane after the previous item (that rule is for the non-dense flow, round 276). **Method note:** a full `css/css-grid/` sweep took 16 minutes here; one that runs well past 25 is machine load, not a hang - confirm per directory with a 100 s cap before killing.

- **Round 278 (2026-11-10): dense packing for lanes along the ROWS.** `css/css-grid` 825 -> 837 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; `item-placement/dense-packing` 25 -> 36 of 45. `layout_lanes_rows` had no dense branch at all (the dispatcher takes the rows path before it ever asks `lanes_dense`), so every `row-*-dense-packing-*` file was laid out as plain masonry. The branch keeps per-lane intervals (`dos`/`doe`/`don`), lays the item out for each candidate lane run, takes the earliest free horizontal position for it, and picks the first run in order within the flow tolerance of the lowest - the transpose of `layout_lanes_dense`, including the span-with-auto-start search from round 277. Left: the `tentative/item-placement/*-reverse-dense-packing-*` files (`fill-reverse`/`track-reverse` combined with dense), the `alignment/*dense-packing-*` family (align/justify with dense, ~25 files) and `grid-lanes-grid-placement-named-lines-dense-packing-001`.

- **Round 279 (2026-11-10): a dense-packed item is aligned within its HOLE - from its own end to the top (or left) of the next item below (or right of) it in any of its lanes, in any placement order.** `css/css-grid` 837 -> 856 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; `grid-lanes/alignment` 47 -> 66 of 136. Neither dense function aligned items at all (the non-dense ones already did, over `tops`/`lefts` of LATER items only). Dense placement is not monotone - a later item can sit above an earlier one - so the limit is the minimum over every other item whose lanes overlap and whose top is at or after this item's bottom; `layout_lanes_dense` gained `ch` (the container's definite height) and per-item `dtops`/`dss`/`dsp`; `layout_lanes_rows` takes the same `j = 0` scan when `dense`. Passes the `align-items`, `align-self`, `justify-items` and `justify-self` dense files, single and multi-span. Left in dense: the `*-reverse-dense-packing-*` files (`fill-reverse`/`track-reverse` combined with dense), `grid-lanes-grid-placement-named-lines-dense-packing-001`, and the dense `flow-tolerance` files that still fail.

- **Round 280 (2026-11-10): `track-reverse` without the physical mirror - measured NET -7 and reverted.** Reading `column-reverse-dense-packing-001`, the reference is a plain `display: grid` whose explicit placements (`grid-column: 3`) sit at the same x as the test's, and the test's lanes come out on the right edge in ours; that suggested the mirror (`lanes_mirror`, `track_rev`) was wrong. Turning it off across `grid-lanes/` (858 files) gained one test (`overflow-reverse-baseline-001`) and lost eight (`row-justify-items-008/010/012/014`, `row-track-reverse-dense-packing-flow-tolerance-001`, `column-reverse-002`, `row-reverse-002/003`), so the mirror is right for most of the family. What the reference shows is instead a different **tie-break or sizing** for the reversed dense files: the span-4 item lands on the LAST run (lanes 2-5) and the container does not stretch to the full width the mirror reflects across. Not pursued: the reference is hand-built and would need the lane extent of a shrink-to-fit container. Left: `tentative/item-placement/*reverse*` (about 28), `alignment/*fill-reverse*`, `abspos/*oof-fill-reverse*`, `overflow/*reverse*`.

- **Round 281 (2026-11-10): a row subgrid whose own `row-gap` differs from its parent's adds half the difference to each side of its contribution to the parent's rows.** `css/css-grid` 856 -> 860 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; `subgrid/grid-gap-*` 3 -> 7 of 16. The column axis already did this (`half_gap` in `subgrid_contributions`); the row axis (`row_extra` in `layout_grid_inner`) added the subgrid's edge padding but not the gap difference, so a subgrid with `row-gap: 100px` under a 20px parent left the parent's rows 80px short. **I first re-wrote the column version by mistake** (a duplicate `let` stopped the build: `half_gap is already bound`) - read the existing contribution loop before adding to it. Left: `grid-gap-001..004` still fail in the plain-grid set and the lanes `gap/*` files (21), which route through `layout_lanes`/`layout_lanes_rows` and do not go through `row_extra` at all.

- **Round 282 (2026-11-10): a grid-lanes container sizes its lanes from the items of its subgrids as well.** `css/css-grid` 860 -> 864 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. The lanes branch of `layout_grid_inner` built its sizing items from `g.items` only (a subgrid spans several lanes, so it contributes to none), while the plain-grid path already appended `subgrid_contributions`. The lanes branch now appends the extras of `subgrid_contributions` unchanged (their start lane is fixed; only the container's own items are copied across every lane they could occupy). Passes `column-subgrid-grid-gap-001`, `column-subgrid-scroller-padding-001`, `column-subgrid-writing-direction-002` and `grid-lanes-subgrid-intrinsic-sizing`. Measured by directory first: `grid-subgridded-to-grid-lanes/gap/` only went 14 -> 15 of 38, the other three came from files outside it - **a directory is not the right unit for judging a change this shared; sweep the whole tree**. Left: the other `gap/*` files (row axis through `layout_lanes_rows`) and `extra-margin`.

- **Round 283 (2026-11-10): a lanes container keeps the implicit lane an item names with a definite line past the explicit template.** `css/css-grid` 864 -> 870 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `layout_grid_inner` cut `g.nc`/`g.nr` back to the explicit count (`ec`/`er`) so that auto-placement's made-up tracks would not become lanes - which also removed the lane a `grid-column: 6` item asked for under `repeat(5, ...)`, and that item then fell back to lane 0. The cap now stops at the furthest definite (`kind 1` line number or `kind 3` named line) placement. Passes `column-auto-repeat-015/026`, `row-auto-repeat-024`, `intrinsic-auto-repeat/column-auto-repeat-auto-019/022` and `row-auto-repeat-auto-022` - six `auto-repeat` files, found while chasing `gap/column-subgrid-grid-gap-002` (which this alone does not fix). Left: the lanes `gap/*` row files and `extra-margin`.

- **Round 284 (2026-11-10): lanes keep their items' contributions when the free width will not be shared out.** `css/css-grid` 870 -> 876 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. The lanes branch zeroed every item's contribution for a `display: grid-lanes` container (`cs.display == .Grid`), on the assumption that the `auto` lanes then stretch to share the width equally. With `place-content: start` (or any non-stretch `justify-content`) nothing stretches, so all lanes stayed 0px wide and everything piled up at the left edge: **a debug print of `g.cols[..].base` at the top of `layout_lanes` read `0 0 0 0 0 0`**. The zeroing now applies only with `stretch_cols`. Passes `column-subgrid-grid-gap-002` and the five `order/column-order-property-auto-placement-001..005`. The previous round's implicit-lane fix was a prerequisite for the first of them (its sixth lane) but was not enough alone.

- **Round 285 (2026-11-10): lanes along the rows count their subgrids' own rows, with the gap difference and edges, towards the lane heights.** `css/css-grid` 876 -> 881 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. The row twin of rounds 281/282: `layout_lanes_rows` sized its lanes from `g.items` copies only, and a subgrid spanning several lanes contributes to none, so a container holding one came out 38px short (`row-subgrid-grid-gap-002`: 60px against the reference's 98px). The natural-height pass already lays the subgrid out stand-alone, which registers its own rows in `l.grids`; those `rsz` values (plus the subgrid's edges on the first and last row and half the row-gap difference on each side facing a gap) are appended to the sizing items with their fixed start lane. Passes `row-subgrid-scroller-padding-001`, `row-subgrid-grid-gap-004`, `-larger-001/002`, `-smaller-001`. Left: `row-subgrid-grid-gap-001/002/003/006/007/009/012` (they place the subgrid on rows past the explicit template - `grid-row: 2 / span 5` under `repeat(4, auto)` - and the stand-alone lane count differs) and `track-sizing/row-subgrid-extra-margin-*`.

- **Round 286 (2026-11-10): the shrink-to-fit width of a grid-lanes container whose lanes run along the ROWS is how far its items run along the lanes.** `css/css-grid` 881 -> 896 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `grid_widths` (the intrinsic width an `inline grid-lanes` box shrinks to) read only the column tracks, which for row lanes are the implicit single column, so the box was as wide as its first item and the rest overflowed: in `row-subgrid-extra-margin-001` the picture was right but the 1px border stopped halfway. It now lays the row lanes out once at `0` (min-content) and once at a huge width (max-content) and takes the right-most item edge. The `lanes_cap` helper (the explicit-lanes cut with the definite-placement exception of round 283) was pulled out of `layout_grid_inner` so both paths share it. 15 files pass, across `baseline/row-grid-lanes-item-baseline-*`, `flow-tolerance-row-001..004`, `row-auto-repeat-*`, `row-auto-placement-003` and one `extra-margin`: **a missing intrinsic width was hiding behind many unrelated-looking failures** - the row twin of what `layout_lanes`' column path had all along. Left: `row-subgrid-extra-margin-002..009`, the six `row-subgrid-grid-gap` files.

- **Round 287 (2026-11-10): `track-reverse` reflects the lanes across their OWN extent, not across the container.** `css/css-grid` 896 -> 908 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `lanes_mirror` reflected each lane area across `lane_extent`, and every caller passed the container's content width (columns) or height (rows); with lanes that do not fill the container (`repeat(3, 80px)` in an 800px box) the reversed lanes jumped to the container's far edge, where the references keep them against the near edge in reversed order. `lanes_span` gives `first lane pos + the lanes' total`, which equals the container's size whenever the lanes fill it, so nothing that already worked moves. Passes `column-align-items-009/011/013/015`, `column-track-reverse-justify-content-001`, `row-track-reverse-align-content-001` and the `tentative/item-placement/column-reverse-*` set. **This is what round 280 was circling**: its reading ("the reference does not stretch to the width the mirror reflects across") was right, but the experiment that disabled the mirror altogether was the wrong test of it. Left in the reverse family: the `fill-reverse` files and the remaining `tentative/item-placement/*reverse*`.

- **Round 288 (2026-11-10): `min-content`/`max-content` item width kept inside the COLUMN lanes placement (`lanes_item_width`) - NET 0, reverted.** `css/css-grid` 908 -> 908 of 1545: it passes `column-fill-reverse-justify-content-001` and `-indefinite-size-001` (items keep their content width inside the 80px lane) and fails `column-fill-reverse-justify-items-001` and `-indefinite-size-001` - the same pair of swaps as round 259's shared-path attempt, so scoping it to the lanes did not remove the cost. The losers' references draw 64px-wide, two-line items from `width: min-content` text ("item 1"), while ours measure 48px: a font-metric / min-content-width difference in the harness, not a placement rule. Left unchanged.

- **Round 289 (2026-11-10): a `fill-reverse` stack is placed by `align-content` / `justify-content`, and `flow-start` / `flow-end` parse.** `css/css-grid` 908 -> 911 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `fill-reverse` flows items from the far end and our mirror leaves the whole stack against it; the references place the stack at the container's start, middle or end by `align-content` (`justify-content` for lanes along the rows). `lanes_stack_align` runs after `lanes_mirror`, only for `fill-reverse`, only with a definite size along the stack, only for `start`/`center`/`end` (and `flow-end`, which is the end the flow does NOT start from, i.e. the physical start). **First version measured NET -1** (907): it also shifted `safe start/center/end` stacks that overflow and treated the unparsed `flow-end` as `normal`; a stack that overflows (`free < 0`) now stays put (CSS-align's safe fallback as the references show it) and `style.Align` gained `FlowStart`/`FlowEnd` at the END of the enum (the keyword index is positional). Passes `column-fill-reverse-align-content-001`, `row-fill-reverse-justify-content-001` and its `-indefinite-size-001`. Left: `column-fill-reverse-align-content-002` and the `fill-reverse` abspos/overflow files.

- **Round 290 (2026-11-10): in a `fill-reverse` stack `flex-start` / `flex-end` follow the flow, like `flow-start` / `flow-end`.** `css/css-grid` 911 -> 914 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Round 289 mapped `flex-start`/`flex-end` to the physical start/end; the references for `column-fill-reverse-align-content-002` put `flex-start` where the flow starts (the far end) and `flex-end` at the container's start. Passes `column-fill-reverse-align-content-002`, `row-fill-reverse-justify-content-002` and `-indefinite-size-002`. Left in the `fill-reverse` alignment family: the `align-items` / `justify-items` / `-self` files and the `multi-span` ones.

- **Round 291 (2026-11-11): an absolutely positioned DESCENDANT of a grid container (not only a child) takes the grid area it names - but only when it names a line on BOTH axes.** `css/css-grid` 914 -> 919 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `abspos` used the container's grid area only when the element's parent was the container (`parent == cbid`); CSS Grid 1 §9 applies it to any element whose containing block is the container, and `positioned-grid-descendant-containing-block-009/010/013/014/015` (a div inside a grid item, with `grid-row`/`grid-column` set) need that. **Three versions measured:** no restriction at all was NET -1 (6 lost: the descendants with NO placement - `-008`, `-012` - keep their static position - plus `descendant-static-position-003/004` and the lanes `out-of-flow-002` pair); "names a line on either axis" was NET 0 (the same `descendant-static-position-003/004`, which name only the column axis and still want their static position on the row axis); both axes is the one that holds. The remaining axis-by-axis case (one axis named, the other from the static position) is not modelled: `grid_abs_area` falls back to the padding box on an auto edge. A full sweep took ~40 minutes this time - machine load again, the process was alive and finished.

- **Round 292 (2026-11-11): a grid item with an `aspect-ratio` and a percentage `height` gets its intrinsic width from that height resolved against its fixed-length rows.** `css/css-grid` 919 -> 925 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `grid_build` took the item's column contribution from `outer_content_widths`, which cannot resolve a percentage height, so `<div style="aspect-ratio:1/1; height:100%">` in an `inline-grid` with `grid-template-rows: 100px` contributed 0 and the container collapsed to nothing. When every row the item spans is a fixed length, `height = px + pct * sum(rows)` now feeds `ratio_width`; an item not yet placed (`r0 < 0`, auto placement) is taken to sit on the first row - **the first version with `r0 >= 0` required passed only `aspect-ratio-002`**. Passes `grid-items/aspect-ratio-001/002/003` and, unexpectedly, `subgrid/row-subgrid-intrinsic-size-transfer-001/002/003`. Left: `aspect-ratio-004/005`.

- **Round 293 (2026-11-11): the aspect-ratio branch of `grid_item_width` resolves a percentage `height` against the grid AREA height, not the area width.** `css/css-grid` 925 -> 926 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. `resolve_len(is.height, area_w)` used the width as the basis of a percent height, so `height: 100%` in a 50px row under a 100px-wide area gave `100% * 100 = 100px` and a 200px-wide `aspect-ratio: 2/1` item; `grid_item_width_h` takes the area height (the old `grid_item_width` passes `-1`, leaving the percentage unresolved) and the main placement loop in `layout_grid_inner` passes it. Passes `aspect-ratio-005`. Left: `aspect-ratio-004` (a replaced `<img>` with `align-self: stretch` in a fixed row: the stretched height must transfer through the ratio into the container's width).

- **Round 294 (2026-11-11): a REPLACED grid item stretched to a fixed row contributes its ratio width to the column.** `css/css-grid` 926 -> 927 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; all five `grid-items/aspect-ratio-*` pass. `<img style="align-self: stretch">` in `grid-template-rows: 100px` was laid out 100px tall but contributed only its natural 50px width, so the inline-grid was half the width of its green square. The round-292 branch in `grid_build` now also covers `ratio_stretch` (a replaced item, `height: auto`, `align-self: stretch`): `row sum - margins - vertical extras` times the replaced item's ratio. **Two traps:** `has_box_ratio` is false for a replaced element (the ratio comes from `replaced_intrinsic`), and `ratio_width` returned 0 for it - a debug print (`rs=100 h=100 rw=0`) found that in one build - so the replaced case computes the ratio itself.

- **Round 295 (2026-11-11): `width: min-content | max-content | fit-content` honoured for items of a PLAIN grid (`grid_item_width`, `!cs.lanes`).** `css/css-grid` 927 -> 932 of 1545 (8 gained, 3 lost), vendored corpus 189/212. Gains: `subgrid/standalone-axis-size-001` and seven `grid-lanes/subgrid/.../row/grid-lanes-subgrid-001a-d`, `-002b-d` (a subgrid with `width: min-content` no longer stretches across the container and shows red). Losses: `grid-lanes/grid-placement/row-explicit-placement-002/003/006` - the same trio round 259 lost. **Their reference is a plain `display: grid` with `.grid > div { width: fit-content }`**: while `grid_item_width` ignored `fit-content` the reference and the lanes test both stretched, so they matched by accident; now the reference is laid out right and the lanes (rows) test differs from it in the available width its fit-content uses (`cw - margins` in `layout_lanes_rows`, against the area width in the plain grid). Shipped with the trade because the cause of the losses is the lanes side, not this rule. Left: `standalone-axis-size-002/004/007/009..013`, and the lanes rows `fit-content` available width (the way to win the trio back).

- **Round 296 (2026-11-11): `grid-lanes-direction: row` makes the lanes run along the rows even with no row template, and `auto` rows size from their items when the container height is definite.** `css/css-grid` 932 -> 935 of 1545 (5 gained, 2 lost), vendored corpus 189/212. The direction property was never read for the choice of axis (`lanes_along_rows` now: no column template, and either a row template or `row` in the direction), so `row-explicit-placement-*` - rows named only by `grid-row-start` - were laid out as column lanes; and `layout_lanes_rows` only gave its lanes item contributions when the height was indefinite or a row was intrinsic, so definite-height `auto` rows simply shared the space (kind 3 now counts). Gains: `row-explicit-placement-001/004/005`, `row-item-percentage-height-001`, `row-subgrid-orthogonal-writing-mode-004`. Losses: `row-scrollbar-gutter-001/002`. Round 295's trio (`row-explicit-placement-002/003/006`) is **not** back: they now fit-content their items correctly but the second item's top/bottom margins (`40px`/`80px`) overlap the third lane - the implicit rows are sized from natural heights, not from heights plus margins.

- **Round 297 (2026-11-11): an item placed on a DEFINITE lane sizes that lane alone.** `css/css-grid` 935 -> 939 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212. Both lane-sizing loops (`layout_grid_inner` columns and `layout_lanes_rows`) copied every item into the sizing of every lane it "might land in" - including items with `grid-column: 3` / `grid-row-start: 2`, which can only land in one, so each such item inflated all the lanes. A definite start (`kind 1` line, `kind 3` named line) now adds one copy at its own lane. Passes `grid-lanes-gap-001`, `grid-lanes-intrinsic-sizing-cols-001-fr`, `row-explicit-placement-006` (the third of round 295's trio) and `column-line-names-011`. Left: `row-explicit-placement-002/003/007/008`.

- **Round 298 (2026-11-11): the natural-height pass of `layout_lanes_rows` honours an explicit `height`.** `css/css-grid` 939 -> 948 of 1545 (10 gained, 1 lost: `grid-lanes-fr-rows-indefinite-001`), vendored corpus 189/212. The pass that measures each item for lane sizing called `flex_place_item(.., -1.0)` - "content height" - so `height: 80px` was lost: **a debug print of `h`/`mt`/`mb` read `h=16 mt=40 mb=80 outer=136` for the item that should have been 80px tall (outer 200)**, which is why the implicit row 2 of `row-explicit-placement-002` was 64px short and the third lane overlapped it. A `px` height is now passed as the definite content height, like every other placement site. Passes `row-explicit-placement-002/003`, the three `row-grid-lanes-item-baseline-cyclic-dependency-*`, `intrinsic-sizing/row-defined-height`, `row-flow-tolerance-infinite`, `grid-lanes-overflow-reverse-002` and the `row-scrollbar-gutter-001/002` pair that round 296 lost. Left: `row-explicit-placement-007/008`, and the new `grid-lanes-fr-rows-indefinite-001` (an `fr` row under an indefinite height).

- **Round 299 (2026-11-11): flexible rows under an indefinite height get their second pass, and lane layouts keep `position: relative` offsets.** `css/css-grid` 948 -> 949 of 1545, no newly failing test, vendored corpus 189/212; passes `grid-lanes-fr-rows-indefinite-001` (the one test round 298 lost). Two defects, neither enough alone: (1) `layout_lanes_rows` sized `minmax(0, 0.4fr)` rows once against the items' contribution (40px each) and never again; with an indefinite height the `fr` size is found a second time against the height the first pass produced (CSS Grid 2's find-fr-size, sum of factors clamped to 1: 80px / 1 = 80, so 32px each) - `size_tracks` again with `avail = tracks_total`; (2) every lane layout set `pb.x`/`pb.y` AFTER `flex_place_item` had applied the item's relative offset, throwing away `position: relative; top: -32px`, so the green item sat 32px low. `relative_offset` is added back at the three lane placement sites (`layout_lanes_rows`, `layout_lanes_dense`, `layout_lanes`). Not audited: the mirror (`lanes_mirror`) reads `pb.x`/`pb.y` to find a lane, so a relatively positioned item in a reversed lane may land one lane off.

- **Round 300 (2026-11-11): `min-width`/`max-width` apply to table cells, columns and column groups, and `min-height`/`max-height` to a table's own specified height.** `css/CSS2/normal-flow` 627 -> 632 of 675, no newly failing test; `css/CSS2/tables` unchanged at 208 of 250; vendored corpus 189/212 (`css/css-grid` was not re-run: the change is in table code only). The table builder took a cell's `width` straight from `resolve_len` into `cspec`, so `width: 3in; max-width: 1in` on a `display: table-cell` (or a `<col>` / `<colgroup>`) was 3in wide; each now goes through `clamp_width`. `layout_table` took the table's specified height as given; `ch_spec` is clamped by `clamp_height` first. Passes `max-width-applies-to-005/006/007` and `max-height-applies-to-013/014` - **the first CSS2 round that broke a measured "long tail": a four-test family found by grouping failure names by their prefix**. Left: `max-width-applies-to-015` / `max-height-applies-to-015` (the same clamp on a `table-caption`), `max-height-applies-to-017` (a percentage height against the clamped `max-height`), and `max-height-separates-margin`.

- **Round 301 (2026-11-11): a table caption honours its own `width`, `min-width`/`max-width` and `max-height`.** `css/CSS2/normal-flow` 632 -> 636 of 675, `css/CSS2/tables` unchanged at 208/250, vendored corpus 189/212, no newly failing test. `layout_table` laid every caption out at the table's content width and ignored the caption's `width: 3in; max-width: 1in` (and `height`/`max-height`); the width now starts from the explicit length, goes through `clamp_width`, and `cap.w` follows; the height goes through `clamp_height`. Passes `max-width-applies-to-015`, `max-height-applies-to-015` and two further caption tests in the family. Left in the family: `max-height-applies-to-017` (a percentage height inside an `inline-block` with `max-height`) and `max-height-separates-margin`.

- **Round 302 (2026-11-11): an absolutely positioned element is clipped by its containing block, not by the static `overflow` boxes between it and that block.** `css/CSS2/positioning` 444 -> 453 of 498, `normal-flow` 636/675, `css/css-grid` 949/1545 and the vendored corpus 189/212 all unchanged, no newly failing test. `push_entry_clips` (paint) walked from a positioned box's parent up to its containing block and pushed the clip of **every** ancestor that clips overflow - including `overflow: scroll` boxes between an abspos element and a containing block further up, which the element escapes (`abspos-overflow-001..009`: a green `position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0` box inside an `overflow: scroll` div painted behind the red box it should cover). It now pushes only the clip of a positioned ancestor. **First version cost five corpus tests** (`css-overflow__clip-002..005`, `clipmargin-001`): a `position: relative` element stays in its parent's flow, so every clipping ancestor applies to it; the relaxed rule is for absolute/fixed elements only. Found by grouping `positioning` failure names by prefix (`abspos-overflow` 11) and diffing one picture. Left in that family: `abspos-overflow-011/012`; and the `absolute-replaced-width/height` files, which use `<svg:svg>` (inline SVG through a namespace prefix - infrastructure).

- **Round 303 (2026-11-11): a fixed-layout table sizes a percentage column from the table's width, plus its cells' horizontal padding and border.** `css/CSS2/tables` 208 -> 215 of 250, `normal-flow` 636/675, `positioning` 453/498 and the vendored corpus 189/212 unchanged, no newly failing test (`css/css-grid` not re-run: table code only). `distribute_columns`' `fixed_layout` branch handled only `px` columns (`col_spec`) and gave every other column an equal share, so `td { width: 50% }` in `table-layout: fixed; width: 100px` came out a third of the table instead of `50% * 100 + 50px of padding`. Columns with `col_pct` now take `col_pct * avail` plus the widest `horizontal_extras` of their single-column cells before the remaining columns share what is left. Passes seven of the `fixed-table-layout-017..031` files (`fixed-table-layout-025` among them) - found by grouping `tables` failure names by prefix (`fixed-table-layout` 14). Left: `fixed-table-layout-022/023` and the rest of that range; and a `<col>` whose own `width` is a percentage still does not reach `col_pct` (`17` reads only `px`).

- **Round 304 (2026-11-11): a `<col>` / `<colgroup>` whose own `width` is a percentage now feeds the percentage column list.** `css/CSS2/tables` 215 -> 222 of 250, `normal-flow` 636/675, `positioning` 453/498, vendored corpus 189/212 unchanged, no newly failing test; **`fixed-table-layout` is now 67 of 67**. Round 303's note ("a `<col>` with a percentage `width` still does not reach `col_pct`") was the remaining half: the table builder read a column element's width only as a `px` length into `cspec`, so `col#test { width: 40% }` was ignored in both table layouts; `is_pct_width` widths now raise `cpct` at the three sites (`<col>`, `<col>` inside a `<colgroup>`, a childless `<colgroup>`). Left in `tables`: `table-anonymous-objects` (11), `empty-cells-applies-to` (3), `row-visibility` / `column-visibility` and the `border-collapse` files.

- **Round 305 (2026-11-11): the root element's background IMAGE covers the whole canvas.** Vendored corpus 189 -> 191 of 212, `css/CSS2/backgrounds` unchanged at 312/332, no newly failing test (`css-grid`, `tables`, `positioning` not re-run: a paint-only change to the root's own `background-image`). `paint_canvas` already filled the canvas with the root's background COLOR, but its image was painted by the box painter and clipped to the root's border box, so `html { background: url(green_box.png) repeat-x; margin: 1in }` showed only the strip inside the margins. `paint_layers` now uses the view rectangle as the painting area for the root box (`bb.parent == p.l.root`), keeping the root box as the positioning area (`background-origin`) - the picture of `background-root-002` now matches the reference. **The test itself still fails**: its text wraps at a different width than the reference's, so it is not a background problem. Measured by directory first: `backgrounds` did not move (the ten `background-root-*` failures all have a second cause), the two corpus pages did.

- **Round 306 (2026-11-11): `background-root-*` read to the end - a harness font effect, not a background defect.** No source change. `background-root-002`'s reference is `body { margin: 1in 0 auto 0 } p { padding-left: 104px; height: 84px }`: its text wraps at `800 - 104 = 696px`, the test's (`html { margin: 1in }`, `body { margin: 8px }`) at `800 - 2*96 - 2*8 = 592px`. The two only match when the sentence fits both lines of text without wrapping, and with the harness's wide placeholder glyphs (16px per character, 49 characters) it does not, so the wrap differs by construction. The ten `background-root-002/007-010/015/016/018/019/024` failures all contain text; round 305 fixed the image half (the picture matches) and nothing more can be done here without real font metrics. Do not spend a round on them.

- **Round 307 (2026-11-11): a generated `::before` / `::after` box with `display: list-item` gets its marker.** `css/CSS2/generated-content` 174 -> 176 of 204 (`after-content-display-003`, `before-content-display-003`); `lists` 143/155, `selectors` 443/465, `text` 344/381, `normal-flow` 636/675 and the vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. `gen_pseudo` built the pseudo-element's box (`inner_for_display(pd)`) and its text but never the marker that `build_element` adds for `display: list-item`, so `div:after { content: "Filler text"; display: list-item }` painted its line with no bullet; the reference shows a `disc` at the line's start. The marker is a `.Marker` box on the pseudo's own style, ordinal 1. Not done: the ordinal of a pseudo list item inside a counted list (`list-item` counter), `::marker` styling of the pseudo. Left in `generated-content`: `content-counter-*` (digits are drawn as solid blocks by the harness font, so a wrong value shows only as a width or glyph-shape difference), `quotes-035/036`, `before-after-*` and `content-004/056/174/175/177`.

- **Round 308 (2026-11-11): `#1digit` and an empty `:lang()` are invalid selectors.** `css/CSS2/selectors` 443 -> 445 of 465 (`id-selector-005`, `lang-selector-002`); `css/CSS2/syntax` now reads **235 of 275** (the handover's 233 was measured at round 22-38; this is the first re-measure); vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. The selector parser built an id selector from any hash token, but `#1digit` is a hash token whose `hash_id` flag is false (an id selector needs an identifier start), so the rule `#1digit { color: red }` applied; and `:lang()` with no argument was accepted instead of making the whole selector - and, through a selector list, the whole rule (`:lang(), div { color: red }`) - invalid. Both now return `rejected`. Left in `selectors`: `lang-selector-005/006` (the language taken from a `<meta http-equiv>`), `attribute-value-selector-007/010`, `first-letter-*`, `first-line-pseudo-012..016`, `pseudo-007`.

- **Round 309 (2026-11-11): attribute selectors compare the values of HTML's case-insensitive attributes without regard to case.** `css/CSS2/selectors` 445 -> 447 of 465 (`attribute-value-selector-007`, `-010`), vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test (the neighbouring suites were not re-run: a change confined to attribute matching on HTML elements). HTML §4.16.1 lists the attributes (`lang`, `type`, `dir`, `rel`, `media`, `target`, ...) whose values selectors match ASCII case-insensitively; `attr_compare` only honoured an explicit `i` flag, so `div[lang = "es"]` missed `lang="ES"` and `div[lang |= "es"]` missed `lang="ES-MX"`. `html_ci_attr` carries that list and applies to elements in the HTML namespace. Not modelled: an explicit `s` flag (case-sensitive) does not switch it back. Left in `selectors`: `lang-selector-005/006` (language from a `<meta http-equiv="content-language">`), the `first-letter-*` / `first-line-pseudo-*` files and `pseudo-007`.

- **Round 310 (2026-11-11): a block's first-line `text-indent` is part of its intrinsic (min- and max-content) width.** `css/CSS2/text` 344 -> 345 of 381 (`text-indent-012`); `normal-flow` 636/675, `floats-clear` 172/207 and the vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test (`css-grid` not re-run). `content_widths` took an inline formatting context's widths from `inline_widths` and left out the indent, so a `float: left` block with `text-indent: 3em` shrank to its text alone and the indent pushed the text out of it. A positive `px`/`em` indent (percentages are indefinite here) is now added to both. **Partial**: `text-indent-intrinsic-001..004` still fail - the later boxes in that family put an inline-block child on the first line and an `inline-block` followed by `&#8203;`, where the indent interacts with where the break may fall; adding it to the minimum is the coarse reading. Left in `text`: `text-indent-014`, `-intrinsic-*`, `-rtl-*`.

- **Round 311 (2026-11-11): a list marker paints its `list-style-image`.** `css/CSS2/lists` 143 -> 144 of 155 (`list-style-021`: `ul { list-style: none; list-style-image: url(cat.png) }`); `generated-content` 176/204 and the vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. The style carried `list_style_image` (inherited, shorthand-expanded) but `paint_marker` never read it, so an image marker was always the glyph of `list-style-type` - and `none` meant nothing. It now loads the image like a background layer (`parse_layer_image`, `url_image`) and draws it at its natural size, right edge on the marker box's right edge, bottom on the baseline; the image replaces the glyph and wins over `list-style-type: none`. Not done: the marker box is still sized for the glyph, so a wide image sticks out to the left of an outside marker rather than moving the text, and `list-style-position: inside` does not reserve the image's width. Left in `lists`: `list-style-020`, the `*-applies-to-012/014/017` files (inline-block / inline-table / list-item wrappers).

- **Round 312 (2026-11-11): the `list-style` shorthand rejects a declaration that cannot be parsed.** `css/CSS2/lists` 144 -> 145 of 155 (`list-style-020`); `generated-content` 176/204, `normal-flow` 636/675 and the vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. `expand_list_style` took any token it did not recognise as the type and kept the last image and position, so `list-style: none url(a.png) none` (one `none` too many) or `square url(a.png) none` (three components, only two usable slots left once the type and image are named) were accepted and replaced the earlier valid `list-style: disc`. A declaration with two positions, two images, two types, or more `none` keywords than there are free slots (type and image) now emits nothing, so the previous declaration stands. Left in `lists`: `list-style-position-024` and the `*-applies-to-012/014/017` files.

- **Round 313 (2026-11-11): a REPLACED grid item with `align-self: stretch` stretches to its area's height.** `css/css-grid` 949 -> 951 of 1545 (`alignment/replaced-alignment-with-aspect-ratio-004`, `-005`), vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. The row-height branch of the grid placement loop (`(align == .Normal || align == .Stretch) && height auto && ... && ib.inner != .Replaced`) left every replaced item at its natural / ratio height, even when both axes asked for `stretch` - where CSS Box Alignment says the ratio is then ignored and the element fills the area; the width side already did (`just == .Stretch` returns the available width). `align-self: normal` still behaves as `start` for a replaced item. Left: `replaced-alignment-with-aspect-ratio-006` (the same, with auto margins), `-009` (stretch in one axis applying the ratio in the other), and `grid-item-mixed-baseline-*`.

- **Round 314 (2026-11-11): a replaced grid item stretched in the block axis gets its width from the ratio when its own width is free.** `css/css-grid` 951 -> 954 of 1545 (all six `alignment/replaced-alignment-with-aspect-ratio-*` now pass: `-006`, `-009` and one more), vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. Round 313 made the replaced item fill the area's HEIGHT; its width still came from `used_replaced_size` at the natural height, so with `justify-self` not stretching (auto margin, or the default `normal`) a 50x50 image in a 100px row was 100 tall and 50 wide. `grid_item_width_h` now returns `(area_h - vertical margins and extras) * ratio` for a replaced item whose `align-self` resolves to `stretch`, with `height` and `width` auto and no auto vertical margins, taking the ratio from `replaced_intrinsic` as round 294 did. Left: `grid-item-mixed-baseline-*`.

- **Round 315 (2026-11-11): the first half of `column-subgrid-extra-margin-*` - an EMPTY subgrid contributes nothing, so its edges never widen the lanes - built, measured inert, reverted.** `css/css-grid` stayed 954/1545 with no newly failing test (corpus 191/212). `subgrid_contributions` only emits items taken from the subgrid's own content, so `.subgrid { grid-column: span 2; padding-left: 50px; padding-right: 40px }` with no children left the four `repeat(4, auto)` lanes at 10px each (**a debug print in `grid_widths` read `nc=4 total=40 n=5`**). Pushing two synthetic items - `edge_s` on the first spanned track, `edge_e` on the last - widened the first two lanes and the picture moved towards the reference (container 112px against the reference's 202px), but none of the nine files passed: the reference's four lanes come out equal (about 50px each), so the 90px of edge is spread over all four tracks by a rule not yet found (probably the auto-placed span 2 lands on lanes the algorithm picks differently, or the extra is shared by the track-sizing algorithm's span step). Not shipped. **Method:** the intrinsic width of a lanes container is `grid_widths`, and a print there is the fastest way to see what the lane sizing sees.

- **Round 316 (2026-11-11): an `auto-fill` repeat with no definite size takes the FEWEST repetitions that reach the container's minimum size, and a `min-height` carries through an `aspect-ratio` into the width.** `css/css-grid` 954 -> 958 of 1545, `CSS2/normal-flow` 636/675 and the vendored corpus 191/212 unchanged, no newly failing test. Passes `grid-definition/grid-auto-repeat-aspect-ratio-001` and three lanes files (`column-auto-repeat-003`, `row-auto-repeat-003`, `intrinsic-auto-repeat/row-auto-repeat-auto-006`). CSS Grid 2 §7.2.3.2: when the free space is indefinite, `auto-fill` repeats the smallest positive number of times that satisfies the minimum (`min-width`, or - with an `aspect-ratio` - the width a `min-height` transfers). `inline-grid; aspect-ratio: 1/1; min-height: 60px; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, 50px)` is therefore 2 tracks (100px), not 1; the code made it 1 and the 60px green box did not match the reference's 100px square. Two pieces: `GridParse.min_avail` (set in `grid_build` when the width is indefinite) drives the count, and `content_widths` raises an auto-width box's min and max to `ratio_width(min-height)`. **An earlier version with only the second piece rendered a 60px square - a debug print (`mn=50 mx=50 minh_px=60 ratio_w=60`) showed the transfer worked and the count was the missing half.** Left: `grid-auto-repeat-aspect-ratio-002` (the same inside a `height: 100%` chain with a percentage `min-height`).

- **Round 317 of the conformance goal (2026-11-11): a block inside an inline resolves its over-constrained margin by the direction of the nearest non-inline ancestor.**
  - `used_width_block` took the direction of the promoted inline span; the spec's containing block is the anonymous block, which inherits from the span's block parent. `block-in-inline-margins-001b`/`002b` pass.
  - normal-flow 636 -> 638; positioning 453, vendored corpus 191 unchanged.

- **Round 318 (2026-11-11): remaining `block-in-inline-*` failures are placeholder-glyph tests; nothing built.**

- **Round 319 (2026-11-11): an auto-height inline promoted to a block passes its parent's definite height to its children.**

- **Round 320 (2026-11-11): zeroing border/padding of a promoted inline is inert; reverted.**

- **Round 321 (2026-11-11): an inline split around a block child draws no decoration and has no edges.**
  - Round 320's edge zeroing alone was inert because paint read the style; `paint_decor` now skips a non-anonymous, non-inline-level `display: inline` flow box, and `resolve_edges` zeroes its border/padding. normal-flow 639 -> 644 (the `block-in-inline-empty-*` and `whitespace-*` family); positioning 453, tables 222, generated-content 176 unchanged.
  - Ceiling: a split inline with text before or after the block should still paint its fragments; this treats every promoted inline as fragmentless.

- **Round 322 (2026-11-11): a relatively positioned split inline does not offset the floats inside it.**
  - `block-in-inline-float-between-001`: the reference keeps the float at its unshifted place while the in-flow blocks move; `apply_relative` now takes the offset back off direct float children of a promoted inline. normal-flow 644 -> 645; positioning 453 unchanged (corpus not rerun, change is limited to promoted inlines).

- **Round 323 (2026-11-11): `block-in-inline-*` is 21/24; the three left are text-wrapping bound.**
  - `empty-001` to `-004` and `whitespace-001a` pass since round 321. `first-line-002` (`10ch` width, `text-indent: 5ch`, wrapped digits) and `nested-001`/`002` (wrapped words) compare wrapped placeholder glyphs; they need real font metrics, not a layout rule.

- **Round 324 (2026-11-11): the split-inline rule of round 321 broke grid-lanes items; restricted to block-container parents.**
  - Custom-element grid items (`<item>`) keep `display: inline`, so round 321's zeroing of edges and decoration stripped them: css-grid fell 958 -> 951 (flow-tolerance, justify-content). `is_split_inline` now also requires the parent to be a Flow/FlowRoot box; css-grid back to 958 with an identical failure set, normal-flow 645.
  - Lesson: a CSS2 box-tree change needs a css-grid sweep too; the earlier round-321 checks never ran it.
  - Negative: capping an `auto` track minimum by a fixed maximum (for `subgrid/standalone-axis-size-004/009`) is untested in isolation; re-measure alone before keeping.

- **Round 325 (2026-11-11): an `auto` track minimum is limited by a fixed maximum (css-grid 11.5.1).**
  - `size_tracks` clamped item min-contribution to the fixed max for span-1 tracks. css-grid 958 -> 962, exactly four tests flip and none regress: `subgrid/standalone-axis-size-004` and `-009`, `grid-item-minmax-img-001`, `grid-minimum-size-grid-items-017`. normal-flow 645 and tables 222 unchanged.

- **Round 326 (2026-11-11): a subgrid with no parent tracks takes as many tracks as it spans.**
  - `grid_build` set `er`/`ec` from the dummy axis' own `grid-row`/`grid-column` span; before, `grid-auto-flow: column` with `grid-template-rows: subgrid` placed every item in one row when the parent was sizing it. css-grid 962 -> 966, four flips and none regress: `subgrid/standalone-axis-size-007`, `subgrid/grid-gap-003`, `grid-lanes/.../row-subgrid-grid-gap-014`, `row-subgrid-extra-margin-003`. normal-flow unchanged.

- **Round 327 (2026-11-11): `subgrid/line-names-006` is not an areas-widening problem; nothing changed.**

Stopping `grid-template-areas` from raising the column count for a subgrid axis left the subgrid directory at 44 of 88; reverted. Rows 7 and 11 of the same file are separate name-resolution cases (`[a] [a]` with `span 3`, and an area name on a subgrid).

- **Round 328 (2026-11-11): `grid-lanes/intrinsic-sizing/*-001-auto` — measured, not fixed.**
  - Printing the lane inputs for `cols-001-auto`: an auto item has `cmin = cmax = 15` (the placeholder glyph advance at 15px) but the item with `width: 2ch` has 30, so `ch` resolves as 1.0 em there; every lane then sizes to 30 and the container comes out 81 wide against the reference's ~66. The reference writes the same `2ch` as `repeat(4, 2ch)` tracks and renders ~15px per track, i.e. 0.5 em. Where the two diverge (item `width` vs track list) is not found; `style.e` line ~1768 sets `ch = 1.0` only for an Ahem family, which this test does not use.
  - Next probe: print `ch` at the point `width: 2ch` resolves for the item, and for the track list.

- **Round 329 (2026-11-11): grid track lists resolve `ch` like the cascade does (1 em in this harness).**
  - Probes in `tmp/ch`: `1ch` equals `1em` (15px at a 15px font) for monospace and serif alike, so every `width: Nch` is N em; `grid_build` built its track-list context with `ch = 0.5`, so `repeat(4, 2ch)` was half the size of the same `2ch` on an item. `ctx.ch = 1.0` there. css-grid 966 -> 969: `grid-lanes-align-content-004`, `intrinsic-sizing-rows-005`, `-007`; none regress.

- **Round 330 (2026-11-11): lanes intrinsic width through the every-lane item list is net negative; reverted.**
  - `grid_widths` sizes a lanes container's columns from the placed items only, while layout repeats each auto-placed item on every lane; the mismatch is why `cols-001-auto` is 81 wide against 130. Feeding `grid_widths` the same expanded list gave intrinsic-sizing 25 -> 23 (`cols-001-fr` and `cols-007` regress, nothing gains), so the width path needs the fr / minmax cases handled separately before it can share the list.

- **Round 331 (2026-11-11): the every-lane list in `grid_widths`, limited to all-`auto` lanes, is inert; reverted.**

- **Round 332 (2026-11-11): all-`auto` lanes width through the every-lane list, with spanning items, is a wash; reverted.**

Full css-grid 969 -> 969: gains `grid-lanes-gap-002`, `column-auto-repeat-auto-006`; losses `column-subgrid-grid-gap-002`, `column-auto-repeat-minmax-004`. `cols-001-auto` still fails on a few pixels in the grids with spanning items (a 4ch item over lanes 2-3 and a span-3 item).
  - Retry only with the two losses understood first.

- **Round 333 (2026-11-11): an all-`auto` lanes container's intrinsic width counts each auto-placed item on every lane, spanning items included.**
  - `grid_widths` now builds the same every-lane list layout uses (`lanes_all_lane_items`) when every column is plain `auto`; spanning items contribute only on this width path (`spans` flag). Round 332's two losses came from letting spanning items into the layout path as well. css-grid 969 -> 971: `grid-lanes-gap-002`, `column-auto-repeat-auto-006`; none regress.
  - `cols-001-auto` still fails on a few pixels where spanning items sit (4ch over lanes 2-3, span 3).

- **Round 335 (2026-11-11): `cols-001-auto` measured to the pixel; only lane widths inside two grids differ.**
  - With round 334's tweak (`span N / line` counts as one definite lane) every grid in the test has the reference's outer width (border columns identical on both rows), so that tweak is right even though it flips no test. The remaining 2400 differing pixels are inside the grids holding a span-3 item (`width: 5ch; grid-column: span 3/4`) with another item over lanes 2-3: reference lanes are 19 / 26 / 26 / 15 px (the 75 px item over lanes 1-3), ours come out near 24 / 25 / 24 / 21, so lane 4 also grows in ours.

- **Round 336 (2026-11-11): a block replaced element's percentage width resolves against its containing block, not against itself.**
  - `layout_block_child` called `used_replaced_size` with the child's own content width, so `width: 50%` on a 15x15 image at 200px gave a 100px width but a 50px height (50% of 100). It now passes the containing block width. normal-flow 645 -> 646 (`block-replaced-width-006`); css-grid 971 with an identical failure set.

- **Round 337 (2026-11-11): a `min-height` or `max-height` that changes the content height keeps the last child's bottom margin inside the box.**
  - The WPT expectation (not the CSS 2.1 text, which would let a trapped margin grow the box): with `min-height: 100px` over a 30px child with `margin-bottom: 550px` the box is 100px and the margin is dropped; a `max-height` that clamps does the same. `layout_block_child` no longer passes the margin up when `height_limited(s)` and the clamped height differs from the content height. A `min-height` below the content (`margin-collapse-min-height-003`) still collapses through.
  - normal-flow 646 -> 650: `margin-collapse-min-height-001`/`-002`, `min-height-separates-margin`, `max-height-separates-margin`. A first version with the unconditional rule lost `-003`; the clamp test fixed it. tables 222, positioning 453, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-grid 971 (identical failure set).

- **Round 338 (2026-11-11): `min-height` / `min-width` reach table parts that have no `height` / `width`.**
  - `layout_table`: an auto-height table is at least its `min-height` tall (rows share the extra); a caption with auto height is at least its `min-height`; a `<col>`, `<colgroup>` or cell without `width` still takes its `min-width` as the column or cell width. Before, each only looked at a pixel `height` / `width`.
  - normal-flow 650 -> 656: `min-height-applies-to-013`/`-014`/`-015`, `min-width-applies-to-005`/`-006`/`-007`. tables 222, positioning 453, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-grid 971 (identical failure set) unchanged.

- **Round 339 (2026-11-11): an empty block with a percentage height in an auto-height parent is self-collapsing.**
  - `self_collapsing` returned false for any percentage height. Against an auto-height parent the percentage behaves as `auto`, so the box is empty and its margins collapse through (`margin-collapse-through-percentage-height-block` and `-inside-table-cell`; the two `margin-collapsing-in-table-caption-*` tests passed in the same run). A grid or flex item may be stretched to a definite height, so a parent directly inside a grid or flex container keeps the old rule: the first version regressed `css-grid/grid-items/percentage-size-subitems-002` (971 -> 970) until that case was excluded.
  - normal-flow 656 -> 658; tables 222, positioning 453, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-grid 971 (identical failure set) unchanged.

- **Round 340 (2026-11-11): a table cell with no line of text does not share its row's baseline.**
  - `layout_table` gave a cell without a first baseline `up = border + padding top`, so an empty cell sat in the baseline group with a text cell: row = text baseline above (13) + empty cell below (48) = 61 instead of 48 (`width-applies-to-006`). Such a cell is now treated like a top-aligned one and only sets the row's minimum height. A bare copy of the test with empty cells passed before, one with text did not: `tmp/ch/t1`/`t2` probes.
  - normal-flow 658 -> 659; tables 222, positioning 453, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-grid 971 (identical failure set) unchanged.
  - Read, not fixed: `inlines-013`/`-020` compare rendered words in the placeholder font; `inline-table-width-001a` and `block-formatting-contexts-011` not examined.

Not examined: `abspos-007/012/022/024-026`, `abspos-inline-007/008`, `abspos-overflow-011/012`, `absolute-non-replaced-*` (9).

- **Round 344 (2026-11-11): an empty block's margins were counted twice under a parent that had already collapsed them through.**

That branch now adds nothing while `consumed.v`, and `inner_collapsed` brings the margins of empty blocks nested in an empty block into both paths. `abspos-022` passes (probe: `tmp/ch/m`).

A `position: relative` inline also moves the static position of the abspos boxes in it (`abspos-inline-007`, `-008`).
  - positioning 455 -> 457; normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-grid 971 (identical failure set) unchanged.

Remaining unexamined in `abspos*`: `-block-level-001`, `-float-with-inline-container`.

- **Round 347 (2026-11-11): `abspos-block-level-001` read; the first block matches, the right-to-left blocks do not.**

In the right-to-left rows the reference draws a lime shrink-to-fit abspos box at the right edge covering the red inline text, and ours sits left with the red text visible; 40-character lines wrap at the placeholder font's 1-em advance, so it was not narrowed to a rule. `abspos-float-with-inline-container` still waits on the abspos containing block of a split inline being its fragment box.

- **Round 348 (2026-11-12): grid-lanes containers keep their track geometry for abspos children.**
  - `layout_grid_inner` returned from its three lanes branches (rows, dense columns, columns) without pushing a `GridGeom`, so `grid_abs_area` found none and an abspos child kept its static position whatever `grid-column`/`grid-row` said. `lanes_register_geom` now pushes the lane tracks plus one empty track for the stacking axis (an auto line there is the padding edge, as in a grid); a right-to-left column-lanes container sets `rtl` so the lines mirror.

- **Round 349 (2026-11-12): grid-lanes `align-content`/`justify-content` move the stack in normal flow too.**
  - `lanes_stack_align` returned at once unless the container was `fill-reverse`, so `align-content: end|center` (column lanes) and `justify-content` (row lanes) never moved the in-flow stack of an ordinary lanes container. `center` now centres it and `end`/`flow-end`/`flex-end` push it to the far end; the `fill-reverse` mapping is unchanged. (`grid-lanes/abspos/*oof-align-content-*`, `*oof-justify-content-*`.)
  - css-grid 983 -> 988 (5 fewer failures, none new); positioning 457, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged. The six `*oof-fill-reverse-*` abspos tests still fail.

- **Round 350 (2026-11-12): a `fill-reverse` lanes container reverses the static position of its abspos children.**
  - The in-flow stack of a `fill-reverse` lanes container is reflected across the stack extent, but an abspos child without insets on that axis kept its near-edge static position. `GridGeom` now carries `stack` (0 none, 1 the y axis, 2 the x axis) and `ext` (the stack extent: container size when definite, else the content size) for lanes containers, and `layout_abs` reflects the static top/left: `ext - static - size`. (`grid-lanes/abspos/*oof-fill-reverse-*`; all 24 `grid-lanes/abspos` now pass.)
  - css-grid 988 -> 994 (6 fewer failures, none new); positioning 457, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged.

- **Round 351 (2026-11-12): a flex item with `width: min-content` is its longest unbreakable run wide.**
  - The row flex basis used the max-content width for any content-keyword `width`; `min-content` now takes the min-content width, so `<item>item 1</item>` as a flex item is 4 characters wide, not 6. Found through `grid-lanes/alignment/row-fill-reverse-align-content-001`, whose reference is a flex row of such items (the test itself still fails: the lane items come out about 54px tall where `height: min-content` over two wrapped lines gives 32, not traced). `row-fill-reverse-align-items-indefinite-size-001` passes.
  - css-grid 994 -> 995; css-flexbox 636 and the failure set identical to before; positioning 457, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged.
