Traced from Round 351's open item: `<item style="height:min-content">` came out 70px (the lane) where 32px (two wrapped lines) is right.
  - css-grid 995 -> 998 (`row-fill-reverse-align-content-001`, `-002`, `row-fill-reverse-align-content-indefinite-size-001`); positioning 457, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged.

- **Round 353 (2026-11-12): in column lanes with `fill-reverse`, `align-items: start` and `end` swap before the stack is reflected.**
  - An item is aligned inside the room from its own end to the next item below it in its lanes (or the container end), in flow coordinates; `fill-reverse` then reflects the whole stack, so `start` must push the item to the flow end (the physical top after reflection) and `end` leave it put. Applied in `layout_lanes` and `layout_lanes_dense`; `center` and `stretch` are symmetric. The reference (`column-align-items-008`) is a one-row grid with `margin-bottom` equal to the flow offset plus the item's own height, which is exactly this room.
  - css-grid 998 -> 1005 (`column-align-items-008/010/012/014`, `column-fill-reverse-align-items-multi-span-indefinite-size-001`, `column-fill-reverse-dense-packing-align-items-multi-span-001/003`); other suites unchanged. The row-lanes counterpart (`row-justify-items-*`, `row-fill-reverse-*justify*`) is not done.

- **Round 354 (2026-11-12): the same `start`/`end` swap for row lanes with `fill-reverse` (`justify-items`).**
  - `layout_lanes_rows` aligns an item along the stack (x) inside the room from its own end to the next item in its lanes; with `fill-reverse` the reflection follows, so `justify-items: start` moves the item to the flow end and `end` leaves it (as Round 353 did for `align-items` in column lanes).
  - css-grid 1005 -> 1010 (`row-justify-items-007/009/011/013`, `row-fill-reverse-dense-packing-justify-items-multi-span-001`); other suites unchanged.

- **Round 355 (2026-11-12): `flow-start`/`flow-end` follow `track-reverse` in lane alignment.**
  - `cross_offset` (the alignment along the lane axis in row lanes) ignored `flow-start` and `flow-end`, so both acted as `start`. In a `track-reverse` container `flow-start` is the physical end and `flow-end` the physical start; without it `flow-end` is the end. `row-track-reverse-align-items-001` passes.
  - css-grid 1010 -> 1011; other suites unchanged.

- **Round 356 (2026-11-12): `row-fill-reverse-align-items-002` read; not changed.**
  - A `last baseline` row-lanes container narrower than its min-content items (`width: 180px`, six `min-content` items): the reference is a column of two `flex-direction: row` lines with `align-items: last baseline` and overflows to the right, ours reflects the overflowing stack and spills left of the container (x < 0). The test needs both the last-baseline alignment in lanes and the overflow direction of a `fill-reverse` stack that does not fit; neither is derivable from this one pair, so nothing was changed. Related open: `-003`, `row-fill-reverse-align-items-indefinite-size-002`, `row-justify-items-016`.

- **Round 357 (2026-11-12): lanes `track-sizing` triaged; no cheap rule.**
  - Of 63 `grid-lanes/track-sizing` failures, about 50 are subgrid (`*-subgrid-*`, `*-inherited-tracks-*`, `*-extra-margin-*`, `*-standalone-axis-size-*`). `column-track-sizing-002` (`minmax(15px, min-content) max-content auto`, items spanning lanes 1-2): the reference sizes the tracks 30/45/25, ours leaves the `max-content` lane at 0 and the `auto` lane at 70, i.e. the spanning item's contribution is not distributed to the `max-content` track the way the intrinsic-size algorithm (with the `minmax` minimum counted first) does. `grid-lanes-percentage-rows-indefinite-height-003/004` need `repeat(auto-fill, ...)` rows with a percentage track in an indefinite height. Next candidate: spanning-item distribution in `size_tracks` for lanes.

- **Round 358 (2026-11-12): an inline split around blocks with no line of its own is as wide as its padding for abspos.**
  - `abspos-float-with-inline-container`: the span (`position: relative; padding-left: 100px`) holds only a block, so it has no `Segment` fragment and a 0 x 0 box; its abspos descendant (`left: 0; right: 0`) came out 0 wide. The containing block of such an inline is its padding (what its first and last fragments hold), now `padding-left + padding-right` in `layout_abs`.
  - positioning 457 -> 458; css-grid 1011 (identical failure set), normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged.

- **Round 359 (2026-11-12): rtl block-level abspos before inline content; change inert, reverted.**
  - In an rtl line the pieces are in visual order (bidi reorders `Abs` and `Atomic` like characters), so Round 342's "follows content, so sits below the line" test fired for an abspos that comes *before* the content in the text: a probe (`<div dir=rtl><div abs>..</div><span inline-block></span></div>`) put it 16px low where ltr puts it at 0. Scanning an rtl line from its far end fixes the probe, but changed no suite result (positioning 458, css-grid 1011, others identical, failure lists identical), because `abspos-block-level-001`'s rtl rows also differ in 40-character line wrapping. Reverted; the probe shape is the starting point if that wrapping ever matches.

- **Round 360 (2026-11-12): per-axis grid area for abspos descendants; negative, reverted.**
  - `grid/abspos/descendant-static-position-001/002` name only `grid-column` on a descendant (not a child) of the grid; `layout_abs` gives a deeper descendant the grid area only when it names a line on both axes. Switching that to "either axis" fixed neither test and regressed `descendant-static-position-003` and `-004` (css-grid abspos 97 -> 95 of 141). The reference puts the descendant's row at its static position inside the grid item (`margin-top: 2px`) and its width at the column area, which needs the static position taken inside the intermediate block rather than at the grid's padding edge; not tried.

- **Round 361 (2026-11-12): `::first-letter` does not apply to a grid or flex container.**
  - `gen_box` called `apply_first_letter` for any non-inline-level box; a grid or flex container is not a block container, so its `::first-letter` has nothing to style (its items' own are another matter). The call now skips `Grid` and `Flex` inner types: `grid-model/grid-container-ignores-first-letter-002` and `grid-first-letter-001`.
  - css-grid 1011 -> 1013; css-flexbox 636 (identical failure set), positioning 458, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged. Read, not fixed: `grid-first-line-003` and the `grid-inline-first-*` tests wrap their long text at the placeholder font's width (second line stays red where the reference is all green).

- **Round 362 (2026-11-12): `grid-template-columns-fit-content-001` isolated per column; the track sizing is not the failing part.**
  - Each `.grid` block of the page's second column (12 cases: the `fit-content`/`minmax`/content-sized tracks the reference replaces with fixed tracks) was cut out with its reference block and run as its own reftest: all 12 pass. The full page still fails (columns 2 and 4 differ from the third block on), so the difference is not a track-size rule but something the blocks do to each other in the shared page (the `floatLeft` columns, the stacked `.grid` boxes with `margin-bottom` and their abspos `.test` bars). The probe generator is a throwaway (regex over the test and reference; sections 0 and 2 did not split evenly, so only column 2 is established). Next: bisect the full page by dropping blocks from the end of a column until it passes.

- **Round 363 (2026-11-12): a `fit-content()` track stays within its argument after spanning items; the page tests need more.**
  - Correction to the previous entry: the probes lacked `/css/support/grid.css` and `/fonts/ahem.css` (root-relative links the runner resolves only inside the WPT tree), so both sides lost the `.autoRow*Column` placement and passed trivially. With `grid.css` inlined, 19 of the 41 per-block pairs of `grid-template-columns-fit-content-001` fail. The first: `repeat(2, fit-content(40px))` with a span-2 item whose max-content (70) exceeds the tracks' growth limits; `size_tracks` clamps a fit-content limit before the multi-span pass and the pass then added the extra to it (limit 40 -> 52.5). The clamp is now re-applied after the spanning distribution. 3 more blocks pass (25 of 41).
  - No suite moved (css-grid 1013 with the same failure set, css-sizing 335, css-flexbox 636, positioning 458, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191), because each page needs every block right. Remaining failing blocks: `bt0_4/_7`, `bt1_0-5/_9-11`, `bt2_4/_7`, `bt3_4-6`; `bt0_4` has the right track sizes now but a taller spanning-item row (ours 22px, reference 12px). Probes: `tmp/ch/fc/` (grid.css inlined; regenerate rather than reuse).

- **Round 364 (2026-11-12): what a capped `fit-content()` track cannot take goes to the other spanned tracks.**
  - `size_tracks` shared a spanning item's extra growth-limit space equally and then clamped each `fit-content(N)` track to N, so the clamped part was lost (`repeat(2, fit-content(40px))` + a 70px span-2 item gave limits 52.5/12.5 then 40/12.5, tracks 40/12.5 instead of 40/25). It is now water-filled: each pass splits the remainder over the tracks that can still grow, a fit-content track taking at most its argument. Isolated `fit-content-001` blocks 25 -> 30 of 41 (`bt0_4`, `bt0_7`, `bt2_4`, `bt2_7`, `bt1_1`).
  - Tried and backed out: giving the extra only to `auto`/`max-content` tracks when the span has any (fixes `bt1_0/2/9/10/11`, breaks `bt1_6/7/8` and `bt3_7/8/9`: 29 of 41). The references show both behaviours (`fit-content(40px) max-content` = 12.5/82.5, but `fit-content(30px) min-content max-content` = the same as `auto min-content max-content`), so the rule needs the min-content-contribution step (which sets the base sizes) before it can be pinned down.
  - No suite moved (css-grid 1013 with the same failure set, css-sizing 335, css-flexbox 636, positioning 458, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191). Remaining isolated failures: `bt1_0/2/3/4/5/9/10/11`, `bt3_4/5/6`.

- **Round 365 (2026-11-12): a spanning item's extra growth-limit space goes to track types in a fixed order.**
  - Replaces Round 364's equal share: the extra goes to the spanned tracks with a `max-content` maximum first; if there are none, to those with `auto` or `fit-content()`; only then to `min-content` ones (`lane_tier_ok`), with the water-fill over a `fit-content` cap kept inside a tier. Pinned by the references: `fit-content(40px) max-content` = 12.5/82.5 (the fit track gets nothing while a max-content one exists), `min-content fit-content(40px)` = 12.5/40, and `fit-content(30px) min-content max-content` = the same as the same line with `auto`. Isolated `fit-content-001` blocks 30 -> 39 of 41.
  - No suite moved (css-grid 1013, same failure set; css-sizing 335; css-flexbox 636; positioning 458; normal-flow 659; tables 222; generated-content 176; corpus 191). Four of the page's six test files pass now; `grid-template-columns-fit-content-001` and the rows twin still fail on `bt3_4` (`fit-content(110px) fit-content(40px)`, expected 45/20) and `bt3_5` (`max-content fit-content(110px)`, expected 92.5/12.5).

- **Round 366 (2026-11-12): `bt3_4` analysed; "only tracks whose limit no single item set" is negative, reverted.**
  - `fit-content(110px) fit-content(40px)`, item `XX` in column 2 (limit 20 from the item) and a span-2 `XXX XXX` (min 30, max 70): the reference is 45/20, so the extra max-content space (70 - 5 - 20 = 45) all went to column 1, the track no single item sized, and column 2 stayed at its own 20. Restricting the share to tracks without a single-span-item limit fixes that shape but costs `bt1_2/5/11` (36 of 41 against 39 with the tiered rule), so the two rules fight; the next try is to restrict by tier first and by "no own limit" only inside the `auto`/`fit-content` tier, with the `bt3_5` case (`max-content fit-content(110px)`, 92.5/12.5) as the other pin. Nothing changed in the source (the code is as committed in Round 365).

- **Round 367 (2026-11-12): spanning-item sizing runs in the spec's order, so `grid-template-columns-fit-content-001` passes.**
  - Three changes in `size_tracks`: (1) the base sizes of the tracks a multi-span item covers are raised for all such items (min-content contributions) before any growth limit is touched, instead of item by item interleaved with the limits; (2) a new step raises the bases of tracks whose minimum is `max-content` by the items' max-content contributions (`max-content fit-content(110px)` = 92.5/12.5 needs this: bases 12.5/12.5 from the two items' mins, then 80 more to column 1); (3) in the base step a `fit-content()` track a single item sized takes no more than its limit and the rest goes to the other tracks, and in the limit step the tiered sharing skips such tracks while an `auto`/`fit-content` track no single item sized can take it (`fit-content(110px) fit-content(40px)` = 45/20). All 41 isolated blocks of the page pass.
  - The first form of (3) capped every single-item-sized track, not only fit-content ones, and cost five tests (`grid-lanes-align-content-004`, `grid-lanes-justify-content-001/002`, `grid-percent-cols-spanned-shrinkwrap-001`, `grid-spanning-item-with-paddings-auto-rows-001`: css-grid 1009); bisected by switching each part off (the cap, not the `max-content` step or the skip). Restricted to `max.kind == 6`.
  - css-grid 1013 -> 1014 (`grid-template-columns-fit-content-001`); css-sizing 335, css-flexbox 636, positioning 458, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191 unchanged. The rows twin `grid-template-rows-fit-content-001` still fails.

## Round 368 — orthogonal grid items contribute real min/max-content to row sizing

`layout_grid_inner` row measurement gave an orthogonal item (`vertical-*` inside a horizontal grid) one fixed row size, so min and max contributions were equal and `fit-content()` rows could not clamp it. Now an orthogonal item measures `content_widths` in its own writing mode for `rmin`/`rmax`. css-grid 1014 -> 1015 of 1545: fixed `grid-template-rows-fit-content-001`, `grid-lanes-intrinsic-sizing-rows-001-mix1`, `-002-fr`, `-002-mix1`; regressed `column-grid-lanes-item-baseline-004a`, `row-explicit-placement-006`, `grid-lanes-intrinsic-sizing-rows-005` (all lanes with vertical-* items; open, not yet diagnosed). Every other suite (positioning 458, normal-flow 659, tables 222, generated-content 176, corpus 191, css-sizing 335, css-flexbox 636, css-writing-modes 638) unchanged.
